# Verion — 16-Week Roadmap

**Status:** Draft v1.0
**Related:** `PRODUCT_SPEC.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`
**Audience:** primary developer + Claude Code as pairing partner

---

## How to use this document

This roadmap is organized as **Milestones → Issues**, following the module boundaries defined in `ARCHITECTURE.md` and the functional requirements (FR-1 to FR-10) in `PRODUCT_SPEC.md`.

Conventions for every issue:
- **Module** — which hexagon it belongs to (from `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3).
- **Depends on** — issues/milestones that must land first.
- **Acceptance criteria** — the definition of done; Claude Code should not consider an issue complete until these are all true, including tests.
- **Completion marker** — a shipped issue's heading ends `— done`, the form M3.7–M4.5 already used. Decided, and applied to every shipped issue, at the M5→M6 boundary review (2026-09-15). It records that the issue shipped, not that every criterion survived later amendment; the issue's dated notes say which did not. A `Delivered` line in an issue's body records what shipped and is not the marker. An issue moved elsewhere ends `— moved to M<n>.<n>` instead.
- Every issue that touches `domain/` or `application/` must ship with **unit tests using in-memory port fakes** — no exceptions, per the architecture's enforcement rules.
- Every issue that adds an adapter must ship with an **integration test** against the real dependency (Postgres/Redis/tool CLI).

Suggested workflow with Claude Code: work one issue at a time, open a branch per issue, keep PRs small enough to review in one sitting. Use this roadmap as the shared source of truth for scope — if an issue needs to grow, update this document first, then the code.

## Milestone 0 — Foundations (Week 1)

**Goal:** empty-but-real skeleton: repo, CI, module scaffolding, no business logic yet.

- **M0.1 — Repository & tooling setup** — done
  Module: `platform/` · Depends on: —
  - Repo initialized with the folder structure from `ARCHITECTURE.md` §7 (empty modules with `domain/application/ports/adapters` subfolders).
  - Linting, formatting, pre-commit hooks configured (ruff/black or equivalent).
  - `docker-compose.yml` with Postgres + Redis for local dev.

- **M0.2 — CI pipeline skeleton** — done
  Module: `platform/` · Depends on: M0.1
  - GitHub Actions workflow: lint → unit tests → build.
  - Import-linter rule added and passing on empty scaffolding, enforcing the dependency rule from `ARCHITECTURE.md` §10.

- **M0.3 — FastAPI app bootstrap + DI wiring** — done
  Module: `platform/` · Depends on: M0.1
  - App factory pattern, settings via env vars, health check endpoint.
  - Dependency-injection container wiring ports to adapters at startup (even if adapters are stubs for now).

- **M0.4 — ADRs formalized** — done
  Module: docs · Depends on: —
  - `docs/adr/0001-modular-monolith.md`, `0002-hexagonal-architecture.md`, `0003-explainable-scoring.md`, `0004-llm-downstream-of-scoring.md` written in full (context/decision/consequences), based on the summaries in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §12.

## Milestone 1 — Identity & Projects (Weeks 2-3)

**Goal:** a user can sign up, log in, and create a project. FR-1, FR-2.

- **M1.1 — Identity domain & use cases** — done
  Module: `identity` · Depends on: M0
  - `User` entity, password hashing, `RegisterUserUseCase`, `AuthenticateUserUseCase`.
  - Unit tests with in-memory `UserRepositoryPort` fake.

- **M1.2 — Identity adapters** — done
  Module: `identity` · Depends on: M1.1
  - Postgres `UserRepository` adapter + migration.
  - FastAPI routers: `POST /auth/register`, `POST /auth/login` (JWT).
  - Integration tests against real Postgres.

- **M1.3 — Projects domain & use cases** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: M1.1
  - `Project`, `Repository` entities. `CreateProjectUseCase`, `ConnectRepositoryUseCase`.
  - RBAC check (owner/member) enforced at use-case level, not just API level.

- **M1.4 — Projects adapters** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: M1.3
  - Postgres repository adapter + migration.
  - REST endpoints for project CRUD + repo connection.

- **M1.5 — GitHub OAuth / App connection** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: M1.4
  - `VcsProviderPort` + `GitHubAdapter` (repo listing, metadata read).
  - E2E flow: log in → connect a real GitHub repo.

## Milestone 2 — Security Context (Weeks 3-4)

**Goal:** connecting a repo produces a real Security Context. FR-3.

- **M2.1 — Context extraction domain logic** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: M1.5
  - `SecurityContext` entity, `BuildSecurityContextUseCase`.
  - Detection rules for language/framework from repo file signatures (e.g. `package.json`, `requirements.txt`, `Dockerfile`) — implemented as a pure domain service, testable without a real repo.

- **M2.2 — Context extraction adapter** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: M2.1, M1.5
  - Adapter that pulls the relevant files via `VcsProviderPort` and feeds the detection service.
  - Integration test against a handful of real sample repos (fixtures).

- **M2.3 — Manual context annotation** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: M2.1
  - API + minimal UI for user to confirm/edit context (`public_facing`, `handles_pii`, etc.).
  - Note: the UI portion ships as part of M8.4's onboarding flow, which already depends on this
    issue — M2.3 itself delivers the API only (detect/get/annotate routes, Postgres persistence,
    owner/member permission gating).

## Milestone 3 — Scanning Infrastructure (Weeks 4-6)

**Goal:** a scan can be triggered and produces raw tool output, end to end for one scanner first. FR-4.

> **Note — multi-scanner orchestration was M3.7, and is resolved.** M3.2/M3.4/M3.5 each added exactly one scanner path end to end (per M3.2's own "first scanner, walking skeleton" framing), and `platform/worker.py` dispatched `SemgrepAdapter` alone. Carrying that gap unassigned through three issues was deliberate, but a post-M3 review moved it onto the critical path: M4 designs the `Finding` schema and its per-scanner mappers, and M5 exists specifically to correlate findings across *different* tools — neither was meaningfully exercisable while a `Scan` could only ever produce one `ScanResult`. Closed by M3.7 below (see `docs/adr/0016-multi-scanner-dispatch.md`), ahead of M4; G1 in the Deferred gaps register records the full history.

- **M3.1 — Scan orchestration domain** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M2
  - `Scan` entity, `TriggerScanUseCase`, `JobQueuePort`.
  - Redis-backed `JobQueuePort` adapter.

- **M3.2 — ScannerPort + SemgrepAdapter (first scanner, walking skeleton)** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.1
  - Define `ScannerPort` interface.
  - `SemgrepAdapter`: runs Semgrep against a cloned repo, returns raw SARIF/JSON.
  - This is the **walking skeleton** — first fully working slice from trigger → raw results stored.

- **M3.3 — Worker process** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.2
  - Background worker consumes queue, runs `ScannerPort`, persists raw `ScanResult`.
  - Idempotency: safe to retry a failed scan job (per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9).

- **M3.4 — TrivyAdapter** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.2
  - Second `ScannerPort` implementation — this issue is also the first real test of "adding a scanner only needs an adapter," per the architecture's core promise.

- **M3.5 — ZapAdapter (DAST, optional per project)** — done *(its "allow-list" wording is struck by `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9's 2026-09-15 note: the gate rejects address ranges and allows the rest)*
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.2
  - Drives ZAP Automation Framework via YAML plan against a user-confirmed, allow-listed target.
  - **SSRF protection enforced here** — validate/allow-list target URL before invocation (`PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §11).

- **M3.6 — CI-triggered scanning** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.3
  - GitHub Actions integration / webhook receiver triggers `TriggerScanUseCase` on push.

- **M3.7 — Multi-scanner dispatch** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.3, M3.4, M3.5 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0016-multi-scanner-dispatch.md`**
  - One trigger fans out to every scanner enabled for the project. **Prerequisite for M4 and M5**, not a nice-to-have: `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` (M5.1) exists to link findings from *different* tools, and a `Scan` could previously only ever produce one.
  - **This was drift, not an open design question.** The schema and the architecture doc already assumed N tools per scan — `ScanResultModel`'s `UniqueConstraint(scan_id, tool)`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4's `raw_results: [ScanResult]  # one per tool that ran`, and §8's sequence diagram showing `ScannerPort (Semgrep/Trivy/ZAP)` returning per-tool results. Only the roadmap and the code had diverged from that.
  - **Decided — dispatch shape:** one arq job per `Scan`, scanners concurrent via `asyncio.gather`, **not** fan-out per `(scan, tool)`. The deciding argument turned out not to be the job budget but *snapshot coherence*: fan-out means one checkout per job, so a push landing mid-scan lets Semgrep and Trivy see different commits — which makes M5's cross-tool correlation unsound with nothing recording that it happened. `arq`'s default 300s `job_timeout` had to be raised to 600s, since concurrent wall-clock is `max()` (ZAP's 300s) plus checkout.
  - **Decided — partial-failure semantics:** per-tool `status`/`failure_reason` on `ScanResult` (a failed tool still gets a row), `ScanStatus.PARTIAL`, and `Scan.status` derived from the per-tool outcomes. **M4's entry point is `ScanResultRepositoryPort.get_succeeded_by_scan_id`** — never `Scan.status`, which is a human-facing summary. A DB `CHECK` constraint makes "succeeded implies non-null `raw_output`" true for any writer, so M4's mappers take `str`, not `str | None`.
  - **Decided — scanner configuration:** a `ScannerConfig` entity in `projects` (one row per project, `enabled_tools` array + `zap_target_url`), read by `scanning` through `ScannerConfigRepositoryPort` (rule 3). `SecurityContext` was rejected on lifecycle grounds — detection re-derives it and would clobber configuration. Unconfigured defaults to Semgrep+Trivy, with ZAP structurally undefaultable because only a user can supply its target. Ships with an owner-gated `PUT /projects/{id}/scanner-config`, without which ZAP dispatch would be dead-by-construction.
  - **Decided — `ScannerPort` identity:** gains `tool` (a shared `ScannerTool` enum) *and* `target_kind`, the half the question above did not name — Semgrep/Trivy take a checkout path, ZAP takes a URL, and dispatch routes on that rather than on a `tool == "zap"` branch (rule 4).
  - `ScanStatus` / `ScanResult` schema changes landed with Alembic migrations (`b1c4d7e29a03`, `c2d5e8f31b14`).
  - **SSRF re-validation stays inside `ZapAdapter.run()`** — persisting a validated target does not replace ADR-013's gate, since DNS can rebind between configuration time and scan time, which is the entire reason the gate exists. The write path checks well-formedness only, deliberately, so the stored URL never looks pre-approved.
  - Integration test: one trigger produces `ScanResult` rows for three real tools, and a deliberately failing scanner does not discard a succeeding scanner's output — asserted through `get_succeeded_by_scan_id`, the query M4 will actually use, not merely by both rows existing.

---

## Milestone 4 — Normalization (Weeks 6-7)

**Goal:** raw scanner output becomes the common `Finding` schema. FR-5.

- **M4.0 — Pipeline boundary: what triggers normalization, and what `Scan.status` means once it exists** — done
  Module: `scanning` + `normalization` · Depends on: M3.7 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0017-normalization-trigger-and-pipeline-progress.md`**
  - **Prerequisite for M4.1**, which is why it sits before it rather than being folded into it: M4.1 builds `Finding` and the per-scanner mappers, and cannot say where they are invoked from, what they run on after a partial scan, or what marks the work done. Surfaced by the M3→M4 milestone-boundary review as its most consequential finding, and escalated into an issue for the reason G1 became M3.7 — a finding recorded only as prose is one nobody acts on.
  - **This issue is a decision first and code second** — the questions below get answered and recorded in an ADR (next free number: **0017**) before implementation, the same ordering M3.7 used.
  - **Scope:** the decision, the `ARCHITECTURE.md` reconciliation, and any `ScanStatus`/schema change the decision implies. The `Finding` entity and the per-scanner mapping functions stay M4.1.
  - **The drift being closed.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` §8 shows `TriggerScanUseCase` orchestrating the whole pipeline synchronously — scan → normalize → correlate → score → brief — and setting `status=complete` only once `SecurityBrief` is persisted. The code does something else: `TriggerScanUseCase` creates a `Scan` and enqueues; `RunScanUseCase` ends at persisting `ScanResult` rows and sets `COMPLETED` there. So `COMPLETED` means "every enabled scanner finished", not "the pipeline finished" — and §8, which M4.1 is told to build from, describes a design that was never built.
  - **Decision — what triggers normalization:** inside the same arq job after the scanners return, a separate enqueued job, or lazily on read. The three bullets below state what each actually costs.
  - **Option — inside the same arq job.** The snapshot-coherence argument that decided ADR-016's decision 1 **does not extend here**: normalization consumes persisted `ScanResult` rows, not the checkout, which `RunScanUseCase`'s `finally` deletes anyway. What it buys is a single failure domain. What it costs is an ordering trap with no safe side — appended *after* `self._scans.update(scan)`, a normalization exception still commits `COMPLETED` (`worker.py` commits in `finally`), propagates to arq, and the retry returns immediately at `if scan.status == ScanStatus.COMPLETED: return`, so normalization silently never runs, permanently, with no error state; placed *before* that update, a JSON-parsing bug instead re-runs every scanner on retry, ~64s of container work.
  - **Option — a separate enqueued job.** Decouples the failure domains: a normalization bug retries without re-running scanners. The cost is **not** the fan-in problem M3.7 avoided — that was N jobs racing to decide one `Scan`'s status, whereas this is a linear one-to-one handoff. It is a **dual write**: the Postgres commit and the Redis enqueue cannot be made atomic, so either the commit lands and the enqueue is lost (a `COMPLETED` scan that silently never produces findings) or the job runs before the commit is visible. Name the mitigation — transactional outbox, or an idempotent normalize job plus a reconciliation sweep — rather than leaving the gap implicit.
  - **Option — lazily on read.** Makes normalization latency user-visible at `GET /projects/{id}/findings` (M4.5; M4.3 when this issue was written), and sits badly with the milestones either side: M4.2's dedup ("re-running a scan doesn't duplicate identical findings") needs findings that persist across scans, and §8 has M5 reading `Findings` from the database, not from a request-scoped computation.
  - **Decision — how `PARTIAL` interacts.** A partial scan has succeeded rows worth normalizing and failed ones that aren't; `get_succeeded_by_scan_id` already draws that line. Normalization must run on `PARTIAL` — skipping it would discard a succeeding scanner's output one layer up, the same corruption `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §12 forbids and ADR-016 decision 2 was built to prevent. Decide what the `Scan` looks like afterwards: it must not become `COMPLETED`, or the record that a tool failed is erased. Decide the degenerate case too — a scan where every tool failed has nothing to normalize; is the stage skipped, or does it run and produce zero findings?
  - **Decision — what `ScanStatus` means once M4 exists.** If normalization is a separate stage, `COMPLETED` becomes ambiguous in exactly the way §8 already is. The constraint to design against: ADR-016 decision 2 fixed `Scan.status` as a *derived summary of per-tool outcomes* and had M4 read `get_succeeded_by_scan_id` instead, specifically so a human-facing field never becomes a pipeline's input. Growing the enum with pipeline stages contradicts that and conflates two orthogonal axes — per-tool outcome × pipeline progress — which multiply out as M5, M6 and M7 each add a stage. The alternative is keeping `ScanStatus` scanner-scoped and tracking pipeline progress separately. Decide it and record it; do not let it drift.
  - **Reconcile `ARCHITECTURE.md` in the same change**, per CLAUDE.md's rule that a document gets fixed in the PR that changed the reality — the four items below.
  - **§8's sequence diagram**, redrawn to whatever is decided. It is the spec M4.1 reads, and it is currently a picture of a pipeline that was never built.
  - **§4's `Scan` and `ScanResult`.** `Scan` still has no `failure_reason` (added M3.3, two milestones stale), and `ScanResult` has no field breakdown at all — so M3.7's `status`, `failure_reason` and nullable `raw_output` are absent from the document M4.1 is told to build `Finding` from.
  - **§4's `Finding.source (semgrep|trivy|zap)`**, which should reference `shared_kernel/scanner_tools.py`'s `ScannerTool` rather than re-establishing a literal set — the duplication ADR-016 decision 4 centralised that enum to prevent.
  - **§9's claim that `IngestScanResultUseCase` is idempotent**, about a use case that exists nowhere in `src/`. Whatever this issue names the normalization entry point settles whether that sentence becomes true or gets removed.
  - **Decided — `ScanStatus` stays scanner-scoped.** No enum change, no `scans` migration. Its blast radius turned out to be a single `if`: across all of `src/` the only behavioural reader is `RunScanUseCase`'s `== COMPLETED` retry guard. ADR-016's "today that is the scanning API surface" named a consumer that does not exist — `scanning`'s one endpoint is the webhook, whose ack `status` is a literal string. Corrected in ADR-0017's Context rather than by amending an accepted ADR.
  - **Decided — a separate enqueued job, with the progress row as the outbox.** `NormalizationRun` (one row per scan, `UNIQUE(scan_id)`), owned by `normalization`, written by `RunScanUseCase` through a **primitives-only** port inside the same transaction as the `ScanResult` rows — so the Postgres-commit-plus-Redis-enqueue dual write stops existing rather than being compensated for. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9's unit-of-work rule is amended for stage handoffs specifically. Same-job was rejected as *incompatible with ADR-016*, not merely expensive; lazily-on-read on M4.2/§8 grounds.
  - **Decided — normalization runs on `PARTIAL` and on an all-tools-failed scan**, on one invariant chosen to avoid a second code path: a row exists **iff** `ScanResult` rows were persisted. A `PARTIAL` scan cannot later become `COMPLETED` — structurally, since all three writers of `Scan.status` live in `scanning`.
  - **Two ordering/idempotency constraints, both pinned by tests** rather than left to comments: the handoff row is written *after* the upsert loop and *before* `_scans.update(scan)` (reversed, a failure would commit `COMPLETED` and the retry would short-circuit, losing normalization permanently); and the write is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, never `DO UPDATE`, which would reset a running/completed row to pending.
  - Schema change landed with an Alembic migration (`d4a7c1b8e630`), `upgrade` and `downgrade` both verified.
  - **Deliberately not shipped, trigger M4.4:** the arq enqueue, a `normalize_scan` job, the reconciliation sweep query, and the partial index the sweep will want. With no `Finding` entity they would be dead-by-construction. *Trigger was M4.1 when this issue closed; M4.1 moved it to M4.4, because all four also need the `Finding` table that M4.3 creates — see ADR-0017's Amendments.* Until M4.4 lands the system writes a `pending` row on every scan that nothing advances — intended, and recorded in ADR-0017's Consequences.
  - Integration test: a `PARTIAL` scan leaves a `pending` handoff row and is reachable from `normalization_runs` + `scan_results` alone, with no query in that path touching `scans` — plus round-tripping all four statuses through the real adapter, which is what proves the enum and the `CHECK` agree on the same strings.

**M4 is six issues where this roadmap originally planned three.** M4.0 was inserted by the M3→M4 boundary review; M4.1 then split into four because six of the items it carried all write `Finding` rows, and the table for those does not exist until M4.3. M3 went 6 issues → 7 for the same reason (M3.7 was work M3.4/M3.5/M3.6 kept deferring). Recorded here so the growth is visible rather than discovered.

- **M4.1 — Finding domain model** — done
  Module: `normalization` · Depends on: M4.0 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0018-normalized-severity-and-shared-kernel-scope.md`**
  - `Finding`, `Evidence`, `Location` entities and `shared_kernel`'s `Severity`, per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.
  - **Captured real Semgrep/Trivy/ZAP output as fixtures before writing the mapping functions.** Landed in `tests/fixtures/scanners/`, at the repo root of `tests/` rather than under `unit/` so M4.4's integration test reads the same files through the same `scanner_fixture` conftest loader — otherwise one issue's files get moved in another issue's diff. Provenance, byte counts, redactions and coverage gaps are in that directory's README.
  - **Correction to this issue's original premise, which was wrong.** It said a single run of M3.7's `test_multi_scanner_dispatch.py` "yields the whole set". It does not: that test scans `octocat/Hello-World`, which holds a README and nothing else, against a one-rule Python-only ruleset — so Semgrep returns `results: []` and Trivy finds no manifest, and two of three fixtures would have been empty envelopes. The per-adapter targets were used instead; each already has a passing test asserting a real finding against it.
  - Pure mapping functions per scanner (Semgrep→Finding, Trivy→Finding, Zap→Finding), unit tested against those fixtures plus labelled synthetic ones for the rows real output does not reach (Trivy emits no `CRITICAL`/`LOW`/`UNKNOWN` against this target; ZAP emits no riskcode `0` or `3`).
  - **Decided — the normalized severity scale, and `shared_kernel/`'s admission criterion.** Six members including `UNKNOWN`; `native_severity` keeps what the collapse discards. `shared_kernel/` now has a written rule — *closed vocabularies two or more modules must compare or order, not merely transport* — applied three times in the issue, twice in the negative (`Confidence` and `Location` both declined). `Severity` overrides its comparison operators because a plain `StrEnum` makes `HIGH >= CRITICAL` true alphabetically, which would put a silent lie inside `risk_engine`.
  - **Deliberately not in `Finding`:** `confidence` (→ M6.1) and `dedup_hash` (→ M4.2). Both were pinned absent by one test so re-adding either was deliberate. *M4.2 added `dedup_hash` and narrowed that test to `confidence` alone; the `confidence` half still holds.*
  - **Moved out of this issue, to M4.4:** the arq enqueue, the `normalize_scan` job, the reconciliation sweep, the partial index, the `started_at`/`finished_at` state machine, and the `PARTIAL`-scan integration test. All six write `Finding` rows and the Postgres adapter for those is M4.3, so shipping them here would have been the dead-by-construction code ADR-0017 deferred them to avoid. ADR-0017's trigger was corrected to M4.4 in an `## Amendments` entry in the same change.
  - Verified: 267 unit tests pass, ruff/format clean, mypy 175 files with suppressions still 0/0, import-linter 17/17, check_claims 4/4. No `src/` file outside `normalization/domain/` and `shared_kernel/severity.py` changed; no migration, no port, no adapter.

- **M4.2 — Deduplication and `Finding` identity** — done
  Module: `normalization` · Depends on: M4.1 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0019-finding-identity-and-deduplication.md`**
  - Dedup hash strategy; re-running a scan doesn't duplicate identical findings.
  - **Resolves G5.** Landing *before* M4.3 persists anything is what made the register's `Status: assigned → M4.2` safe: no wrong shape ever reached disk. Deciding the hash without deciding identity is not possible in either order, which is why they were one issue.
  - **Decided — the sighting model.** `Finding` is durable and project-scoped, identified by a derived `dedup_hash` property; `FindingSighting` (`finding_id`, `scan_id`, `observed_at`, `match_count`) records each scan's observation, with a composite natural key and no surrogate id. `Finding.scan_id` is gone; `Evidence` gains `scan_id` as the provenance of the retained payload. Deliberately no `last_seen_*` on `Finding` — both are `max()` over sightings, and a denormalized copy would put a silently-staleable summary into M9.1's path.
  - **Decided — one hash function over the common schema, which required closing a hole in it.** The schema had no field for *what fired*: `check_id`, `VulnerabilityID` and `alertRef` were melted into `title` beside advisory-mutable prose, so hashing `title` would re-key every Trivy finding on an advisory rewording. `Finding.rule_id` is the normalized identifier, and `compute_dedup_hash` is one pure function over `source` + `rule_id` + `file_path` + `package` + `url` + `http_method` + `parameter` — never per-tool, so rule 4 holds and M5 compares keys with one definition. `"v1:<sha256>"`, version-prefixed because the input set is a contract; a bump re-normalizes from the retained `ScanResult.raw_output`.
  - **Decided — the exclusions, on one stated principle:** *where identity is uncertain, prefer the failure that under-counts over the failure that fabricates events.* Line numbers are out (a refactor would otherwise re-key an untouched finding — `match_count` is the receipt for the two-matches-in-one-file collapse it costs); `installed_version` is out (a bump between two vulnerable versions fixes nothing); severity, CVSS, CWE, OWASP and title are out (an NVD rescore must not re-key).
  - **Decided — one `Finding` per (alert, instance) for ZAP**, discharging ADR-0018's deferral. The measurement settled it: one alert's instances arrive in crawl order (ids `6, 7, 5`), so an alert-level finding's `instances[0]` location is not stable across scans, and fixing one URL out of three would be invisible to M9.1. `zap_scan.json` goes 4 findings → 11; *(**M5.1:** both figures are M4.2 measurements against the pre-G23 corpus and are left as the record of that decision. Against the current fixture the same mapping is **5 alerts → 13 findings**, and the crawl-order ids are `6, 5, 1, 0` — the non-monotonicity the decision rests on is unchanged. See ADR-0019's Amendments, 2026-08-24.)* evidence 8,523 → 18,148 bytes (2.13×), which raises a one-time constant and leaves the per-scan term untouched. `raw_payload` is the alert with `instances` narrowed to one — ADR-0018 decision 6 clarified, not amended.
  - **Decided — `Evidence` stays 1:1 with the `Finding` and is refreshed latest-wins**, unconditionally, with the cost named: a later poorer payload replaces a richer earlier one. FR-9 asks for the output that produced *this* assessment, and ranking payloads by "richness" would need per-tool comparison. M9.2's before/after evidence view is the trigger for per-sighting retention.
  - **Decided — `dedup_hash` stays in `normalization/domain/`**, ADR-0018's `shared_kernel/` criterion applied a third time in the negative: it is an open-valued string only `normalization` compares.
  - **Deliberately not shipped, trigger M4.3 — *discharged in M4.3*:** `normalization_runs.project_id`, its migration, the port signature and the `RunScanUseCase` call site. So the three mappers take a `project_id` no production path yet supplies — the *mappers*, not `compute_dedup_hash`, which excludes the project by design (scope is the `UNIQUE` constraint, not a hash input). Acceptable because they are pure and their tests pass one directly, so they are executable and verified (ADR-0017 decision 2's deferred-*consumer* case, not ADR-016 decision 3's dead-by-construction one).
  - **Opened G9**, found while verifying the identity inputs: Semgrep's `path` is an absolute per-scan temp path in production, and the fixture's redaction hides it.
  - Verified: 299 unit tests pass (418 in the full suite, plus the 5 known Trivy-binary failures — **local run**), ruff/format clean with `ruff check --diff` empty over `src/` and `tests/` (G8), mypy 176 files with suppressions still 0/0, import-linter 17/17, check_claims 4/4. No `src/` file outside `normalization/domain/` changed; no migration, no port, no adapter.

- **M4.3 — `Finding` persistence — done**
  Module: `normalization` · Depends on: M4.2
  - Postgres adapter and Alembic migration for the shape M4.2 decided. `FindingRepositoryPort`, `Base` from `platform/db.py` (rule 8), string IDs (rule 9).
  - Depends on M4.2 rather than M4.1 deliberately: the table cannot be created before the identity decision that determines its keys.
  - **`UNIQUE(project_id, dedup_hash)`** — the scope is the constraint, not a hash input (ADR-0019 decision 3). Upsert semantics keep the stored `id` and refresh the mutable attributes; `merge_observation` in `normalization/domain/finding.py` is the executable spec for what is frozen and what is refreshed, so the policy is not re-invented in an `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` clause. The `dedup_hash` column is a materialization of the entity's derived property, not a second source of truth.
  - **`finding_sightings`, keyed `(finding_id, scan_id)`** with `observed_at` and `match_count`, no surrogate id. It is what makes "was this present in scan N?" answerable, and M9.1 depends on it.
  - **`normalization_runs` gains `project_id`** — decided in ADR-0019 decision 7 and implemented here, because normalization can reach the dedup scope no other way: the reconciliation sweep selects on that table alone (ADR-0017 decision 2), so a read port back into `scanning` could not supply it. Column with **no foreign key**, following `scan_id`'s precedent in the same table. Existing rows have none, so the migration is **add-nullable → backfill from `scans` → set `NOT NULL`**, not a bare `NOT NULL` add. `NormalizationRunRepositoryPort.request` gains the parameter, `RunScanUseCase`'s call site passes `scan.project_id`, and ADR-0017 gets an `## Amendments` entry. `NormalizationRun.__post_init__` and both `CHECK` constraints are unaffected — their invariants are about `status`/`failure_reason`.
  - Ships persistence with no production writer, which is this project's established shape for a deferral (ADR-016 decision 3 cites persistence in M2.1 and `di.py` wiring ahead of a route). The writer arrives in M4.4.
  - **Hydration must reconstruct `Severity(...)`, not leave a bare `str`.** `Severity.HIGH == "high"` is True but `Severity.HIGH >= "high"` **raises** — equality survives a `String` column, ordering does not, and ordering is the half M5/M6/M8 use (ADR-0018 decision 2). Same for `ScannerTool`.
  - **Decided — ADR-0020, on the one question ADR-0019 left open: how a pure function and one SQL statement stay in agreement.** `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` **transcribes** `merge_observation` rather than re-deciding it, because that function's refresh set is total except the two surrogate ids — so the `SET` clause is exactly `_RULE_LEVEL_ATTRIBUTES` plus the three `Location` fields the hash excludes, and `id` being absent from it *is* `id=existing.id`. Read-modify-write rejected on a window the obvious bound misses: `UNIQUE(scan_id)` bounds duplicate jobs for **one** scan, not two scans of the same project normalizing concurrently — which is two pushes in quick succession — so it would hit the `IntegrityError` ADR-014 and ADR-0017 both rejected. Three test layers hold the agreement (column partition, refresh-set derived from `dataclasses.fields`/`inspect.signature`, and a whole-object comparison against `merge_observation`'s own output); mutation-verified — dropping one column from the SET clause fails all three. The equivalence **expires** if a field must ever *not* refresh, with M6.1's `confidence` named.
  - **`upsert` returns the resolved `Finding`**, unlike `NormalizationRunRepositoryPort.request`'s `None`: identity is the hash and `id` is a surrogate, so only the upsert settles which id wins, and M4.4 cannot write a sighting without it. `record_sighting` overwrites a per-scan **total** rather than summing — retries are guaranteed (ADR-016 decision 1), so summing double-counts silently and only overwriting is idempotent, which makes "total, never an increment" a precondition on the caller, stated in the port docstring and pinned by a test.
  - **Closed the model/migration gap rather than registering it.** The integration suite now builds its schema with `alembic upgrade head` instead of `Base.metadata.create_all`, plus `test_schema_matches_models.py` comparing tables, columns and named constraints both ways. It had been holding only by undocumented step ordering in `ci.yml` — migrations run before `pytest` against the same service, and `create_all(checkfirst=True)` skips existing tables, constraints included — which was invisible locally and covered only constraints some test exercised. This issue tripled the constraint surface, so a `CHECK` on a model and missing from its migration would have passed the suite and failed in production. **Rule 8 becomes partially mechanical** as a side effect, recorded in `CLAUDE.md`'s Tier 1 table. Mutation-verified, and the first draft was *vacuous* — `Base.metadata` holds only the tables whose modules have been imported, so the comparison has to run in both directions.
  - **Opened G10**, measured while checking ADR-0019's ruleset-path caveat: Semgrep's `check_id` is the config path relative to the **worker's CWD**, not merely the file's location — and **G9's own prescribed fix fires it**.
  - **Opened G11**: three tables now depend on `scans` and `projects` never being deleted, with no foreign key recording it. **Opened G12**, found by the review of this change: `pre-commit`'s pinned ruff (v0.6.9) and CI's resolved ruff (0.16.3) disagree, so a green hook does not mean a green build — measured, with the hook passing exactly the file CI rejected.
  - Verified: 450 passed (299 unit) plus the 5 known Trivy-binary failures (**local run**), `alembic upgrade head` → `downgrade -1` → `upgrade head` all clean, mypy 177 files with suppressions still 0/0, import-linter 17/17, check_claims 4/4, `ruff check --diff` empty over both `src/` and `tests/` (G8), and `ruff check .` / `ruff format --check .` clean **over the repo root, which is what `ci.yml` runs** — scoping the format check to `src/ tests/ alembic/` was how a Markdown code block in ADR-0020 passed review and would have failed CI. Opened **G12** for the version skew underneath that.

- **M4.4 — Close the scan→normalize handoff — done**
  Module: `normalization` + `platform` · Depends on: M4.3 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0021-normalization-job-execution.md`**
  - The four items ADR-0017 deferred, plus the state machine and the acceptance test that need them. This is the issue where the pipeline first actually runs end to end.
  - `NormalizeScanUseCase`, reading `get_succeeded_by_scan_id` and never `Scan.status`; a `normalize_scan` arq job registered in `WorkerSettings.functions`; the enqueue from `run_scan`, which belongs **after** the commit since the row is the record and the enqueue is only a latency optimization.
  - The reconciliation sweep, which **must select on `normalization_runs` alone and never read `Scan.status`** (ADR-0017 decision 2, stated there as an invariant), plus the partial index in the migration that ships the query it serves.
    - **This bullet said "`normalization_runs` and `scan_results`" until M4.4, and was wrong** — contradicting, in the same sentence, the ADR it cites. ADR-0017 decision 2 is explicit that no join to `ScanResult` is needed: by decision 3's invariant the row exists **iff** `ScanResult` rows were persisted, so the row's own existence already carries what such a join would establish. Corrected rather than reinterpreted, because a sweep built from this bullet would have taken exactly the dependency the invariant exists to forbid.
    - **Shipped selecting `pending` AND `running`, with the index predicate matching** — a deviation from the `WHERE status = 'pending'` ADR-0017 anticipated, argued in ADR-0021 decision 2 on an asymmetry: a pending-only sweep can never recover a row a killed worker left `running` (silent and permanent), while the failure it risks instead — re-enqueuing a live job — is a no-op via arq's job-id dedup. Threshold **900s, derived from `WorkerSettings.job_timeout` (600)** and pinned as a constraint by `test_sweep_settings.py` rather than left as a comment, since a later `job_timeout` bump would otherwise silently invert the trade.
    - **The invariant is proved behaviourally, not by reading the query**: `test_normalization_sweep.py` sweeps a `pending` row whose `scan_id` names a scan that does not exist — legal by construction (no foreign key; G11), and something every implementation reading `scans` by any route returns nothing for. Mutation-tested by adding the forbidden filter; two tests failed.
  - The `started_at`/`finished_at` state machine, left unconstrained in both the domain guard and the `CHECK` because those transitions are this issue's to define.
    - **Defined and constrained in both places** (ADR-0021 decision 3): `NormalizationRun` gains `start`/`complete`/`fail`, `__post_init__` gains a per-status timestamp invariant, and `ck_normalization_runs_timestamp_shape` mirrors it. **`COMPLETED` is the only terminal state** — `FAILED` is deliberately re-claimable, since a terminal `FAILED` would make arq's retry reach the claim, get `None`, and silently do nothing. The SQL constraint is written as implications rather than an exhaustive disjunction so a row with an unrecognised status violates `ck_normalization_runs_status` **alone**; the disjunction form made two pre-existing tests pass by depending on Postgres's constraint-evaluation order, which is how that was found.
  - **Integration test: a `PARTIAL` scan is normalized, produces findings from the succeeded tools only, and the failed tool contributes none** — asserted through `get_succeeded_by_scan_id`, not by row counts alone. Build it like `test_normalization_handoff.py`: real Postgres, **in-process fake scanners fed M4.1's committed fixtures as their `raw_output`**. Reusing `test_multi_scanner_dispatch.py`'s three real adapters would add a fourth ZAP-class test, and per `CLAUDE.md` roughly three more of those trip the 120s CI split. *(Moot 2026-09-15, not false: the arithmetic was `CLAUDE.md`'s at the time, and one active-scan test crossed the threshold at M5.9 — `CLAUDE.md`'s 2026-09-08 note.)*
  - **Prerequisite: G9 and G10 — both closed here.** They are one problem, because one lever moves both fields: `cwd` governs `path` *and* `check_id`, and both are `dedup_hash` inputs, so G9's own prescribed fix (`cwd=target`, pass `.`) flips `check_id` to the absolute dotted install path and re-keys every Semgrep finding through `rule_id` in the same change. Resolved by adding **`--no-rewrite-rule-ids`**, semgrep's own documented switch, which takes the path out of `check_id` entirely and thereby decouples the two. Measured across four CWD/argument combinations; the table is in ADR-0019's Amendments. **G10's proposed mitigation — pinning the ruleset path in the container image — was dropped rather than deferred**: it makes the value stable within one deployment and different between a developer's machine, CI and production, which is the disconnected verification G8 and G9 both record.
    - **The adjacent Trivy unknown is closed, and the existing capture already answered it.** G9's note distrusted the fixture for being captured inside a container mount, but the mount is what makes it *valid*: the question is whether an absolute argument is echoed into `Results[].Target`, and `/target` is absolute. Re-measured against `aquasec/trivy` with a mount path shaped like `GitRepoCheckout`'s `mkdtemp` output — `ArtifactName` echoes the absolute path, `Results[].Target` is the relative `requirements.txt`. `map_trivy_output` reads `Target`, so **Trivy was never affected and `TrivyAdapter` needs no change.**
    - **The committed Semgrep fixture was half pre-fix and half post-fix** — the CWD-dependent `check_id` beside a `path` already redacted to the relative form the fix produces, so one hash input was asserted in its broken shape and the other in its fixed shape, in one file. Both are now the production shape, and `test_semgrep_mapper.py`'s two `endswith("dangerous-eval")` assertions became equalities: the suffix form passed for all three CWD-dependent values and could not have caught any of this.
  - **Sightings are written here, never by a mapper**, against the ids M4.3's upsert resolves, via `collapse_by_identity` — the pure within-a-scan counterpart to `merge_observation` that M4.2 shipped with tests and no production caller.
  - **Decide what the use case does when `collapse_by_identity` raises**, which M4.2 left open for want of a caller (ADR-0019 decision 2). It raises when a collapsed group disagrees on a rule-level attribute; that is normally a mapper bug, but it is *reachable from data* through the `"(unidentified)"` `rule_id` fallback — two findings whose tool named no rule, at the same location, share an identity and will usually differ on `title`. Letting it propagate aborts a whole scan's normalization over one malformed element, which is the `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §12 corruption the `Location` guard was removed to avoid, arriving through a different validation rule. Latent, not live, and measured rather than assumed: **all 30 elements across the six committed fixtures carry their tool's identifier — 0 missing** (`check_id`, `VulnerabilityID`, `alertRef`/`pluginid`), so the fallback is unreached by anything ever captured. *(**M5.1:** 39 elements after G23's re-capture, still 0 missing — the bound holds and this bullet's reasoning is unaffected. See G20.)* The options are skipping the element, failing the run, or marking `NormalizationRun` failed — all of which need this issue's error-handling context. **Whatever is chosen is pinned by a test over a synthetic element with no identifier**, since the decision is only half the criterion: an unexercised choice here is one that gets reverted by the next refactor.
  - Closes the window, open since M4.0, in which every scan writes a `pending` progress row that nothing advances.

- **M4.5 — Findings read API — done**
  Module: `normalization` + `projects` + `platform` · Depends on: M4.4 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0022-findings-read-api-surface.md`**
  - `GET /projects/{id}/findings`, plus `GET /projects/{id}/findings/{finding_id}/evidence`. Depends on M4.4, not M4.3: an endpoint shipped before the writer would return an empty list for a whole issue, which is dead-by-construction on the read side.
  - **Decided — two routes, because FR-9 asks for a *link* and rule 12's hazard is the bulk shape.** The listing returns evidence metadata (`scan_id`, `source_tool`, `captured_at`, `payload_chars`) and never `raw_payload`; an addressable route returns the payload one finding at a time. Measured: the committed fixtures' 34 findings carry **93,792 characters** of scanned source (24 and 71,532 before the M5.1 re-capture), and inlining it would make every listing a source-code export with nothing in its signature saying so. A `?include_evidence=true` flag is the same exposure behind a parameter and leaves no distinct record of what was read. The evidence route's docstring carries the rule-12 statement, and **G7's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` now names that route** rather than "M4.5" in the abstract.
  - **Decided — `payload_truncated`, named for the fact rather than the test.** It asserts "this payload is an incomplete prefix of its source element", not "this is valid JSON"; the two coincide only while a character slice is the sole lossy step, and **G16** carries the trigger for when they stop. A stored `Evidence.truncated` column is refused on a measurement, not a preference: the largest payload ever produced is **9,888 chars against a 20,000 cap (49%)**, so the cap has never fired and the column would describe a state nothing has reached (ADR-016 decision 3's shape). A length test was rejected outright — it *fabricates* truncation at exactly the cap.
  - **Decided — `ProjectAccessPort`, a verdict-returning port `projects` publishes**, because this is the first project-scoped read outside that module and there was no cross-module precedent. `ProjectMembershipRepositoryPort` is a *persistence* port, so consuming it would put "authorization means a membership row exists" — `projects`' domain knowledge — in every consuming module, one copy each, and a later VIEWER role would silently not reach them. The rule stays as `domain/authorization.may_read`. **One method, so both denials are 404**: with no vocabulary for which reason applied, a non-member cannot distinguish an existing project from an absent one. M5.2, M6.3, M7.2 and M8.2 inherit this shape.
  - **Opened G17 and G18, split rather than combined.** G17 is the divergence this issue introduces (findings 404, `projects` 403) and its real cost is that **the 404 conceals nothing while a sibling route still answers 403** — existence is recoverable with a second request. G18 is the pre-existing enumeration leak on `projects`' six routes, there since M1.4/M2. Their fixes point different ways: G17 is closed by converging, and *which way* is G18's decision, so closing G18 as "accepted" leaves G17 open. Both `assigned → M10.2`.
  - **Decided — the envelope says whether the list is complete, and `unfinished_runs` is the load-bearing half.** `latest_run` alone would report `completed` for a project whose three earlier scans never normalized — G15's exact shape. Two new within-module port methods, answerable only because ADR-0019 decision 7 put `project_id` on `normalization_runs`. `failure_reason` is safe to return **because M4.4 made it type-only**, naming this endpoint as the reason — and **the assertion pinning that lives in M4.4's `test_normalize_scan.py`, not here**, which an earlier draft of this bullet got wrong in the direction that matters: a pointer to a test the consuming issue does not ship is the G8 shape. M4.5 consumes the guarantee and adds no second copy. One thing it does add: `failure_reason` carries `dedup_hash` values on the skip branch, so **that field is the one place a hash leaves the system** — deliberate, since the identifier is what makes the message actionable, and pinned by its own route test rather than left to the "never exposed" claim, which was too broad. **G15 is narrowed, not closed** — the recovery half is untouched — and its claim that the reason is reachable "only by querying Postgres by hand until M4.5" is now corrected.
  - **`ix_finding_sightings_scan_id` does NOT ship here, and this bullet said it would.** It assumed a "still open" filter; there is none, so there is no scan-first query, so ADR-0017's "an index without its query is a guess" applies with nothing against it. The per-finding sighting summary correlates on `finding_id`, the sightings PK's leading column. **The index and its query go to M9.1 together** — see that issue's entry, which now carries all four items rather than leaving them scattered across two ADRs' Consequences and a bullet here. What ships instead is `ix_normalization_runs_project_id` on `(project_id, requested_at DESC)`, carrying the two queries above on a table that had no index at all and grows one row per scan forever.
  - **Decided — project-scoped and scan-independent; no `?status=open`.** It is the strongest product story available and that is why it is the trap: it needs "the latest scan for this project" (no such method on `ScanRepositoryPort`) *and* the succeeded-tools scoping ADR-0019's Consequences requires. Without the second, one failed Trivy silently resolves every dependency finding in the project — G4's shape. The endpoint exposes when a finding was last seen and never whether it is resolved; a test asserts the response carries no such field.
  - **`min_severity` coerces at one named line in the adapter**, explicitly rather than via `Annotated[Severity | None, Query()]`, so "where does coercion happen" is a line a test can point at rather than a framework detail (ADR-0018 decision 2). Filtering is `severity IN (…)` over members with `rank >= minimum.rank`; ordering is a `CASE` generated from `Severity.rank`, so there is no second copy of the scale. **One sharp consequence, documented and pinned**: `UNKNOWN` ranks below `INFO`, so any `min_severity` but `unknown` excludes findings whose severity no tool could determine.
  - **`EXPLAIN`ed at real volume, and the measurement reversed the shape.** 100k findings / 300k sightings via the versioned `scripts/seed_findings_benchmark.py`. The first implementation aggregated sightings whole-table and merge-joined the page: **763 ms**, a seq scan of 300k rows with a 22 MB disk sort to return 50. Rewritten as a LATERAL correlated to one finding: **3.07 ms**, 250×. **The near-miss is the more useful half** — it first measured 8.8 ms because the synthetic ids were sequential, which clustered one project's findings and let the merge join terminate early; real ids are UUIDs (rule 9) and do not. The generator now emits UUID-shaped ids and says why at the top of the file, because a benchmark whose data lacks a property production has is G8/G9's disconnected verification arriving through a seed script — **registered as G19**, since a comment in that file cannot reach M5.3's correlation fixture set. **ADR-0019's `last_seen` cache invitation re-read and declined**: the problem was never that aggregating is expensive, it was aggregating every finding to serve one page, and correlating fixed it without denormalizing anything.
  - Verified: **553 passed (355 unit)** plus the 5 known Trivy-binary failures (**local run** — they come from Trivy not being on the local `PATH`; CI installs it and reads 558/558, confirmed at M5.0), up from M4.4's 510. *(Corrected at the M4→M5 boundary review from "552 passed … which is exactly the 18 unit and 24 integration tests this issue adds". The total is re-derived rather than restated: `pytest --collect-only -q` reports **558 collected**, and 558 − the 5 local-only failures = **553**. The old figure was one low, and its "exactly 18 + 24" clause accounted for 42 of the 43 tests actually added — so the arithmetic was self-consistent and wrong, which is why the count is now stated as a measurement with its command rather than as a difference between two remembered totals. Which of the two summands is off by one is not recoverable without re-collecting at M4.4's commit, and is not worth a checkout; the verified number is the total.)* `alembic upgrade head → downgrade -1 → upgrade head` clean; mypy 188 files with suppressions still 0/0; import-linter 17/17; check_claims 4/4; `ruff check .` and `ruff format --check .` over the repo root, which is what `ci.yml` runs (G12). The rule-12 listing assertion was mutation-tested by adding `raw_payload` to `FindingResponse` and confirming it fails. **`CLAUDE.md`'s runtime baseline was left at 64s pending a CI reading**, since no ZAP-class container-bound test was added and that file says to measure in CI rather than locally. *(That reading arrived and this sentence outlived it by one milestone: the M4→M5 boundary review read this very commit's CI run — `32715167778` — at **71s** and moved the baseline there. Corrected rather than left standing, because "unchanged pending a CI reading" is a present-tense status claim that had quietly become false, which is the exact drift class review step 2 exists to catch — and it survived a pass whose own edit was on this line.)*

---

## Milestone 5 — Correlation Engine (Weeks 7-9)

**Goal:** related findings become a single candidate Risk, grouped on the asset/location signals FR-6 admits. FR-6. This is one of the two "star" components.

***(Goal rewritten 2026-08-25, and dated rather than overwritten because a milestone's goal changing is a fact this project records. It read: "related findings **across tools** become a single candidate Risk." That is falsified by M5.1's measurement — no equality over any of the twelve candidate signals correlates any pair of the three tools, and thirty-two of the thirty-six tool-pair/signal cells are empty for reasons no corpus can change (ADR-0023's Amendments). What M5 delivers is **intra-tool** grouping: Trivy 20 findings → 3 by `package`, ZAP 13 → 4 by `url`. Cross-tool is not abandoned — it is conditioned on **G6**, on **G24 + G27** for the SAST↔DAST route, or on a target whose `file_path` vocabularies overlap; see M5.1 and M5.3. Note also that `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §5 defines Correlation as linking findings from "different tools/sources" while FR-6 restricts by signal kind and says nothing about tool count: the two disagree, M5 is built on FR-6's reading, and M5.1 records the disagreement without reconciling it.)***

***(Second amendment, 2026-08-25, from the active-scan probe — dated by its cause rather than only by its date, since the amendment above carries the same date and a reader needs to tell them apart. **The Goal sentence itself is not rewritten a third time, and that is the finding rather than an omission.** The amendment above already replaced "across tools" with a formulation that names neither tool count nor tool identity, so the sentence survives a change that moves in the opposite direction — which is what a goal line stated in terms of *what is grouped* rather than *how many tools contributed* buys, and is worth noticing before the next rewrite. What changes is the parenthetical above's closing clause. It conditions the SAST↔DAST route on "**G24 + G27**" as though both were open questions of the same kind; **G24's half is now measured, and measured in the affirmative.** ZAP's active scanner does detect the `eval()` sink — alert `90036` "Server Side Template Injection (Blind)", riskcode 3, confidence 3, param `expr`, on `/calculate`, established by time delay rather than by response matching. So the correct reading of that clause is now: cross-tool by **field equality** is still empty **on the committed passive-only corpus**, and the narrowing above stands unchanged on that evidence — with one exception this milestone must not misstate, since the same probe produces it: under an **active** plan the `cwe` cell on Trivy↔ZAP is **not** empty, and the single pair it yields is a false match (a confidence-1 ZAP alert against an unrelated Werkzeug CVE), which is why ADR-0023 Decision B is *strengthened* by it rather than reversed. Cross-tool by **derived location** is no longer unobtainable, and what stands between M5 and it is three issues rather than an open scanner question — **M5.4** (make the finding exist, behind consent), **M5.5** (make comparing it well-founded, G27), **M5.6** (build the derivation). The narrowing and this widening rest on different measurements of different things and neither supersedes the other: the first is about equality over twelve nominated fields, the second about whether a DAST finding about the sink can exist at all. See G24's `Note (2026-08-25, active-scan probe)` for the measurement and ADR-0023's amendment for what it does to Decisions B and C.)***

***(Dated 2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary: the three issues have landed. M5.4, M5.5 and M5.6 shipped, and production produces the `/calculate` `{semgrep, zap}` group for a declared Flask project (`tests/integration/test_derived_group_end_to_end.py`). What M5 delivers is intra-tool grouping plus that one cross-tool route, conditioned on the declaration.)***

- **M5.0 — CI toolchain version pass** — done
  Module: `platform` · Depends on: — · **Resolves G3 and G12**
  - **Not a design prerequisite for M5.1, and the distinction matters because M5.1 has one of its own directly below.** M4.0 was inserted by a boundary review because M4.1 could not start without its answers. This issue is the opposite: M5.1 can start without it, and nothing in M5's design depends on it. It sits at a boundary because a boundary is the only point in this project's cadence where a non-feature issue is not bundling — `CLAUDE.md`'s "one roadmap issue at a time, don't bundle unrelated changes" is precisely why G3 and G12 have each been deferred rather than done. Do not read the M4.0 precedent as "boundary reviews insert design issues".
  - **G3 is the reason this issue exists; G12 rides along because the pass is open, not because it earned one.** G3's trigger is the only one in the Deferred gaps register that fires without anybody doing anything — every other entry waits on someone editing a mapper, writing a seed script, adding a token or proposing a retention policy, while G3 waits on GitHub removing a compatibility shim, on no schedule visible from here. When it fires, *all of CI fails at once*, which makes every M5 issue's definition of done unsatisfiable until it is fixed, under exactly the time pressure G3's own rationale says the work should not be done under. G12 by contrast fails **loudly** — a red CI, roughly two minutes, obvious cause — and has been caught once already; on its own it would stay deferred.
  - **G3:** upgrade `actions/checkout`, `actions/cache` and `astral-sh/setup-uv`. Read each major's changes rather than bumping blind — seven `setup-uv` majors alone, including cache handling and new `python-version`/`activate-environment` inputs that could change which interpreter CI runs. **Pin an exact version or a SHA**, consistent with `aquasecurity/setup-trivy@81e5143…`, because `setup-uv` stopped publishing floating major tags after v7 (`v8`/`v9`/`v10` do not resolve). Verify the Node-runtime annotation is gone from a real run, not just that CI is green.
    - **The targets this bullet named were stale, and the first thing the issue did was find that out.** It said checkout v4→**v5** and cache v4→**v5**, copied from G3. Checked against the upstream repositories per ADR-0009: checkout is at **v7.0.1** and cache at **v6.1.0**, with v5 and v6 both real intermediate majors. All of them declare `node24`, so the stated targets would have satisfied the trigger — the entry was wrong about the distance, not the fix, and taking it at its word would have shipped two majors of known debt in the commit that closes a gap opened by exactly that. **ADR-0009 governs claims made by this register too, not only by dependencies.** Corrected in G3 rather than annotated, dated, per the treatment G4's falsified measurement received.
  - **Explicitly not in scope:** the `actions/cache` save/restore failures on every run since run #1. They are GitHub-side, affect both cache consumers independently, and upgrading is not expected to fix them — G3 says so and this issue does not quietly acquire them.
  - **G12:** make one ruff version definitionally true for both `pre-commit` and CI. Two options and they are **not** equivalent: pin `.pre-commit-config.yaml`'s hook to the exact version `uv.lock` resolves and keep them in step by hand, or drop the `ruff-pre-commit` repo for a `local` hook invoking `uv run ruff`. **The second is the one that cannot drift again** and is the recommendation.
  - Before staging, run `ruff check --diff` and `ruff format --diff` over `src/`, `tests/` **and the repo root** and read what changes. Ten minor versions is real rewrite surface over a codebase whose tests G8 records being silently weakened once — a `--fix` rewrite landing in this issue is the exact failure G8 describes, arriving inside the issue that changes the tool.
  - **Re-read G8 as part of this**, since its analysis of which rules `--fix` can rewrite was performed against the *pinned* hook and therefore against the wrong rule set. Whether that analysis survives the bump is this issue's to state.
  - **Delivered.** All four actions SHA-pinned at the newest major, with the pinning convention now a comment at the top of `ci.yml`'s `steps:` and `setup-trivy` recast as its origin rather than its exception. `ruff-pre-commit` replaced by two `local` hooks running `uv run ruff`, `ruff-format`'s selectors carrying `markdown` deliberately. `[tool.ruff.lint] unfixable` added for the **30** expression-rewriting codes. **No `src/` file touched**, and `CLAUDE.md`'s Tier 1 table deliberately unchanged, because no CI *step* was added, removed or renamed — only `uses:` values and comments, which `check_claims` does not read.
    - **The measurement that changed a decision:** G12's recorded mechanism was wrong. Running both binaries over the repo root, `ruff format --check .` reports **278 files under v0.6.9 and 308 under 0.16.3** — 278 `.py` plus exactly 30 `.md`, because 0.16.3's formatter reads Python blocks inside Markdown and 0.6.9's does not. It was never the ten-version rule gap; both trees were clean under both. That is what ruled out simply bumping the `rev`: upstream's hooks are still `types_or: [python, pyi, jupyter]` at v0.16.3, so the ADR code block that produced G12 would have gone on slipping past a correctly-versioned hook.
    - **G8 narrowed, not closed**, with the residual named in its entry: `unfixable` covers **30** codes, `--fix` still rewrites anything else, and `check_claims` cannot see ruff config either way. Its analysis re-derived against the right tool — **111** autofixable rules under v0.6.9, **122** under 0.16.3, with `E402`'s import-relocating fix newly among them. *(The first re-derivation gave 130 and 142; it dropped every `C4` rule and counted preview rules that cannot fire. G8 carries the correction and the 18 comprehension autofixes it turned up, all now guarded.)* Bounded in the other direction too: the 30 new Markdown files are a *formatting* surface, not a `--fix` one, since `ruff check docs/` reports "No Python files found".
  - **Post-push readings from run `32739032131` (commit `713a50e`), recorded including the ones that changed nothing — so nobody re-measures them wondering whether anyone looked.**
    - **The Node 20 annotation is gone, and so are both cache annotations**: `annotations_count` 3 → **0**, empty annotations array. G3 carries the full before/after and the correction to its own Note, which had predicted the cache failures would survive.
    - **Runtime: measured, in band, anchor deliberately not moved.** The `Tests (pytest — unit + integration)` step reads **69s** by the jobs API's second-granularity timestamps (14:30:54Z → 14:32:03Z), with pytest's own total at **67.71s** — the gap being process start/teardown, which `CLAUDE.md` already describes. The recorded baseline is **71s ±8s**, so both figures sit inside 63–79s. Per that file's own instruction the baseline **stays at 71s**: a reading inside the band is not evidence, and re-anchoring on one would turn ordinary noise into a trend. The toolchain change therefore cost nothing measurable, which is the expected result and is worth having on the record as a measurement rather than an assumption.
    - **558 passed, 0 failed.** The 5 Trivy-binary failures that M4.2–M4.5's `Verified:` lines mention were always **local-only** — they come from Trivy not being on the local `PATH`, and CI installs it, so CI never had them. Those three lines are records of local runs and are not wrong; none of them claims CI. But none says "local" either, and M4.5's line does arithmetic on the number (`558 collected − 5 = 553`), which a reader could take as a standing property of the suite. It is not: **the suite's CI result is 558/558.**
  - **Two entries opened rather than bundled: G21** (`pre-commit-hooks` two majors behind, no CI counterpart, file-rewriting, trigger = next time that file is opened) and **G22** (checkout v7's fork-PR block is inert only while every workflow triggers on `pull_request`).

- **M5.1 — Correlation strategy design + domain logic** — done
  Module: `correlation` · Depends on: M4 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0023-correlation-match-key.md`** *(read "to be recorded" until 2026-09-15)*
  - **Decide first, and record it before any matching code: how `correlation` names the type it correlates.** Surfaced by the M4→M5 boundary review as its most consequential finding, and escalated into acceptance criteria for the reason G1 became M3.7 — a finding recorded only as prose is one nobody acts on. This issue's own text said `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` is "implemented as pure domain logic, unit tested with constructed Finding fixtures", and **as written that is not buildable**: `pyproject.toml`'s `cross-module-correlation` contract forbids `verion.modules.normalization.domain`, `layers-correlation` makes `domain` the innermost layer so it may not import `correlation/ports/` either, and `mypy --strict` requires every parameter annotated. A pure function in `correlation/domain/` has no legal way to write `findings: list[Finding]`.
    - **The precedent the docs point at does not reach this case**, which is why it needs deciding rather than looking up. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §7 and ADR-0018 decision 2 both say a structure travels by indirect import — fields reachable by attribute without importing the type — and `NormalizeScanUseCase` is the worked example, importing `ScanResultRepositoryPort` and deliberately *not* `ScanResult`, letting the type come from the port's return annotation. **What does not transfer is that inference needs a call site whose type comes back from a port, and a pure `domain/` function has only its own signature.** Every instance of that precedent in `src/` today is application-layer.
      - **Stated precisely, because the loose version of this is false and was in an earlier draft of this bullet.** It is *not* true that `domain/` may not import a port: `normalization/domain/mappers/` imports `ClockPort` and `IdGeneratorPort` from `shared_kernel.ports` and annotates them under `mypy --strict`, and seven `domain/` modules across four modules import `shared_kernel`. What is true is narrower and is what actually binds here — no module's `domain/` imports **another module's** code, `layers-correlation` stops `correlation/domain/` reaching `correlation/ports/`, and `cross-module-correlation` forbids `normalization.domain`, which is where `Finding` lives. That last one is the whole blocker; the rest is context.
    - **Five options, each with a real cost, all to be weighed in the ADR** — including the one that is legal and probably still wrong, so it is rejected on the record rather than omitted as if the linter settled it:
      1. A structural `Protocol` in `correlation/domain/` describing only the fields correlation reads.
      2. The matching logic in `application/` rather than `domain/`, taking the type by inference the way `NormalizeScanUseCase` does.
      3. An anti-corruption type `correlation` owns, with a mapping at its boundary.
      4. Promoting `Finding` to `shared_kernel/` — **rejected in advance** by ADR-0018's criterion, which says exactly this would "pull in `Location`, then `Finding`, and hollow out `normalization/domain/`". Reopening it means overturning that criterion deliberately, not by convenience.
      5. `correlation/domain/` importing `verion.modules.normalization.ports` directly. **This is contract-legal and the ADR must say why it is nonetheless wrong rather than leaving a reader to assume it was forbidden:** `cross-module-correlation` lists only each module's `.domain` and `.adapters`, not `.ports`, and `layers-correlation` constrains only modules inside the `correlation` container — so import-linter permits it. It does not actually solve the problem (importing the port yields no *name* to annotate with, only a return type at a call site), and it would put a persistence interface inside a layer that is supposed to know nothing about persistence. Legal and unhelpful is the worst combination to leave undocumented, because the next person finds it legal and stops there.
    - Note for whichever option wins: `rule_id` and `cwe` reach `correlation` as **fields on the object**, never as `normalization`'s own helpers. `canonical_cwe` is a function in `normalization/domain/`, so it is on the far side of the same contract — correlation compares `finding.cwe` values that mapper already canonicalised, and must not reach for the function.
    - Whichever is chosen binds M6, M7 and M8 too, since every one of them reads another module's entity. Say so in the ADR.
  - Define the concrete matching signals for MVP. ***(Superseded at M5.1 by measurement over G23's corpus. Read this bullet and its sub-bullets as the design **intent**, and ADR-0023's Amendments as what that intent produced on real data: **no equality over any of the twelve candidate signals correlates any pair of the three tools**, and thirty-two of the thirty-six tool-pair/signal cells are empty for reasons no corpus can change — twenty-nine by mapper construction and three by disjoint `rule_id` vocabularies. Of the four that remain, two turn on the pinned ruleset (**G6**) and two were the genuinely open questions. The bullet is kept rather than rewritten because its reasoning is precisely what the measurement tested — the two open questions it hands forward, below, are both now closed in the negative.)*** **`Finding.cwe` and `Finding.location`'s fields are the pair designed for cross-tool comparison** — ADR-0018's Consequences names exactly them: *"M5 gets a canonical CWE it can compare across tools, and a `Location` whose fields it can compare without knowing which tool produced either side."* The *fields*, per the note above, not `normalization`'s `canonical_cwe` function, which sits on the far side of the same contract.
    - ***(Corrected at M5.1. This bullet previously read that `Finding.rule_id` and `Finding.cwe` "are both available as fields and both were designed for this", citing ADR-0019 decision 2 and ADR-0018 decision 4. The `rule_id` half is wrong about what it was designed for: ADR-0019 designed it for grouping **across scans within a tool** — "M5 groups by rule across scans", and "'the same rule fired here and there' was previously only reachable by parsing `title`" — and it is the tool's own namespaced identifier, so it cannot cross tools at all. Rewritten rather than annotated, per the treatment this project gives a claim that turns out wrong rather than incomplete.)***
    - **What that does to G6 is worse than this entry said, and the difference is the point.** With `rule_id` removed from the cross-tool set, **CWE is the only cross-tool signal the roadmap ever named** — so G6, which makes every Semgrep finding carry `cwe = None`, does not *constrain one option out of two*; it **removes one tool of three from the only option there is**. The bullet below still describes it as constraining a choice; read it with this correction.
    - **And ADR-0018's `Location` half is only partly confirmed**, said here rather than swapping one unexamined claim for another. It fails as *equality* **for ZAP against either other tool**: ZAP populates no file or package fields and Semgrep and Trivy populate no URL fields (see G6's M5.1 note), so ZAP and the others share no populated `Location` field and no location signal can reach DAST. **It does not fail for Semgrep↔Trivy**, which both populate `file_path` — that pair is open and needs G23's corpus. ***(Closed at M5.1 against that corpus, empty: `app.py` against `requirements.txt`. Note what did and did not change — the ZAP half of this bullet was structural and is unchanged, while the Semgrep↔Trivy half was corpus-dependent and is now measured. The measurement does not promote it to structural: two tools that both populate a field could overlap on a repository where Trivy scans something other than a manifest.)***
    - **The two examples this bullet used to give sit either side of that line, which is why they are worth keeping.** They were: *"same endpoint path referenced by SAST taint sink and DAST result"* and *"same file/dependency referenced by SCA and SAST"*.
      - The **SAST↔DAST** example cannot be a field read at all: ZAP emits URLs and Semgrep repo-relative paths, on disjoint fields, so expressing it means normalising a URL path against a file path — **a derivation with its own cost and its own false-positive behaviour**. Structural, holds for any corpus.
      - The **SCA↔SAST** example *is* expressible as a field read, since both populate `file_path` — but Trivy's is a manifest path and Semgrep's a source path, so it would likely need derivation too (matching a package name inside a source path). **That "likely" is not measured and cannot be**, per G23. *(An earlier version of this bullet argued it from `requirements.txt` against `vulnerable.py` — two unrelated captures, which is the basis this same commit declares inadmissible three bullets above. Corrected.)*
      - Keep both as goals; read the first as unavailable and the second as unvalidated. ***(Both closed at M5.1 against G23's corpus, in the negative. The **SCA↔SAST** pair's "likely, not measured and cannot be" is now measured and is not likely but empty: Semgrep's `file_path` is `app.py` and Trivy's is `requirements.txt`, and the vocabularies cannot overlap on this repository — Semgrep's `paths.scanned` is `['app.py']`, so it never read the manifest, and Trivy's `Target` is the manifest and never a source file. The **SAST↔DAST** pair remains structurally unavailable as a field read, as this bullet says, and its derivation is deferred by ADR-0023 Decision C on a ground this bullet did not anticipate — not cost, but that the derived group carries no information. See that decision.)*** ***(That last ground is RETRACTED, 2026-08-25, by the active-scan probe — and the retraction is recorded here rather than left because this sentence names Decision C's basis and would otherwise send a reader to the ADR for a reason the ADR no longer gives. "The derived group carries no information" was measured on a passive-only scan plan; under an active one, two alerts fire only on `/calculate` and the derived group is discriminating. **The deferral itself is unchanged** and now rests on **validity** rather than on information content: nothing asserts the crawled URL serves the tree Semgrep read (**G27**, assigned to M5.5). The first half of this bullet — that the pair is unavailable as a *field read* — is untouched and remains structural. Note what this is: a sentence this change falsifies without touching, in a file it edits, which is **G28**'s own class arriving in the commit that extends G28; it is enumerated there too.)***
  - **Measured at M5.1 — what M5 actually delivers is INTRA-tool grouping, and FR-6 is the requirement it satisfies.** With every cross-tool equality measured empty, the grouping that remains is within a single tool's findings, and it is not a consolation prize: it is a real reduction on real output. Measured over the committed fixtures through the real mappers, and re-derived by two further paths that share no code with the first (raw-JSON parse; `grep -o` over the file bytes), all three agreeing:
    - **Trivy: 20 findings → 3 groups by `package`** (`urllib3` 12, `Werkzeug` 6, `Flask` 2). This is the grouping a developer acts on — one version bump resolves twelve findings.
    - **ZAP: 13 findings → 4 groups by `url`** (`/` 5, `/calculate?expr=2*3` 4, `/robots.txt` 2, `/sitemap.xml` 2).
    - **Semgrep: 1 finding**, so it groups trivially and contributes nothing either way on this corpus.
  - **FR-6 restricts by SIGNAL KIND, not by tool count, and that is what admits this.** Verbatim: *"System groups findings that share **asset/location/context signals** (e.g., same endpoint referenced by SAST and DAST) into a single candidate Risk."* The cross-tool case is an `e.g.` — illustrative, not constitutive — while the operative restriction is on what kind of signal is shared. `package` and `url` are location signals and qualify. **`rule_id` does not**: it is a rule identifier, which is none of asset, location or context.
    - **The empirical result agrees with the wording, which is worth stating because it did not have to.** `rule_id` groups ZAP 13 → 5, but is a **no-op on the other two** — Trivy 20 → 20, since `VulnerabilityID` *is* that tool's identity, and Semgrep 1 → 1. And ZAP's 13 → 5 partially **reverses** ADR-0019 decision 4's deliberate per-instance split, which exists so that "fixed on `/` but not on `/robots.txt`" stays expressible. Grouping by `url` does not have that property; grouping by `rule_id` does.
  - **`PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §5 and FR-6 disagree about whether same-tool grouping is Correlation's job at all. Recorded, not reconciled.** §5 defines Correlation as *"The process of linking findings from **different tools/sources** that plausibly describe the same underlying attack surface"* — a restriction by tool, which puts intra-tool grouping outside. FR-6, quoted above, states no tool-diversity requirement at all, which puts it inside provided the shared signal is asset, location or context. Both are in the same document and neither cites the other. **M5 is built on FR-6's reading**, and this milestone's deliverable should be read that way; whether §5's definition is amended or FR-6's example is promoted to a requirement is a `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` decision and is not taken here.
  - **G6 is now `assigned → M5.1`, and it constrains which signals are actually available.** The pinned Semgrep ruleset declares no `metadata:` at all, so **every Semgrep finding in production carries `cwe = None` and `owasp_category = None`**. CWE-based matching therefore sees nothing from SAST — not an error, not a gap anything reports, just one of three tools permanently absent from that signal. Choosing CWE matching without resolving G6 means M5.3's regression suite demonstrates correlation over Trivy↔ZAP only, with nothing saying so. Resolve it or choose signals that do not depend on it; either is fine, silently assuming it works is not. ***(Discharged at M5.1 by the second option. ADR-0023 Decision B puts `cwe` out of the match key, so no signal M5 reads depends on G6. The entry's status is now `open — the M5.1 assignment is discharged, the gap is not`: it still blocks M6, and Decision B is conditional on it staying unresolved, so closing it is a trigger to re-read that decision rather than to reverse it. See G6's own `Discharge (M5.1)` field.)***
  - `CorrelateFindingsUseCase`, unit tested with constructed Finding fixtures covering both "should correlate" and "should NOT correlate" cases. ***(QUALIFIED 2026-08-25, and the treatment is deliberate: this bullet is **not struck**, because it was never wrong about its own subject — only about which issue contained it. M5.1 closed in three commits on `main` — `6408d04`, the PR-#1 merge `ae97de2`, `a41fd6e`, or four counting the merge's two capture commits separately — and **this bullet's work is in none of them**: nothing named `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` was written, and `git grep` finds the type in `.md` files only. Its neighbours in this block carry "Superseded at M5.1", "Corrected at M5.1", "Both closed at M5.1" and "Discharged at M5.1"; **this bullet and the G8 one below carry none** — which is how a hole reads from inside a closed issue: like a bullet somebody forgot to annotate rather than like work nobody did. The work is now **M5.8** and the hole is registered as **G30**.)***
