  - **G8 applies to the negative cases specifically.** Its re-read trigger is "a test whose subject is argument order, operand order, or expression structure", and correlation **symmetry** — does A match B iff B matches A — is exactly that. Write those assertions in `operator`-function form as `test_severity.py` now does, so `ruff --fix`'s SIM300 has no comparison expression to rewrite. Costs nothing; G8 records what it cost when it was not done. ***(QUALIFIED 2026-08-25, with the bullet above and for the same reason: its trigger never arrived, because the tests that fire it were never written. The instruction itself is unchanged and moves to **M5.8** with them, as that issue's criterion (c). See **G30**, and G8's own dated note.)***

- **M5.2 — Correlation persistence + API** — done
  Module: `correlation` · Depends on: M5.8 *(was M5.1 until 2026-08-25: the use case this issue persists the output of is M5.8's, not M5.1's — see **G30**)*
  - `Risk` entity (candidate, unscored yet), Postgres adapter. ***(HALF FALSIFIED 2026-08-26 by `docs/adr/0025-candidate-risk-persistence-and-read-surface.md` decision 1, and annotated rather than struck because the first half shipped. A candidate Risk is a **projection, not a stored entity**: no ORM model, no migration, no port, no repository, and no `risks` table. The entity ships as a type — `MatchGroup`, from M5.8 — and the Postgres adapter does not. Two structural properties decided it, the first being that `group_by_match_key` emits one group per no-signal finding, so two groups in one project can carry equal `MatchKey` values and the key cannot be a conflict target. Nothing before **M8.1** is forced to write a row; see that ADR's decision 2 for why a score does not force one either.)***
  - Endpoint to inspect a Risk's constituent Findings (evidence traceability, `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` **FR-9**; §12's "evidence-first data model" is the architectural principle). *(Cited `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9 until the M4→M5 boundary review — §9 is Transactions / Error handling / Idempotency / SSRF / Testing strategy and says nothing about traceability.)*
  - **This is the first issue to inherit ADR-0022 decision 2, and it inherits the shape rather than re-deciding it.** Consume **`ProjectAccessPort`** — never `ProjectRepositoryPort` or `ProjectMembershipRepositoryPort`, which are *persistence* ports, and consuming one would put "authorization means a membership row exists" (`projects`' domain knowledge) in a second module where a later VIEWER role would silently not reach it. The port returns one `bool`, so there is no vocabulary for which denial applied and **both answer 404**. M6.3, M7.2 and M8.2 copy this too; M5.2 is where it stops being a precedent of one.
  - **G17 grows here.** These routes make the third answering 404 while `projects`' six still answer 403, so a caller recovers a project's existence with one extra request. Not this issue's to fix — the convergence direction is G18's decision at M10.2 — but the divergence should be recorded as widening rather than discovered later.
  - **The endpoint returns constituent Findings, so ADR-0022 decision 1 governs it directly.** The rule-12 hazard is the **bulk shape**: a response carrying every constituent finding's `Evidence.raw_payload` is a source-code export with nothing in its signature saying so, and a Risk correlating N findings makes that strictly worse than the listing decision 1 already refused. Link to `GET /projects/{id}/findings/{fid}/evidence` — an addressable route **is** FR-9's link — rather than inlining payloads.
  - **Decide what this response says about its own completeness, which M4.5 answered for findings and nobody has answered for Risks.** `GET /projects/{id}/findings` returns `latest_run` plus `unfinished_runs` precisely because a list shortened by a failed normalization is indistinguishable from a clean project (G15). A Risk listing inherits that identically and adds a second layer, since a Risk is only as complete as the findings it correlated. Either carry the same envelope or state why not.
  - **G11 grows here too:** a `Risk`→`Finding` link is cross-module, so it carries no foreign key by ADR-0017 decision 1 — a fourth table depending on rows never being deleted. ***(FALSIFIED 2026-08-26 by ADR-0025 decision 1: there is no link table, because there is no table. The forecast moves to **M8.1**, the issue that first has a value it cannot recompute. G11's own post-M4 note is annotated in place — it is `open`, so the frozen-field distinction G34 draws does not apply to it.)***
  - **Delivered 2026-08-26.** `ListProjectRisksUseCase`, `GET /projects/{id}/risks`, correlation's first router, first response schemas and first two DI factories. The full-project read this route recomputes was measured before any of it was written, at ADR-0022's volume and over a project each run's own guard counts confirm holds 2,000 findings and 2,000 evidence rows. **The query is bimodal over eight runs** — three on a parallel index-scan plan at 11-12 ms, five hashing all 100,000 evidence rows at 26-30 ms — and is reported as **two plans rather than a range or an average**, since at three of eight the faster one is behaviour rather than noise. **Cache state is not what selects the plan**; ADR-0025's Consequences carries both plan shapes, records what did vary as an observation rather than a mechanism, and names the trigger for the volume nobody has measured.

- **M5.3 — Correlation accuracy test suite** — done
  Module: `correlation` · Depends on: M5.8 — **and, for its `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10 claim only, M5.4 + M5.5 + M5.6** *(added 2026-08-25; the intra-tool suite still depends on M5.1 alone and can be built before any of the three, which is why they are a qualified rather than an unqualified dependency)*
  - **M5.1 → M5.8, 2026-08-25, and the parenthetical above is qualified rather than rewritten:** its argument survives whole — only the issue it names moves. The "pure domain logic with constructed fixtures before any persistence exists" this suite runs against **is** the matcher, which M5.1 did not build (**G30**), so read its "M5.1 alone" as "M5.8 alone" and its §10 clause exactly as written. The same "M5.1, not M5.2" phrasing recurs in this issue's third bullet below, in **G19**'s `Assignment`, and in **G23**'s `Blocks-if-unresolved:`; in all three the argument — no persistence exists yet, so the fixtures are the only input — is unaffected, and the number is deliberately not corrected there.
  - A curated fixture set (deliberately vulnerable sample app findings) with expected correlation groupings, used as a regression suite — this becomes a demonstrable "proof" artifact for the CV/portfolio narrative.
  - **G19 is `assigned → M5.3` and is acceptance criteria here, not a re-read.** Its own text names this issue as the next occurrence of "synthetic data whose *shape* differs from production makes a benchmark read as evidence while measuring the generator". The planner reads shape — cardinality, ordering, distribution, width — and so does a matcher: a fixture set whose findings differ from real ones in a way that looks cosmetic can make correlation appear to work on data the system will never see.
  - **What sharpens it here is this issue's `Depends on:`.** M5.3 depends on **M5.1, not M5.2**, so the suite runs against pure domain logic with constructed fixtures before any persistence exists. The fixtures are not one input among several — they are the **only** input, and there is no second source that could contradict a wrong shape. G19's blast radius at M4.5 was a slow query blessed by a benchmark; here it is a proof artifact blessing a matcher, in an artifact this roadmap explicitly intends to be shown to people.
  - Concretely: real ids are UUIDs (rule 9) and do not cluster; `rule_id` is the tool's own identifier, not a synthetic label; Semgrep findings carry `cwe = None` in production until G6 is resolved, so a fixture set giving them CWEs proves a signal that does not exist. State which properties of real findings the fixtures reproduce and which they do not.
  - **Scope corrected at M5.1: this suite demonstrates INTRA-tool grouping, and cannot honestly demonstrate cross-tool correlation.** Every cross-tool equality over the twelve candidate signals measures empty on G23's corpus, twenty-nine of the thirty-six cells by mapper construction and three more by disjoint `rule_id` vocabularies (ADR-0023's Amendments). What the suite can regress is what M5 delivers: Trivy grouped by `package` and ZAP by `url`, with the negative cases G19 already requires. **A fixture set that shows cross-tool groups would be showing something the system does not do** — which is G19's failure mode arriving through the matcher rather than through the data, in the one artifact this roadmap intends to put in front of people.
    - ***(Amended 2026-08-25 by the active-scan probe. The scope correction above is **narrowed to what it actually measured, not withdrawn**: every cross-tool **field equality** over the twelve nominated signals is still empty **on the committed passive-only corpus**, and a fixture set showing cross-tool groups *on that basis* would still be showing something the system does not do. **The one exception makes the point harder, not softer:** under an active plan `cwe` on Trivy↔ZAP is non-empty, and its single pair is a **false match** — so a fixture set that reproduced it would demonstrate correlation succeeding on a finding that does not exist, which is the worst version of what this bullet forbids rather than an escape from it. What is no longer true is the unqualified reading — that the suite cannot honestly demonstrate cross-tool correlation **at all**. It can, by **derived location**, once **M5.4**, **M5.5** and **M5.6** land, because ZAP's active scanner does report on the `eval()` sink (G24's `Note (2026-08-25, active-scan probe)`). Two things this does **not** license, stated because the gap between them is where a proof artifact goes wrong: the suite may not claim a derived-location group before M5.5 exists, since an unfounded group looks exactly like a founded one; and it may not present derived location as field equality, since they are different claims with different failure modes. **G19 applies harder here, not less** — a fixture set can now be built that makes cross-tool correlation *succeed*, which is precisely the outcome its author wants and precisely what G19 warns is not evidence.)***
  - **The SAST↔DAST route specifically becomes available only when G24 AND G27 are both resolved. Neither alone opens the path, and the distinction is not decorative:** *(Scoped to that route deliberately. It is **not** true that cross-tool in general waits on these two: resolving **G6** would reopen `cwe` on Semgrep↔Trivy, and that pair's `file_path` result is corpus-dependent rather than structural, so a repository where Trivy scans something other than a manifest could make it non-empty with neither gap touched.)* G24 is a condition on **existence** — passive-only scanning means no DAST finding about the sink exists to correlate — and G27 is a condition on **validity** — nothing asserts the crawled URL serves the tree Semgrep read. Resolving G24 alone produces a real pair whose correlation is unfounded; resolving G27 alone produces a valid path with nothing to put through it. See ADR-0023 Decision C, which carries the table.
    - ***(Updated 2026-08-25. The pairing is unchanged and remains the point — what changes is that both halves now have issues, so this bullet names them rather than leaving a reader to find two open register entries. **G24 → M5.4**, **G27 → M5.5**, and the derivation itself is **M5.6**, which this bullet did not previously account for as separate work: G24 and G27 make the pair *exist* and make comparing it *well-founded*, and neither of them writes the route-path-to-source-line mapping. Three issues, not two. The asymmetry the bullet describes is also sharper than it was: G24's condition is now discharged by a decision about scan policy rather than by an open question about whether the finding is obtainable, since the probe measured that it is.)***
      - ***(Annotated 2026-09-09, not rewritten: **`G27 → M5.5` is now `G27 → M5.5 + M5.6`**. ADR-0028 split that entry — the declaration half is M5.5's and the conditioning half M5.6's — so M5.6 is no longer only "the derivation itself"; it also carries the gate that makes the derivation conditional. "Three issues, not two" survives and is if anything reinforced.)***
  - **FLAG, not decided — `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10's demonstration goal is NOT satisfied by M5 as currently scoped.** Verbatim: *"At least one clear, demonstrable case where correlation **across 2+ tools** produces a materially better prioritization than looking at raw tool output."* Intra-tool grouping cannot discharge that sentence whatever its quality, since the sentence names tool count explicitly — and note that §5 restricts by tool too, so §5-versus-FR-6's disagreement (M5.1) offers no way around this one either. **But note §10 says "2+ tools", not "SAST and DAST"** — so the SAST↔DAST route is the most compelling way to satisfy it and not the only one, and the bullet above is the reason: a Semgrep↔Trivy pair is two tools. **Five ways out, none taken here**, roughly in ascending order of cost: resolve **G6**, which reopens `cwe` on Semgrep↔Trivy; demonstrate on a repository where Trivy's `Target` is not a manifest, since that pair's `file_path` result is corpus-dependent rather than structural; pull **G24 + G27** into M5 for the SAST↔DAST case; move the demonstration goal to a later milestone; or amend §10. **This is recorded for a decision, not decided.**
    - ***(DECIDED 2026-08-25. The third of the five is taken: **G24 + G27 are pulled into M5** as M5.4 and M5.5, with the derivation as M5.6 — so "none taken here" is no longer true of this bullet and the flag it raises is answered rather than still open. What made the choice is that the probe removed the cost that ranked it third: it was priced as expensive because enabling an active scan was an open question about whether a DAST finding was even obtainable, and that is now measured. It is still the most expensive of the five in *work*, and it is chosen anyway because it is the only one that produces the demonstration §10 actually describes — the other four either produce a weaker pair (**G6**'s Semgrep↔Trivy on `cwe`), depend on a repository nobody has (`file_path` on a non-manifest `Target`), or move the goalposts. **The FLAG's central claim survives untouched and must not be read as discharged: M5 as *currently built* still does not satisfy §10.** M5.1–M5.3 deliver intra-tool grouping, which cannot discharge a sentence naming tool count whatever its quality. What has changed is that the milestone now contains the issues that would, and that they are numbered rather than deferred. Read the §10 report in this milestone's own terms: satisfied **when M5.4, M5.5 and M5.6 land**, and conditioned on M5.5 in particular, since without it the correlation can be confidently wrong.)***
      - ***(Condition met 2026-09-15: M5.4, M5.5 and M5.6 have landed, and a declared Flask project gets the `/calculate` `{semgrep, zap}` group in production (`test_derived_group_end_to_end.py`). §10 asks for a materially better **prioritization**, and nothing is prioritized before M6, so whether §10 is met is carried to M6.1 rather than answered here.)***
  - **The measured fallback is thinner than G24's entry implies, and M5.3 should not plan around it.** That entry offers correlation by *derived location* as the best this issue could demonstrate. Measured on G23's corpus: no ZAP alert fires only on `/calculate`, and its four alerts are a **strict subset** of the five that fire on `/`. So a derived group would pair `dangerous-eval` with four alerts the site root also carries — zero discriminating power. See G24's `Note (M5.1)` for the per-URL sets.
    - ***(FALSIFIED 2026-08-25 by the active-scan probe, and the sentence above is left standing rather than rewritten because **it was never wrong about its own subject** — it measured the passive corpus, said so, and is accurate about it. What it was wrong about is the word this bullet leads with: the fallback is thinner **on that corpus**, not thinner as such, and the bullet's instruction "M5.3 should not plan around it" was drawn from a scan configuration rather than from a property of the target. Under an active scan the per-path sets are `/calculate` → `{6-5, 90036, 10038-1, 10020-1, 10036-2, 10021}` and `/` → `{10038-1, 10106, 10020-1, 10036-2, 10021, 10027}`. **Two alerts fire only on `/calculate` — `6-5` and `90036` — and the subset relation is false in both directions**, since `/` carries `10106` and `10027` that the endpoint does not. So the derived group is no longer `dangerous-eval` plus four alerts the root also carries; it is `dangerous-eval` plus a confidence-3 finding that the endpoint executes shell commands, which the root does not have and cannot. **Discriminating power exists.** The measurement that produced the sentence above is exactly the measurement this bullet asked for and got a different answer to once the scan plan changed — which is the argument for M5.4 rather than a defect in the earlier reading.)***

**M5.4–M5.7 were added 2026-08-25, after the active-scan probe. Two things about the numbering, stated here because a reader hitting M5.4 below will otherwise assume both.**

- **They are appended, and nothing is renumbered. Both figures below are measured at the commit BEFORE this one**, and are stated that way because this commit moves them and a present-tense count in the commit that invalidates it is the defect one level down — the same hazard G28's note handles with a two-column table. **At `HEAD` as this was decided:** `M5.0`–`M5.3` were referenced **232 times across 18 files** — 136 on M5.1, 48 on M5.0, 28 on M5.3, 20 on M5.2 — spanning `docs/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.claude/agents/`, `src/` and `tests/`; and **nothing in the repository referenced `M5.4` or above** (`git grep -o -F M5.4 HEAD` and its siblings returned zero). Both change the moment this commits — the issues defined immediately below are themselves M5.4–M5.7 references — which is the point rather than an oversight: **the decision was taken against the tree it had to be compatible with.** So appending leaves every pre-existing reference valid, while renumbering would have broken **28 M5.3 citations** to put the list in dependency order. That trade is not worth taking for ordering, and the register's own convention — an entry's `Status: assigned → <issue>` is a permanent citation — is what makes issue numbers expensive to move.
  - ***(Counted with `git grep -o -F` against `HEAD`, which counts occurrences rather than matching lines and reads every tracked file — a hand-written path list omits `.pre-commit-config.yaml` and `.claude/`. **The trap in a per-file breakdown is reading one file's rows as the repo total**: summing the four `docs/ROADMAP.md` rows gives 172, and pairing that with the number of `docs/` files carrying the tokens gives "172 across nine files" — one scope for the total, another for the file count, in a sentence that then claims to span `src/` and `tests/`.)***
- **So number order is NOT execution order in this milestone**, which is the cost of the choice and is written down rather than left to be discovered. `Depends on:` carries the real order. Specifically: **M5.3's `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10 claim depends on M5.4, M5.5 and M5.6**, all of which are numbered after it. There is precedent for the two diverging and for saying so — G5's `Note (M4.1)` records M4.1's renumbering putting M4.2 ahead of M4.3, and explains why that was safe rather than leaving it to look like an oversight.

- **M5.4 — Active scanning behind explicit per-project consent** — done
  Module: `scanning` + `projects` · Depends on: — · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0024-active-scanning-consent-and-scan-policy.md`**
  - Add an `activeScan` job to `_build_plan_yaml`, a module-level function in `scanning/adapters/outbound/scanners/zap_adapter.py`, gated on an explicit per-project **consent** declaration: *the project owner declares that this target may be attacked.*
  - **This issue makes the SAST↔DAST pair obtainable; it does not obtain one.** Standing the target up, the liveness assertion, the active capture and the measured CI step are **M5.9**, which resolves G24 and G29.
  - **Consent is necessary, NOT sufficient, and must never become a path around ADR-013. This is acceptance criteria, not commentary** — it is easy to get wrong at implementation time and nearly invisible in review, because the wrong version passes every test the right version passes. Both of ADR-013's gates still run before any subprocess call, in that order, against a consented target exactly as against any other; `allow_private_targets` stays a test-only escape hatch and must never be `True` in production wiring. The two govern different things and neither substitutes for the other: **ADR-013 decides *which* targets may be reached; consent decides *what may be done* to a target it has already admitted.** A consented target that resolves to a private address is still refused. Pin this with a test whose subject is the ordering, so "simplifying" it later is a visible act — ADR-016 decision 3 already ships that pattern for the configuration write path, and that ADR's `## Alternatives considered` names the reason: anything that makes the scan-time gate look optional is a liability rather than defence in depth.
  - **Separate from M5.5's declaration, and they must not be folded into one flag, one column, or one consent screen.** Two different claims, made by two different people, about two different things: *"you may attack this host"* is a statement about authorization, made by whoever owns the target; *"this URL serves this repository"* is a statement about topology, made by whoever knows the deployment. Either can be true while the other is false, and the failure modes are unrelated — an unconsented scan is an unauthorized act, while an undeclared coupling is a wrong answer. One field would make each imply the other.
  - **ADR-0024 records the scan policy** — where consent lives and who may grant it (note ADR-016 decision 3's precedent — enabling a scanner is owner-gated because it "can point an attack tool at a URL", and this is that argument's stronger form); duration and scope bounds for the `activeScan` job; what an active scan is permitted to do; and the ADR-013 relationship above. Per `CLAUDE.md`'s working-style rule, anything ADR-0024 defers with a trigger condition seeds a register entry **in the same commit**.
  - **What consent is consenting to, from the probe — this belongs in the ADR as measurement rather than as a warning.** One 62s run against one small Flask app issued **391 well-formed requests across 86 distinct paths**, plus 7 malformed TLS probes at the HTTP port. Those paths include cloud-metadata SSRF probes for **six** cloud providers — `/latest/meta-data/` (AWS), `/computeMetadata/v1/` (GCP), `/metadata/instance` (Azure), `/opc/v1/instance/` and `/opc/v2/instance/` (Oracle), `/metadata/v1` (DigitalOcean), `/openstack/latest/meta_data.json` (OpenStack) — secret-file probes (`/.ssh/id_rsa`, `/.ssh/id_dsa`, `/.env`, `/.git/config`, `/key.pem`, `/privatekey.key`, `/server.key`, `/sftp-config.json`, `/WS_FTP.INI`), and **real shell command execution on the target host** — the finding below was established by the scanner running `sleep 15` twice. Nothing returned 5xx and nothing was persisted, but that is a property of this target rather than a guarantee.
  - **Re-read G31 before shipping the `activeScan` job.** M5.8's match key groups ZAP on the full `Location.url`, which is correct only while the passive plan yields one `?expr` per route; this issue is what makes `url` split `/calculate` into two groups and separate `6-5` from `90036`. Cited here rather than left in the register alone, because a trigger reachable only by someone re-reading the register is the inert shape G28 measures.
  - **Re-read G39 before shipping the `activeScan` job.** Its re-entry trigger is any change to a pinned scanner ruleset or scan plan, and this issue changes the plan. Cited here for the reason the G31 bullet gives.

- **M5.5 — Per-project declaration that the scanned URL serves the scanned tree** — done
  Module: `projects` · Depends on: — · **Resolves the declaration half of G27; the conditioning half is M5.6's** · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0028-declaring-that-the-scanned-url-serves-the-scanned-tree.md`**
  - Build the direction G27 names and deliberately did not design: **an explicit per-project declaration that this URL serves this repository**, signed by a person. ***(The clause that followed — "with correlation between tree-derived and URL-derived findings **conditioned on it** rather than assuming it" — is struck with the bullet below and for the same reason; it is the same claim in a subordinate clause.)***
  - ~~**The conditioning is the deliverable, not the field.**~~ ***(FALSIFIED and STRUCK 2026-09-09 by ADR-0028 decision 0, and struck rather than superseded-and-dated because the two treatments are not interchangeable here. Superseded would say the sentence was true when written and has since been overtaken. It was not: it was **unsatisfiable on the day it was written**, and the structural reason is older than the sentence's consequences rather than newer. `SIGNAL_FIELDS` is `("package", "url")`; `mappers/trivy.py` populates `package` and never `url`, `mappers/zap.py` the reverse, and `mappers/semgrep.py` neither — so no pair of tools can co-group by any route, measured at zero cross-tool groups over both committed corpora. A conditioning gate written in this issue would be green whether or not it is wired in. **The dating, which explains it without blaming it:** G27's assignment note, which set this criterion, is dated 2026-08-25; ADR-0023's amendment freezing the key's three fields is 2026-08-26 and M5.8 implemented the matcher the same day. The criterion was written the day before the code it constrains.)***
    - **The principle in the struck sentence survives and is not struck — it is relocated.** *"A declaration nothing reads is a column"* is correct, is what ADR-0028 decision 5 is built on, and is now **M5.6's acceptance criterion**, written at that issue's own block rather than only here or in the register. G27's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` remains explicit that the dangerous case is the silent one — a group produced from two different systems looks exactly like a group produced from one, and no version string betrays it.
  - **What this issue ships instead:** the declaration entity and its table, the in-force rule as a pure function in `projects/domain/`, and `projects`' own read surface. Per ADR-0028 decision 4 the cross-module verdict port is **decided here and ships at M5.6 with its consumer** — **G48**.
  - **G27's rejected alternatives stay rejected and are cited rather than re-litigated.** Verifying the coupling automatically means trusting the `Server` banner that ADR-0023 Decision A refused on two independent grounds — refused **as a `Location` correlation signal**, which is the qualification this clause lacked and which ADR-0028 decision 2 establishes, since Decision A's own closing paragraph leaves a Security Context deployment-signal read open; that use is unbuilt rather than forbidden; recording a commit on `Scan` (**G25**) makes the coupling *checkable* without making it *true*. Neither is this issue's fix.
  - **Now on the critical path for `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10**, not for correlation alone — see G27's own note. Before the probe this entry's condition was moot because G24 blocked the pair at existence; it is now the only thing between a real pair and a §10 demonstration that could be wrong.

- **M5.6 — Route extractor: URL path → source location** — done
  Module: `projects` *(was **OPEN — decide in this issue**; decided 2026-09-09 by **ADR-0029** decision 1)* · Depends on: M5.8, M5.5 · **Resolves the conditioning half of G27** · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0029-route-extractor-placement-re-derivation-and-key-entry.md`** *(this line read `Depends on: M5.1` and the M5.1 half is corrected here on M5.3's own precedent — the matcher this issue's gate acts on shipped at **M5.8**, since G30 records that M5.1 closed without building it)*
  - **Ships in three commits, and this is one issue rather than three.** Commit 1 is ADR-0029 and the documentation it forces; commit 2 the extractor; commit 3 the key entry, `ServingDeclarationPort`, its `di.py` factory and the gate. **G48's trigger names "M5.6 being descoped, deferred or split" and none of those has happened** — a three-commit issue is not a split, and that entry is discharged by commit 3 rather than by this one.
    - ***(QUALIFIED 2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3: **four commits, not three**, and still one issue. Commit 3 is the port, the gate, the key entry and G31's re-key. ADR-0029's 2026-09-15 amendment decides persistence and puts population at Security Context build time in **commit 4**, together with the archive fetch it prices. The other surviving pieces — commit 3 discharging G48, and a multi-commit issue not being a split — are unchanged. The description of commit 3 is correct and incomplete.)***
  - **ACCEPTANCE CRITERION, moved here from M5.5 on 2026-09-09 by ADR-0028 decision 0: a derived SAST↔DAST group is NOT produced when M5.5's declaration is absent or out of force.** It arrives here because this is the first issue in which it can fail — M5.5 cannot satisfy it, since no pair of tools can co-group before this issue's derivation exists, so a gate written there would be green whether or not it were wired in. *"A declaration nothing reads is a column"* is M5.5's sentence and this is where it becomes checkable. **Written at this bullet rather than only in the register or in the ADR, which is G28's lesson**: a requirement reachable only by somebody re-reading a register is a requirement that gets skipped.
    - **It carries three things this issue's other bullets do not name.** `ServingDeclarationPort` from `projects/ports/`, whose shape ADR-0028 decision 4 fixes — one method, a `bool`, read from `correlation/application/` and never from `correlation/domain/`, where `group_by_match_key` and `matches` live; its `di.py` factory under rule 15; and the gate itself. If this issue has to be split for size, split the port and the gate away from the extractor — **do not defer the criterion a second time**.
    - **What the gate may and may not claim, from ADR-0028 decision 2.** A present declaration voids on **reconfiguration** and never on **drift**: it does not assert that the deployment runs any particular revision, and nothing in the system observes that. So a green gate means *a person declared this coupling and nobody has edited either side since* — **G47** — and never *these two findings are about one system*. Acceptance is that the group is withheld when the declaration is absent, not that a produced group is true.
    - ***(2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3 — two statements, both required, because only one of them is true today.)***
      - ***Commit 3's criterion is met IN CODE.*** `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` reads `ServingDeclarationPort` from `correlation/application/`, and does not attempt the derivation unless the declaration is in force. `test_correlate_findings.py` pins the ordering with a route map that raises when read, and pins the withheld/produced pair. All eight physical mutations below were killed. Each was applied alone, run over `test_correlate_findings.py`, `test_correlation_accuracy.py`, `test_route_extraction.py`, `test_match_key.py`, `test_list_project_risks.py` and `test_risks_routes.py`, then reverted byte for byte:
        1. **Gate removed** (`if True:`). 8 failed, including `test_the_route_map_is_not_read_while_no_declaration_is_in_force`, `test_a_derived_group_is_withheld_when_no_declaration_is_in_force`, and the undeclared corpus test.
        2. **Gate inverted.** 11 failed, including the produced test and the declared corpus test.
        3. **Gate moved after `route_map_for`.** 8 failed, including the ordering test and the withheld test.
        4. **ZAP keyed on the full url again.** 13 failed, including both parametrizations of `test_zap_is_keyed_on_path_whether_or_not_a_declaration_is_in_force` and the route test in `test_risks_routes.py`.
        5. **`paths_serving` ignoring the span**, so a path is derived for a line no route serves. 5 failed, including `test_a_line_outside_every_route_span_derives_nothing`.
        6. **A converter route matching a concrete crawled path.** Only `test_a_converter_route_never_matches_a_concrete_crawled_path` failed.
        7. **`paths[0]` taken when two or more routes serve the line.** Only `test_a_line_served_by_two_stacked_routes_derives_nothing` failed.
        8. **The ZAP re-key gated on the declaration.** 5 failed, including the `[undeclared]` parametrization and the undeclared corpus test.

        Surviving probe, and its fix: forcing the verdict reader to return False left the declared-project route test in `test_risks_routes.py` green. That test now asserts the route map was consulted.
      - ***The issue's criterion is met at COMMIT 4.*** Until then production's `RouteMapPort` returns an empty map, so production produces no derived group at all, whatever a project has declared. **G27** stays `assigned → M5.6 commit 4` for that reason.
    - ***(2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4 — the criterion is met in PRODUCTION, and the "until then" above is dated by it.)***
      - ***This is the fourth and last commit.*** No completion marker is added. M5.5's commit recorded why: `Delivered` marks two issues in this file, and marking one more of M5's shipped issues would imply the others did not ship. *(Superseded 2026-09-15 by the completion-marker convention in "How to use this document".)*
      - ***Production derives.*** `BuildSecurityContextFromGitHubUseCase` fetches the default branch as one archive (`VcsProviderPort.fetch_source_archive`), runs `extract_routes`, and stores the map in `route_maps`. `get_route_map_port` wires `PostgresRouteMapReader`, and `EmptyRouteMapReader` is deleted.
      - ***The acceptance is end to end, through real persistence and the real correlation path.*** In `tests/integration/test_derived_group_end_to_end.py`:
        - `test_a_built_context_produces_the_cross_tool_group_while_the_declaration_is_in_force` — detect through the real router, the map stored, `GET /risks` with no route-map override, one group at `/calculate` holding the Semgrep and ZAP findings.
        - `test_the_same_built_map_produces_no_group_once_the_declaration_is_out_of_force` — the same built map, with the declaration voided by repointing the target: no group.
        - A third test stores a failed download as `FETCH_FAILED` and derives nothing.

        ***The module states what it does NOT prove:*** it serves the committed capture through `MockTransport`, so the archive format belongs to the adapter's real-capture tests alone.
      - ***Not what commit 3's G27 note predicted.*** That note said this commit would flip `test_risks_routes.py::test_a_declared_project_gets_no_derived_group_until_the_route_map_is_populated`. The flip is the first end-to-end test above. The old test is renamed `..._with_no_built_route_map_gets_no_derived_group` and kept for the case that is still true: a declared project with no stored map, which now reads `NOT_BUILT`.
      - ***What production delivers is narrower than "a map per project", because of G55.*** The map is written at a project's first detect. It cannot be refreshed without breaking that project's context reads, and a failed fetch is effectively permanent. A project detected before this commit has no map and cannot get one without that breakage.
      - ***Parser mutations*** — `GitHubAdapter.fetch_source_archive`, the only code in this commit that parses network input. Each was applied alone, run over `tests/integration/test_github_adapter.py`, and reverted byte for byte against its sha256.
        1. Decompressed cap removed: killed by the bomb test.
        2. Compressed cap removed: killed by the over-cap test.
        3. `isreg()` dropped: killed by the symlink/hardlink test.
        4. Prefix strip dropped: 4 failed, including the real-capture keys test.
        5. Non-`.py` member read: killed only by `test_the_real_capture_yields_only_repo_relative_python_source`. This was predicted in advance, since `extract_routes` ignores non-`.py` keys and nothing downstream can catch it.
        6. `Authorization` forwarded to codeload: killed.
        7. Rule 12, three variants — `from exc`, `from None` (which keeps `__context__`), and the link placed in the message: all killed.
        8. SHA taken from the directory prefix: killed by the full-SHA test.
        9. Extras, all killed: the content-encoding guard, the codeload host pin, the overall deadline, the non-canonical-path check, the directory/SHA cross-check, and the duplicate-member check.
        10. **Survived on the first pass: the gzip end-of-stream / trailing-data check.** The only truncation test cut 64 bytes, which `tarfile` rejects on its own. A cut 8-byte trailer and appended bytes both still parse cleanly, as 8 members, without the check. **Fix:** the test is parametrized over all three shapes, and the mutation now fails two of them.
        11. ***Added in the guardian's correction round, after two real parser defects.*** A sparse member is `isreg()` and expands on read past the decompressed cap (a 283-byte gzip returned 209,715,200 bytes). `tarfile` and `urlsplit` raise bare `ValueError`s the port contract did not cover. Three more mutations, all killed:
            - sparse refusal removed — both sparse parametrizations;
            - `tarfile`'s `ValueError` not mapped — the malformed-sparse-header test;
            - `_is_codeload`'s catch removed — the malformed-IPv6 `Location` case.
        12. ***All twenty were re-run after the correction, on an adapter module confirmed green first (43 passed).*** That order is stated because the first post-correction sweep ran on a red baseline. One new test was itself wrong: httpx2 rejects a non-numeric-port `Location` before the adapter sees it, so that case is a fetch failure, not malformed. With the baseline red, every row read KILLED. That run is discarded, not reported, and the case now has its own test asserting what actually happens.
      - ***Pipeline mutations***, run over `tests/unit` and four integration modules — `test_derived_group_end_to_end.py`, `test_postgres_route_map_repository.py`, `test_risks_routes.py` and `test_security_context_routes.py` — all killed:
        - the stored map never read (end-to-end positive test, reader test);
        - a fetch failure swallowed as "no routes" (unit failure test, end-to-end failure test);
        - the declaration gate bypassed (**end-to-end negative test**, plus the unit gate tests);
        - the CHECK loosened to admit `not_built` (migration edited, schema rebuilt; `test_the_database_refuses_a_stored_not_built`);
        - both `RouteMapRecord` invariants dropped;
        - the non-Flask branch fetching anyway;
        - the upsert leaving the SHA out of `set_`;
        - the upsert leaving `framework` out of `set_` — added in the guardian's correction round, because the re-upsert test never changed `framework` and this mutation would have survived it;
        - `RouteMap`'s unread/residue invariant dropped;
        - undecodable members not merged into `unparsed_files`.
  - Build the derivation ADR-0023 Decision C describes: map a crawled URL path to the source location that serves it — for Flask, the `@app.route`-decorated function whose line span contains Semgrep's finding. **Built in M5 rather than reopening M2.**
    - **What a green extractor test may claim is bounded by the trees that exist, and the bound is narrow.** ADR-0029 decision 5, on ADR-0027 decision 5's pattern and written before the code rather than after it. **There is no committed FLASK tree in this repository** — and the qualifier is load-bearing, since `tests/integration/fixtures/semgrep_target/vulnerable.py` **is** a committed Python source file, just a route-free one. What is absent is a tree with routes: the two `tests/integration/fixtures/` GitHub fixtures list `src/main.py` and `pages/index.js` and carry neither in their `contents`, because `relevant_file_paths` filters source out before `get_file_content` is called. So the only real Flask tree is the two-route demo target, reached by live clone. That leaves untested by construction: blueprints, `add_url_rule`, variable path converters, class-based views, routes across modules, any non-literal path, **and the miss case from a real tree**, since the demo target has one source finding and it hits. It also cannot discriminate decorator-inclusive from decorator-exclusive spans. **Fixtures are therefore an acceptance criterion of commit 2, not a nicety** — and `vulnerable.py` already supplies the miss-case shape, so this is half a fixture's work rather than a new tree's.
      - ***(SATISFIED and PARTLY DATED 2026-09-10 by commit 2, annotated here because this bullet is decision 5 restated in a live doc and a reader who fixes only the ADR fixes half — the sibling-restatement class **G46** exists for. The criterion is met: `tests/unit/test_route_extraction.py` ships fixtures for the miss case over the real `vulnerable.py`, for a non-literal path, for a converter, for stacked decorators, for `methods` in both its senses, and for a `@bp.route` inside an app factory. **So the enumeration above is now dated rather than wrong**: of the shapes it lists as untested by construction, `add_url_rule`, class-based views and routes across modules still are; converters, non-literal paths and the miss case are fixtured; blueprints are fixtured on their decorator half only, with the `url_prefix` half registered as **G54**. **The real-tree half of this bullet is untouched and is the part that still holds** — the only Flask tree this project owns is still a two-route app, which is why every one of those fixtures had to be constructed. ADR-0029's `## Amendments` carries the full table.)***
      - ***(QUALIFIED 2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4, and narrowly. **"There is no committed FLASK tree in this repository" is now false**: `tests/integration/fixtures/github_tarball/verion-demo-target-c68caa7.tar.gz` is GitHub's real archive of the demo target at `c68caa7`, committed as the FORMAT contract for `GitHubAdapter.fetch_source_archive`. **Nothing else in this bullet moves.** It is the same two-route app the bullet already calls "the only real Flask tree", so it exercises none of the shapes listed as untested by construction: blueprints, `add_url_rule`, converters, class-based views, routes across modules, non-literal paths and the real-tree miss case are each exactly as tested, or as untested, as the annotation above leaves them. A reader must not take "a Flask tree is committed now" as coverage it does not give. The fixture's README states the same bound at the file.)***
  - **Where it lives is this issue's decision and needs an ADR either way.** Two placements, both real, and `shared_kernel` promotion is **not** a third — ADR-0023 alternative 4 rejected it on ADR-0018's criterion (closed vocabularies, not structures), and alternative 6 records that it returns at M6 as an amendment to that criterion rather than as a local convenience. Do not re-litigate it here.
    - ***(ANSWERED 2026-09-09 by ADR-0029 decision 1: **`projects/`**. The two bullets below are annotated rather than struck, because both remain accurate descriptions of the two options; what changed is which of their grounds decided. **The ground is the boundary `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 and `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §5 set, stated as such and not dressed as a cost comparison** — a route table is the API surface of a tree, §5's Security Context definition names `APIs`, and §3 assigns Security Context to Projects. Two supporting grounds, neither a fetch cost: `correlation` would have to acquire a port to a source tree, and placement (a) would erode ADR-0023 section (b)'s single-conformance-site guarantee by feeding it a second, unannotated provenance.)***
    - **(a) In `correlation/`** — fast in surface terms: no migration, no new module, no ORM column, no response schema. It **violates `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3**, whose module table assigns *"Projects, connected repositories, Security Context"* to **Projects** — and Security Context is the thing that carries framework knowledge, per `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §5's definition of it: *"framework, language, DB, **APIs**, auth mechanism, deployment target, dependencies"*. So it needs an ADR arguing a deliberate deviation. *(Two documents: §3 carries only the one-line module responsibility, and the field list is §5's.)* **But the deciding cost is not the boundary — it is that there is no tree to read.** `correlation` has no port to any source tree; `normalization`'s `FindingRepositoryPort` returns `Finding`s, not source; and `scanning`'s checkout is a `tempfile.mkdtemp` that does not survive the scan (G9's evidence). So the extractor would have to **re-fetch the source at correlation time**, and **G25** means nothing records which commit to fetch — it would parse a tree that is not the one Semgrep read, which is **G27's failure mode reintroduced inside the fix for it**. Secondary but real: a route map is not a `Finding` field, so feeding it into the match key adds a field from a second, unchecked provenance, and ADR-0023 (b)'s conformance guarantee — the construction site as "the single place `mypy` compares correlation's description of `Finding` against the real one" — silently stops being total, which is the narrowing hazard that section already names.
    - **(b) In `projects/`** — satisfies §3, and the extractor's shape is already there: `detect_stack` is a pure function keyed on framework, and `flask`/`django`/`fastapi` are already in `_PYTHON_FRAMEWORK_SIGNATURES`. **The real cost is the fetch.** `build_security_context_from_github.py` fetches only `relevant_file_paths(all_paths)` — a frozenset of 8 manifest filenames plus `.github/workflows/` — and route extraction needs **source files**, so `VcsProviderPort` starts pulling every `.py` in the repository through the GitHub API. That is a volume and rate-limit question, not a boundary one, and it is the thing to measure before choosing. Plus the ordinary widening: a route-map field on `SecurityContext` or a new entity, an ORM column and an Alembic migration (rule 8), a response-schema field (rule 10), and `correlation` reading it through a published port of `projects` (rule 3) — `security_context_repository.py` already exists, so this may be a read on an existing port rather than a new one.
      - ***(The measurement this bullet demands was TAKEN 2026-09-09 and is UNINFORMATIVE, which is why the placement above is decided on the boundary instead. `verion-demo-target` at `c68caa7` holds **one** `.py` file in a six-blob tree, so pulling every source file through `VcsProviderPort` costs **two** API calls — one `list_repo_files`, one `get_file_content` — and no documented rate limit is reachable at any auth mode. **"The thing to measure before choosing" could not choose**: the same two calls are the answer under either placement and under any design. Recorded rather than quietly dropped, because a measurement taken and found not to discriminate is a different state from one never taken, and only the first of the two justifies deciding on other grounds. The rest of this bullet's cost list stands unchanged and is what placement (b) actually costs. The auth mode is read off `GitHubAdapter._headers` and identity's OAuth flow — a user-to-server OAuth App token, `scope: repo` — and **no rate-limit figure is recorded anywhere in this repository**, so ADR-009 applies to whoever first needs one.)***
  - **Price both against the floor, which is measured and is empty.** If the extractor is deferred and correlation joins on **raw path prefix** instead, it delivers nothing on this corpus: ZAP's `Location.url` path is `/calculate` and Semgrep's `Location.file_path` is `app.py`, with no prefix relation in either direction. A raw path-prefix join only works where the framework maps URL paths onto file paths — Next.js `pages/`, Django app directories by convention, a static file server — and **Flask's decorator routing does not**, which is the framework `context_detection.py` detects on this very target. So the floor is not "weaker correlation"; it is the same empty relation M5.1 already measured for field equality, arriving by a different mechanism. **The extractor is not an optimization of the route — it is the whole content of it.**
  - **One constraint binds whichever placement wins:** the route map must be re-derivable **at the tree the scan observed**, or M5.5's declaration covers only half of G27's validity condition — the URL/tree coupling would be declared while the route map came silently from a third tree. In (a) that is the deciding cost; in (b) it is inherited but at least sits beside `connected_repo` where somebody would see it.
    - ***(NOT SATISFIABLE under either placement, established 2026-09-09 and decided by ADR-0029 decision 3 as an **accepted, documented residue** — option (i) of the three, not (ii) waiting on G25 and not (iii) bundling part of G25 into this issue. Nothing can supply the revision: `Scan` carries no commit column, `RepoCheckoutPort.checkout` takes no revision, `GitHubAdapter.list_repo_files` requests `/git/trees/HEAD` and `get_file_content` sends no `ref`. So the route map is re-derived at the **default-branch tip at derivation time** — the third tree this bullet names, arriving as a property rather than as a hazard to avoid. **This bullet's "or" is therefore too strong and is corrected rather than left standing:** the residue does **not** stop M5.6 closing the conditioning half of G27, because that criterion is about whether a group is withheld when the declaration is absent, which is a question about the declaration and not about the revision. What a green derived group does and does not mean is stated in ADR-0029 decision 3, in ADR-0028 decision 2's own "voids on reconfiguration, never on drift" shape one axis over, and registered as **G52**.)***
  - **How a derived location enters the match key is DECIDED here, not left to the extractor.** ADR-0029 decision 4, after establishing that **ADR-0023's amendment section 4 states a preference that does not work as written**: *"M5.6 adds a derived location field and the key admits it"* is true of the type and false of the match, because `matches` is `left == right` over the whole frozen dataclass, so a Semgrep key and a ZAP key carrying an equal derived location still differ on `url` and do not co-group. Verified against `matching.py` rather than read off the sentence. **Decided: the derived route path goes into the EXISTING `url` signal and ZAP is keyed on path**, so both sides become `(project_id, None, "/calculate")` and plain equality works with `matches` and `group_by_match_key` unmodified. **No amendment to ADR-0023 is owed** — the frozen three fields, the matching rule, the partition and the conformance test are all untouched, which is the property that chose this shape over the other two. Its price is that a Semgrep finding's `url` carries a value that did not come off `Location.url`, so ADR-0023 section (b)'s provenance guarantee narrows while `build_match_key` keeps type-checking — section (c)'s own *"semantic changes behind an unchanged signature"*, entered deliberately and registered as **G53**.
  - **Re-read G31 before shipping commit 3, and note that this issue is what makes its first out available.** Cited here rather than left in the register alone, for the reason M5.4's own G31 and G39 bullets give: a trigger reachable only by somebody re-reading the register is the inert shape **G28** measures — and until this bullet, the citation ran **one way only**, since G31's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` names M5.6 by number and this block named G31 nowhere. Keying the `url` signal on path **is** that entry's first out, *"re-keys on path with a measurement behind it"*, and **the measurement now exists**: M5.9's committed active capture groups ZAP's 16 findings into 5 on the full URL and 4 on path, with `6-5` and `90036` in different groups — the silent over-split G31 predicts, measured through the shipped matcher rather than hand-counted. **G31 is not resolved by this commit**, which re-keys nothing; commit 3 is what discharges it, and it says which out it took.
    - ***(DONE 2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3.)***
      - **The first out is taken, unconditionally.** `build_match_key` keys `url` on `urlsplit(url).path or "/"`, whether or not a declaration is in force. It is a property of the key, not of whether cross-tool matching is founded.
      - **Measured through the shipped path:** the passive corpus still gives 4 ZAP groups, and M5.9's active capture gives 4 where the full URL gave 5.
      - **G31 is resolved.**

- **M5.7 — Split container-bound scanner tests into their own CI job** — moved to M11.1
  Module: `platform` · Depends on: M5.9
  - ***(MOVED to M11.1 on 2026-09-15, at the M5→M6 boundary, and recorded as a DEPARTURE rather than a move.)*** `CLAUDE.md` says that above 120s the split becomes a scheduled issue in the *next* milestone, and the step's median over ten readings is 137s. The ground for M11 instead: the driver is container-bound scanner tests, and no issue before M11 names one — M6 is domain logic, and M7.1 tests its LLM adapter with a fake plus one network-bound integration test that a container split would not move either. ***(Corrected 2026-09-17, M7.1, ADR-0032 decision 7: M7.1 ships no network-bound test — its adapter runs over a transport seam, in-process. The forecast is false, and the ground it supported holds more strongly: M7.1 adds nothing either kind of split would move.)*** The highest reading, 144s, is under half the 300s blocking threshold. The departure ends if a reading crosses 300s or an issue before M11 adds a container-bound test. The bullets below move with it unchanged, and the id stays, because issue ids are permanent citations here.
  - **Scheduled on a PROJECTION, and the arithmetic is shown rather than asserted, because the threshold `CLAUDE.md` defines has not actually been crossed yet.** Baseline is the measured `Tests (pytest — unit + integration)` step at **71s** (CI run `32715167778`, commit `7cca54b`), leaving **49s** of headroom to the 120s trigger. One active-scan test at the probe's **62s** crosses it: 71 + 62 = **133 > 120**. That is a projection and not a reading — **62s is a hand-run of a container on one machine, not a suite measurement**, and `CLAUDE.md` is explicit that the tracked number is the CI step and that local timings vary by OS and installed binaries. The projection is also a lower bound in one respect and an upper bound in another: it omits standing the target up (G29) and omits the pull, and it assumes the CI runner is no slower than this machine.
    - ***(Superseded 2026-09-09 by a reading, and annotated rather than rewritten because the projection is what this bullet is the record of. Measured: **136s** at `a745975`, CI run `34265788717`. **Both of the projection's terms fall within the recorded ±8s of the measurement.** Its predicted increment was **62s** and the measured delta is **63s** (136s against **73s** at `25488a6`, the preceding commit with no such test). Its base was **71s**, 2s under the actual pre-test base of 73s. Stated as agreement inside the band and not as accuracy to the second: a delta is a difference of two ±8s readings, so its uncertainty is wider than either gap named here. The threshold conclusion does not depend on that resolution — 136 exceeds 120 by far more than the band.)*** *(The ±8s band this applies was M4's; `CLAUDE.md` replaced it on 2026-09-15 with the range 116–144s.)*
  - **This is scheduled ahead of the measurement that would fire it**, which is a deliberate departure from `CLAUDE.md`'s rule ("above 120s, that split becomes a scheduled roadmap issue in the *next* milestone") and is worth being explicit about: the rule keys on a measured step, and no such step exists yet. Scheduling now costs nothing and means the issue is not being scoped under time pressure in the milestone that trips it — the argument M5.0 already made for G3.
    - ***(Moot 2026-09-09, and annotated because the bullets either side of it are. Its *"the rule keys on a measured step, and no such step exists yet"* is present tense and no longer true: the step reads **136s** at `a745975`, CI run `34265788717`. The departure this bullet declares is therefore over — scheduling ran ahead of the measurement for one milestone, and the measurement has since landed and fired the trigger this issue was already scheduled against.)***
  - **M5.9 must report the measured step, and the two thresholds then decide.** Above **120s** the split is scheduled, which this issue already is. Above **300s** it *blocks* rather than defers, in which case **M5.7 runs before M5.9 lands** rather than after it, and this `Depends on:` inverts. Say which happened.
    - ***(Answered 2026-09-09. **120s crossed, 300s not**: the step reads **136s** at `a745975`, CI run `34265788717`. So the split stays **scheduled**, this issue is already that, and its `Depends on: M5.9` does **not** invert. One further fact rather than leaving it implicit: by the time the reading existed, M5.9 had already landed, so the >300s branch — *"M5.7 runs before M5.9 lands"* — was **unreachable** at the moment it was evaluated, and ADR-0027's Consequences' *"M5.9 says which happened"* is false as to which commit says it, since this one does. A conditional that reads live and was inert is the class this project tracks, so it is named here.)***
  - **Explicitly out of scope, and the coupling is narrower than it looks.** The two artifacts that must move together are **`.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `CLAUDE.md`'s Tier 1 table**. `scripts/check_claims.py` is the **checker**, not a third artifact needing an edit: `_ci_run_commands` matches `run:\s*(uv run .+?)\s*$`, reads no step's `name:`, and has no job awareness at all. Three consequences, each established by experiment rather than read off the regex: **renaming a step does not fail the build**; **moving the existing `pytest` step verbatim into a second job does not either**, since the command strings are unchanged and the parser never sees job boundaries; but **changing a gate command does** — and a real split is exactly that, becoming `pytest -m "not container"` plus `pytest -m container` — unless the Tier 1 table is updated in the same change. So M5.7's obligation is: edit `ci.yml`, edit the Tier 1 table, and leave `check_claims.py` alone unless the split needs a new *kind* of assertion. All three are outside a documentation-only change. *(`CLAUDE.md`'s citation bullet records a previous version of itself asserting that `check_claims` keys on step names — the claim this bullet's three experiments exist to settle.)*

## Milestone 6 — Risk / Decision Engine (Weeks 9-11)

**Goal:** correlated Risks get an explainable priority and confidence. FR-7. The other "star" component. ***(Qualified 2026-09-16 at M6.3, the issue that closes this milestone, and qualified rather than struck: **two of FR-7's three named outputs ship and the third does not.** A scored Risk carries a priority bucket and a `RiskReasoning` whose every signal names the member that produced it; it carries **no confidence**. ADR-0005 deferred the scale, M6.2 decided to emit none and asserted the absence, and M7.1 is where it gets chosen — by the consumer that renders it, since rule 6 forbids the Explanation Layer from supplying one itself. **G63** holds the cost. This marker lands in the same commit as M6.3's done marker, so no window exists in which this line asserts a goal the milestone did not meet.)***

**Rules for M6**, set at the M5→M6 boundary review (2026-09-15), each beside the M5 measurement it answers:

- **An ADR only where reversing the decision is expensive** — a schema, a frozen type, a security gate, an external contract. Anything else is decided in the commit message. M5 added seven ADRs (0023–0029) and edited seven older ones, and `docs/adr/` went from 271,350 to 523,368 bytes (git blob sizes, `fe3d342` to `3a89783`).
- **A register entry needs a trigger and something it blocks.** An observation is not an entry. M5 opened 36 (G21–G56); 28 are still open, and 4 of those (G39, G40, G43, G44) state their trigger only in prose, with no trigger label in any capitalisation.
- **Declare a correction ceiling before every `architecture-guardian` pass, counted in correction rounds.** Fewer than a third of M5's 32 commits (`fe3d342..3a89783`) name a declared round in their message, and at least four record three or more guardian passes (`63e7760`, `e0318e1`, `713a50e`, `a745975`).
- **Document decisions, not the drafting process.** The docs-to-src byte ratio went from **1.36** to **2.09** across M5, and M5's own additions ran **3.91 : 1** — 613,781 bytes of docs to 156,861 of `src/`. Span: git blob sizes from `git ls-tree -r -l` at `fe3d342` and `3a89783`; docs are `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/**/*.md` and `.claude/agents/*.md`, and src is `src/**/*.py`. *(Net, not additions — see the measurement below.)*

**The four rules, measured at the M6→M7 boundary (2026-09-16)** over M6's four issue commits, `82b48d5..8c1d3e6`:

- **ADRs:** 2 written (0005, 0030) and 5 amended (0003, 0004, 0018, 0023, 0025), against M5's 7 and 7. `docs/adr/` went from 526,792 to 572,108 bytes (git blob sizes).
- **Register entries:** 7 opened (G61–G67), all 7 with both a `Blocks-if-unresolved:` and a labelled trigger, against M5's 36, of which 4 of the 28 still open stated the trigger in prose only.
- **Declared correction ceiling:** 3 of 4 commit messages declare a round; `2b38d3b` reports one without declaring it. M5: fewer than a third of 32.
- **Docs-to-src bytes:** a **net** delta of **133,227 to 51,089, 2.61 : 1**, and the standing ratio went from 2.13 to 2.17. Method: `git ls-tree -r -l` totals at `82b48d5` and `8c1d3e6`, same file sets as above. **M5's 3.91 : 1 is the same metric**: its 613,781 and 156,861 reproduce exactly as the difference of those totals at `fe3d342` and `3a89783`, so "additions" above mislabels it, and the two figures are comparable.

***(Where these four rules went, 2026-09-16.)*** *They expired when M6 closed. The M6→M7 boundary review measured them without asking whether they continue, and no checklist step asked; step 7 below now does.*
- ***The ADR criterion** is `CLAUDE.md` **rule 16**, with its scope stated so a reviewer can settle it from a diff, and it **changed direction**: this rule was a ceiling, and rule 16 reviews a floor. The half that reduced ADR count is now the half nobody checks.*
- ***The register-entry rule** is a bullet in the Deferred gaps rules list, marked as not enforced.*
- ***The correction ceiling** is part of `CLAUDE.md`'s `architecture-guardian` bullet.*
- ***The docs-to-src ratio LAPSES.** The principle survives in rule 16 with NO measurement behind it, so nothing will notice if M7 returns to M5's 3.91 : 1. It lapses because it had no threshold and M6's 2.61 produced no action — a choice, recorded as one.*

- **M6.1 — Scoring model design** — done
  Module: `risk_engine` · Depends on: M5 · **Decisions recorded in `docs/adr/0005-risk-scoring-model.md`**
  - Formalize the explainable function: severity + confidence + exposure + reachability (where available) + asset sensitivity + environment → priority bucket (`fix_now`/`plan`/`monitor`) + confidence + `RiskReasoning`.
  - Document the exact formula/weights in `docs/adr/0005-risk-scoring-model.md` — this is the piece most worth being able to explain in an interview.
  - **`Finding.confidence` is this issue's to add or to decline** (deferred here by ADR-0018, since M4.1 had no consumer to design a scale against). Two things to design against rather than around: only ZAP supplies confidence, as an opaque numeric code, and its vocabulary mixes degrees (`High`/`Medium`/`Low`) with **states** (`Confirmed`, `False Positive`) — mapping `False Positive` onto a low degree would tell this engine "weak evidence" when ZAP said "not a finding". Semgrep can supply it via rule metadata, under the same G6 caveat as CWE; Trivy has no such concept. If it becomes a scoring input it needs a scale with a state axis, plus a nullable column on M4.3's table.
  - **Two severity inputs come with caveats from ADR-0018.** `Severity.UNKNOWN` means the tool did not know, not "lowest risk" — the enum ranks it lowest for *sorting* only, and treating that as a score would be deciding by accident. And the normalization discards relative position within each tool's own scale (Semgrep's `ERROR` is its maximum; Trivy's `HIGH` means `CRITICAL` was available and declined), which is the argument for keeping `Finding.source` as a scoring input.
  - **Decide how `risk_engine` names what it scores, before any scoring code.** Added by the M5→M6 boundary review, because it is M5.1's wall again and neither M6 issue carried it. `pyproject.toml`'s cross-module contract for `risk_engine` forbids `verion.modules.correlation.domain`, where `MatchGroup` and `MatchKey` live, and `verion.modules.normalization.domain`, where `Finding` lives; `risk_engine/` is empty `__init__.py` files. ***(Falsified 2026-09-16 by M6.2, which added `domain/scoring.py`, `domain/exceptions.py` and `application/compute_risk.py`. Dated rather than rewritten: the sentence is the true statement of the problem this issue's ADR was written against.)*** ADR-0023's Consequences predicted it (*"M6, M7 and M8 each hit exactly the wall M5.1 hit"*), and its alternative 6, a shared structural type in `shared_kernel/` weighed as an amendment to ADR-0018's criterion, is named there as likely to be re-proposed here.
  - **Decide how a candidate Risk reaches `risk_engine`, and what triggers scoring.** `correlation/ports/` publishes nothing. ***(Falsified 2026-09-16 by M6.2, which published `CandidateRiskPort` there. Dated rather than rewritten, for the reason the bullet above gives.)*** The only producer is `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` in `correlation/application/`, which takes a requesting user, and `ListProjectRisksUseCase`'s docstring expects M6 to call it — importing another module's `application/`, which rule 3 forbids and the import-linter contract does not catch (**G35**, whose trigger this fires). A `MatchGroup` carries only `key` and `finding_ids`, and a candidate Risk has no id (ADR-0025 decision 1), so a `RiskReasoning` has nothing stable to name. If scoring runs per request on top of correlation, it adds to the full-project recompute M5.2 measured at 11–12 ms or 26–30 ms depending on plan, and ADR-0025's trigger — *"an order of magnitude beyond the measured 2,000 findings, or any report of a slow Risk listing"* — reopens that decision rather than inviting a cache.
  - **Two of the named inputs have no source, and a third has only a persistence port.** Nothing in `src/` models asset sensitivity or environment. `SecurityContext` carries `exposure_tags` as free text, readable only through `SecurityContextRepositoryPort` — the kind of port ADR-0022 decision 2 says another module must not consume, and whose read raises once a project has two context rows (**G55**). Add each input (a `projects` change with a migration), derive it, or decline it in the ADR.
  - **Settle whether CWE is a scoring input.** **G6** and **G26** assume it is, and §4.1's `RiskReasoning` signals do not include it. If it is, both block this issue; if not, both entries' M6 clauses are wrong and should say so. Re-read **G33** (the next key's conformance test) and **G53** (a derived route path as a scoring input) before the ADR is accepted.
  - **`PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10 lands here.** M5 produces a cross-tool group, and §10 asks for a *materially better prioritization* than raw tool output — prioritization is this milestone's. Say in the ADR whether the `/calculate` `{semgrep, zap}` group ranks above its members read separately, and why.
  - **Delivered — ADR-0005, and what each question above got as an answer.** The ADR was written against a measurement taken first, which is what decided the shape: **the `/calculate` group's membership is produced by the route map, not by agreement between the findings** — in the passive corpus 4 of 4 DAST members are header hygiene, in the active one 2 of 6 are plausibly the same vulnerability and 4 are still headers, and **no field distinguishes the two cases**.
    - **Decision 0, which orders the rest: a Risk is a SURFACE** — the package or route path its match key names — **not a vulnerability.** Chosen rather than discovered; the vulnerability reading would have blocked M6.2 on a signal that exists nowhere. It generalizes past the route case because 3 of the 7 groups are Trivy package groups, keyed on the three distinct `PkgName`s its 20 findings carry.
    - **The function:** `severity_signal + exposure_signal + corroboration_signal`, bucketed `fix_now` ≥ 6 / `plan` 4–5 / `monitor` < 4, each signal recorded with the member that produced it. **`UNKNOWN` contributes nothing and is never the maximum** — the enum's bottom rank stays a sort position. **Refused inputs, each measured:** CVSS (20/20 on Trivy, and absent from every member of the cross-tool surface — 0 of 5 passive, 0 of 7 active), member count (`urllib3` is n=12), CWE, and `native_severity`.
    - **Naming:** `correlation` publishes its first port; `risk_engine/application/` takes the groups by inference and passes **scalars** into its domain. ADR-0023's alternative 6 was re-proposed as that entry forecast and **rejected** — scalars need no shared name, so its one-copy-instead-of-four argument prices a problem this shape does not have.
    - **Trigger:** per request, persisting nothing, so ADR-0025 decisions 1 and 2 hold and **G37** stays latent. **G35's second trigger fired and the hole was not taken** — the port is why.
    - **CWE declined** (Semgrep contributes 0 of 1; `cwe` is in `_REFRESHED_COLUMNS`), so **G6's and G26's M6 clauses are retired** and both entries say so. **`Finding.confidence` declined**, discharging ADR-0018 decision 5 — with the caveat no document carried: only confidence codes `1`/`2`/`3` appear in either capture, so the degrees-versus-states hazard is **unexhibited** and a scale designed now would be designed against ZAP's documentation. **Asset sensitivity, environment and reachability declined** for having no source, which is a dated amendment to **ADR-0003's** own Decision.
    - **§10 answered at both widths.** The `/calculate` surface scores `6 → fix_now` and is the only one that does; its members scored alone reach 4 in the passive corpus and 5 in the active one, so the surface is strictly above every one of them in both. Whole corpus: **1 `fix_now` and 6 `plan` with the declaration in force, 8 `plan` and no `fix_now` without it**, in both corpora. And the half the block did not ask: it does **not** rank above the other groups on any per-finding field — the lift is entirely two group-level facts, so §10 is satisfied as a traceable difference from raw tool output and **not** as a claim that this surface outranks `urllib3`'s twelve CVEs.
    - **Deferred, and the deferral is a finding about this milestone's own rule:** the Risk's `confidence` value was drafted inside decision 1 and **cut by the declared byte ceiling**, which is that rule working as intended — a second decision hiding inside a long one is what it exists to catch. It goes to M6.2 as **G63**, so ADR-0005 does not fully discharge ADR-0003's shape and says so.
    - **Opened: G61** (a scored listing reads the project's findings twice), **G62** (the corroboration signal cannot tell a substantive pair from a coincidental co-location, and the only measured instance is coincidental) and **G63** (the deferred confidence).
    - **The byte ceiling was declared before drafting and MISSED, and it is recorded as missed rather than re-declared.** Declared: **14,000 bytes** for `docs/adr/0005-risk-scoring-model.md` as a git blob (LF), of which **8,000** for the `## Decision` span. Measured at acceptance: **14,399 and 8,629** — over by 399 and 629. ***(Both figures are "at acceptance" and stay as measured. Recorded 2026-09-16 because the file they measure moved in M6.2: that issue's dated amendment at decision 5 lands inside the `## Decision` span, taking the blob to **15,172** and the span to **9,402**, +773 on each. The ceiling was M6.1's and is not re-declared for an amendment; this note exists so the figures above are not read as current.)*** Five measurements across the drafting: 15,713/9,982 → 15,062/9,335 → 14,430/8,926 → 14,139/8,635 → 13,625/8,121, the last of which **met the total ceiling**, and then the `architecture-guardian` correction round put it back over by 774 bytes. That direction is the point worth keeping: the corrections *lengthened* the text because each replaced a compact wrong figure with a correct one — `0/29` became "0 of 5 passive, 0 of 7 active", "M6.2 and M6.3 write no rows" became the split ADR-0025 decision 2 actually leaves open, and "M6.2 owes a synthetic fixture" became the narrower thing it owes given `trivy_synthetic_edges.json` already exists. A sixth trim would have bought bytes with the precision that round had just paid for. **What the ceiling did earn is not the byte count**: it caught the Risk's `confidence` scale as a second decision drafted inside decision 1 and forced it out to G63, which is exactly the failure the rule exists to detect.
  - *Candidate input, not scheduled:* whether the tree's own source names a vulnerable package in an import statement — a **named-in-source** signal, not reachability. On the committed corpus, 6 of the 20 Trivy findings are Werkzeug's, and the demo target's one source file never names Werkzeug. But Flask imports Werkzeug itself, so those six sit on a package that is loaded at runtime: read as reachability, this signal would call a reachable package unreachable. Derived from the context-build archive, it would inherit G52, G55 and G56.
