  - `ComputeRiskUseCase`, pure domain logic, unit tested extensively — this is the highest-value test suite in the whole project (must be provably explainable and stable).
  - **Implements ADR-0005** (added 2026-09-16): the function, the bucket thresholds, and the two ports of its decision 2. It writes **no rows** — decision 3, and ADR-0025 decisions 1 and 2 behind it.
  - **And decides what ADR-0005 deferred: the Risk's own `confidence` value (G63).** FR-7 and ADR-0003 both require one and M6.1 ships none, so this issue either emits a confidence or records that it does not. The scale drafted and cut was a property of the **grouping** — `read` when every member attached through its own `Location`, `derived` when any member attached through a route map under a serving declaration, which would make **G53**'s provenance user-visible and would mark the `/calculate` surface `derived` in both corpora.
  - **Two branches of the function cannot be exercised by CAPTURED data, and this is acceptance criteria rather than a note.** `UNKNOWN` and `CRITICAL` are **0 of 34 and 0 of 37** across the two real corpora, so decision 1's `UNKNOWN` rule and the top of the scale are unreachable from real scanner output. **Synthetic fixtures producing both already exist** — `tests/fixtures/scanners/trivy_synthetic_edges.json` yields one `critical` and one `unknown` — so what is owed is **scoring-level coverage over those branches**, not a new fixture. Any hand-written `Finding` this issue does add is **G19** and **G40**'s shape, against ADR-0026's profile or explicitly excused.
  - **Also update `_RANK`'s comment in `shared_kernel/severity.py`**, which still reads *"M6 must decide what UNKNOWN means to a score explicitly"* — ADR-0005 decision 1 has now decided it, and M6.1 was a documentation commit that touched no `src/` file, so the stale sentence sits in the one place a reader of the scoring code would trust.
  - **"Pure domain logic" is conditional on M6.1's naming decision** (marked 2026-09-15): as written it is the sentence the M4→M5 boundary review found unbuildable for M5.1. Its hand-built `Finding` or group fixtures are the next case of **G40** and **G19** — fixture shape differing from production — so write them against ADR-0026's profile or say why not.
  - **Delivered.** `score_surface` and `bucket_for` in `risk_engine/domain/scoring.py`, `ComputeRiskUseCase` in its `application/`, and `correlation`'s first published port — `CandidateRiskPort` with `CorrelationCandidateRisks` over `CorrelateFindingsUseCase`, wired in `platform/di.py` where the port-annotated return type is the only place `mypy --strict` checks the conformance. No rows, no migration, no endpoint. **927 tests pass (890 + 37)**; the new tests are in-memory and cost **0.23s** across all three files, so nothing lands on the container-bound budget CLAUDE.md tracks. *(Both figures are post-guardian: the correction round added four tests, and the pre-round figures — 923 and 0.18s — were stale the moment it did.)*
    - **The naming wall held as ADR-0005 decision 2 designed it.** `risk_engine` names no other module's type: groups arrive by inference off the port's annotation, and the domain takes `SurfaceMember` — its own kw-only carrier over `finding_id`/`source`/`severity`. One structural consequence worth recording, because it constrains any future refactor: **no helper in `ComputeRiskUseCase` may take a group or a finding as a parameter**, since `mypy --strict` demands an annotation and the only available annotation is the forbidden import. Inference survives only while the values stay local, so the loop stays inline.
    - **G63 answered: no confidence is emitted, and the absence is asserted.** See that entry for the cost and the reason. **G64 opened:** `fix_now` has exactly one reachable route, so the top bucket is closed to every package surface — the `CRITICAL` fixture reaches `plan`, not `fix_now`, and ADR-0005 decision 5 carries a dated amendment recording that its prescribed branch coverage does not reach that bucket.
    - **§10 is answered at the width the evidence supports, in the test rather than in prose.** The margin is **one point**: strip the corroboration term and `/calculate` ties the `CRITICAL` package surface at 5, in the same bucket, because exposure is not what correlation buys — a DAST-only surface has it too, which the `/search` closure proves. Both closures are named tests, and the `6 > 5` ordering claim is stated *after* the margin that produces it, deliberately. **G62** carries the consequence: the entire §10 lift is one point computed over four header-hygiene DAST members.
    - **Ten mutations, applied physically one at a time, reverted, sha256-verified. My eight had 0 survivors; the `architecture-guardian` then found TWO MORE THAT SURVIVED**, and they are recorded as survivals with their fixes rather than silently as repairs, because a sweep that reports only its final state is the misreport a sweep exists to prevent.
      1. *Deleting the wrapper's `ProjectAccessDenied → CandidateRiskAccessDenied` translation* passed the entire suite. Cause: every test faked the **port**, so the published contract was exercised and the only implementation of it never was — nothing outside `platform/di.py` constructed `CorrelationCandidateRisks`. Fix: `tests/unit/test_candidate_risk_provider.py`, which builds the real wrapper over a real `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` and asserts the domain exception does **not** escape the port.
      2. *Swapping the two ids sent to the port* passed. Cause: `FakeCandidateRisks` advertised "records its calls" and no assertion ever read them. Fix: `test_the_port_is_asked_for_the_project_and_caller_the_use_case_was_given`. Under the real wrapper that swap denies every caller, and it would have surfaced first at M6.3's endpoint.
      Both re-run after the fix: **killed, 0 survivors of 2.** What generalises past these two is registered as **G65** rather than kept here, because a milestone bullet is not what a boundary review reads: faking a port proves the consumer's contract and says nothing about the adapter behind it, and `platform/di.py`'s port-annotated return type checks shape rather than behaviour. Each survivor was also re-measured against the whole suite as it stood when it survived — **923 passed** in both cases, not merely the 630 unit tests the guardian ran.
    - Of the original eight, two are siblings that no bucket assertion can kill and only a value or reasoning assertion can — *let `UNKNOWN` contribute its rank* (its rank is 0 and the no-member fallback is also 0, so the score is identical either way) and *count members instead of distinct sources* (bucket-invariant across every **Trivy package** surface, though only because each tops out at HIGH — Werkzeug is 1 HIGH / 5 MEDIUM, and without that single HIGH it would cross the `plan` boundary and be killed by a bucket after all). ***(Corrected in the guardian round: "bucket-invariant across every committed surface" was the claim and it is FALSE. The `zap_synthetic_edges.json` `/` surface carries two members — `10015` at INFO and the instance-less `99999` keyed to `/` off the site name at LOW — and moves 3 → 4, crossing into `plan`. No test asserts that surface's bucket, so the value assertion is what kills the mutation in practice; the sibling claim holds for the suite as written, not for the corpus.)***
    - **No hand-written `Finding`**, so **G40** is untouched and its count unchanged; every finding comes through a real mapper over a committed capture and every group through the real matcher. The hand-written values are `SurfaceMember` combinations, two of which no pipeline can emit — carried in **G19**'s dated note, with `UNREACHABLE` in the `parametrize` ids.

This one was arithmetically wrong before a line was written. Calling it "missed, on M6.1's precedent" would import that event's meaning onto a different event, and the byte ceiling is the instrument that caught **G63**, so what it reports has to stay legible. **Five** measurements across drafting: **15,308 / 11,309** at first draft; **13,865 / 9,765** after removing duplication the ceiling made visible — decision 1 rejecting two path spellings that `## Alternatives considered` already held, and decisions 6 and 7 restating mechanisms **G64**'s entry and `score_surface`'s docstring carry in full; and **14,106 / 9,429** after moving decision 1's authorization paragraph into `## Consequences`, since that paragraph corrected another document and recorded G17's growth and decided nothing. **That move made the total WORSE by 241 bytes while improving the Decision span by 336** — M6.1's own finding in miniature, that a correction lengthens. Worth naming where it happened rather than only that it did: a summary clause asserting slightly more than the figures printed directly beneath it, inside the one paragraph whose whole purpose is keeping this instrument's reports legible. That is this project's recorded failure class at its smallest scale and in its least forgiving location.)*** So the content sits near what this repository's own density predicts for seven decisions, and the declared number is the thing that was wrong. **It is deliberately NOT re-declared**, here or for the next issue: a ceiling chosen after the measurement it exists to constrain is an arbitrary figure in a criterion's clothes, which is the objection ADR-0025's Consequences already makes about inventing a latency threshold. A further trim now would buy bytes by deleting cited grounds — ADR-0005 decision 3's ground for refusing the widened port, the corrected measurement framing, and the upper-bound reporting condition — which is the trade M6.1 refused at its sixth.)*** ***(The file MOVED in M6.3's second commit, and that is recorded here rather than measured back into the figures above — which are "at acceptance" and stay as measured, on ADR-0005's own precedent, whose ceiling record carries this same note because M6.2's amendment moved the file it measured. ADR-0030's blob is now **20,309** bytes, **+3,232** on the 17,077 it was accepted at. It moved **twice inside this one commit** — first for the measurement decision 7 owed, then for the `architecture-guardian` round's corrections to how that measurement was stated — and the figure here is the final one, measured after the round rather than before it, since a byte figure taken before a correction round is a figure that round invalidates. **The `## Decision` span is UNCHANGED at 11,046 through BOTH moves**: the measurement landed in `## Consequences`, and every guardian correction landed in `## Context`, `## Consequences` or `## Alternatives considered`. So the half this ceiling was actually about never moved, and no decision was added to an ADR that was already accepted — which is the property worth carrying forward, rather than the total. Not re-declared, here or for the next issue.)***

  - **Delivered.** `GET /projects/{project_id}/scored-risks` under `risk_engine` — a **new** route coexisting with M5.2's, because `cross-module-correlation` forbids `risk_engine.domain` and a mapper in `correlation` could not annotate `ScoredSurface`. `ListScoredRisksUseCase` composes `ComputeRiskUseCase`, which is unchanged and still returns `correlation`'s group order; `rank_surfaces` in `risk_engine/domain/` applies the priority order above it, tie-broken by `_group_order`'s own tuple so the two routes agree within a bucket — an invariant across two modules pinned by a test importing **both** orderings. Thresholds ship on the envelope from `scoring.py`'s constants, the completeness envelope is carried, `MemberFindingMissing` is a chosen 500, and the item's key set is asserted to **equal** ADR-0030 decision 3's enumeration so that decision and **G66** cannot go false together while green. **No rows, no migration, no `risks` table** — G37 and G11 stay latent, M8.1 remains the forced write. **647 unit + 311 integration pass.** All 18 tests in `test_scored_risks_routes.py` are in-process against Postgres and **none is container-bound**: the full integration suite reads **162.96s at 311 tests** against **162.74s at 308** before the guardian round added three of them, a difference well inside run-to-run variance. *(Both are LOCAL Windows runs and are deliberately NOT offered as the tracked number, which CLAUDE.md defines as the CI `Tests (pytest …)` step and says to measure in CI precisely because local runtime varies; they are recorded only as evidence that this issue added nothing to the container-bound budget, which is the claim that matters for the M5.7 departure.)*
    - **The measurement this issue owed is taken, and it does not support the decision that prompted it.** At ADR-0025's volume the end-to-end read has a median of **108.8 ms** against a route gap of **115.3 ms**, so the doubled read is the **larger term on every estimator** (94.4% / 52.9% / 73.9% at medians / minima / maxima). By ADR-0025's own standard that is evidence **against** its decision 1 and calls for an amendment to that ADR — which M6.3 does not take, and **G61** carries the escalation with ADR-0025 noted beside the paragraph setting the criterion.
    - **An earlier reading of that run was backwards and is recorded rather than quietly fixed**: it subtracted `EXPLAIN`'s server-side `Execution Time` from an end-to-end difference and called the second read the minor half at ~17–20%. End to end is **4.08×** the server-side figure. `_measure_reads` exists because of it; **G19** carries the failure shape, since sound-looking verification disconnected from what it covers is that entry's subject arriving through a report rather than a generator.
    - **ADR-0025's *"scales with a project's finding count rather than with the number of projects"* is STRUCK, not qualified** — on the `Hash Right Join` plan the evidence side is a `Seq Scan` over the whole table, 12.436 ms of a 26.661 ms read spent on other projects' rows.
    - Dated notes: **G17** (fourth 404-for-both route, and its own post-M4 note's "six" is the figure G18 retired), **G19**, **G38** (names M6.3 and its condition is *not* met), **G61**, **G64** (closure now shown at an endpoint and asserted there), **G65** (instance discharged, entry open). **G37** and **G11** get none: a fired trigger is recorded, an unfired conditional is not.

## Milestone 7 — Security Brief / Explanation Layer (Weeks 11-12)

**Goal:** each scored Risk gets a developer-facing narrative. FR-8. ***(Qualified 2026-09-19 at the M7→M8 boundary review, which closes M7, and qualified rather than struck: **M7 ships three of FR-8's original six parts** — *why it matters*, *what happened*, and evidence sources as each Brief's `finding_ids`. Confidence is **M8.5** (**G63**). Recommended action and estimated effort are cut to V2 by `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` FR-8's 2026-09-19 note.)***

- **M7.1 — ExplanationProviderPort + LLM adapter** — done
  Module: `brief` · Depends on: M6
  - Port interface; adapter calls the model with a strict prompt: structured `RiskReasoning` in, narrative text out — no re-deciding priority.
  - Test with a fake/deterministic adapter for CI stability; real LLM call covered by a small integration test.
  - **Carries G63** (added 2026-09-16): a scored Risk has no `confidence`, and rule 6 forbids the adapter supplying one, so FR-8's confidence is a scale this issue decides in `risk_engine` or records as absent.
  - **Carries G33 and G65** (added 2026-09-16): `brief` may not name `risk_engine.domain` and `risk_engine/ports/` is empty, so `RiskReasoning` in needs a port `risk_engine` publishes first, and that port's adapter needs a test that does not fake it (G65).
  - **Carries G62** (added 2026-09-16): the prompt this issue designs must not have the model assert `corroboration_signal` as two tools agreeing; M7.3 carries the same clause because G62's trigger names it.
  - **Decided in `docs/adr/0032-explanation-provider-port-openai-adapter-and-prompt-boundary.md`** (added 2026-09-17, before any of this issue's code is committed).
    - The first bullet's *"structured `RiskReasoning` in"* is unbuildable as written: the port's parameter cannot name `risk_engine.domain`, and `RiskReasoning` carries no bucket, score or thresholds, so a narrator given only it would have to decide the bucket. `risk_engine` publishes a frozen `ExplainableDecision` in its `ports/` and fills it in its `application/` (decisions 1 and 2).
    - **The prompt's input ends there**: nothing scanned, so no `package`, `url`, finding id or finding text. No confidence (decision 3). Provider OpenAI, model `gpt-5-mini`, raw `httpx2` (decision 5).
  - ***(DEPARTURE from `CLAUDE.md`'s definition of done, recorded 2026-09-17, before this issue's code is committed.)***
    - **What is departed from.** *"Integration tests for any new adapter, against the real dependency."* This bullet's own *"real LLM call covered by a small integration test"* is **not delivered** either.
    - **What ships instead** (ADR-0032 decision 7). `OpenAIExplanationProvider`'s own code runs over `httpx2.MockTransport`; a contract test holds the fake and the real adapter to the same assertions; a factory test sends a request through `get_explanation_provider`. **OpenAI's real contract is exercised by nothing in CI.**
    - **Why this is weaker than its precedent.** `test_github_adapter.py` also runs over `MockTransport`, but replays **captured** payloads. This issue's 200 and 401 fixtures follow openai-python's types and a 2023 user-pasted error, not a capture.
    - **Why not a live test.** With no secret in `ci.yml` it would be this repository's first skip, skipping on every run — the hazard `test_active_scan_finds_the_sink.py`'s docstring names, *"a skip is itself a false negative that reads as green"*, though that docstring argues it for its own test rather than as a rule for all of them. With a secret it costs money per run and cannot be deterministic.
    - **"Captured" below means a response this project records from the real API.** The 2023 401 in AutoGPT issue #1422 is user-pasted evidence, not a capture.
    - **The departure ends when a captured OpenAI response replaces the schema-derived fixtures** — a 200 and a 401 from the real API, redacted, committed, and replayed by the same tests. That puts this adapter on `GitHubAdapter`'s footing, which is the thing departed from in substance.
    - **The captured 200 keeps its `usage` object whole** *(added 2026-09-17)*: `prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, and `completion_tokens_details`, especially `reasoning_tokens`. Do not trim the fixture to the fields the adapter reads. `gpt-5-mini` is a reasoning model: its reasoning tokens are billed as output and never appear in `content`, so this capture is the **only measurement of what one narration costs**. The per-Brief figure the model was chosen against assumed 250 output tokens and so counted no reasoning tokens. Also record with the capture the one condition the figure depends on: the request sends no `reasoning_effort`, so it measures the model's default effort, and changing that invalidates it. The numbers go into ADR-0032's Consequences. **`Explanation` gains no usage field**: nothing consumes one, on ADR-016 decision 3's and ADR-0021's precedent against speculative fields.
    - **M7.2's first real call path is when the capture becomes OWED, not an end.** A production caller changes nothing that CI exercises; only a capture changes the footing. So the commit that first calls `explain` outside a test either takes the capture or records, in one line, why not, and that line re-dates this departure.
    - **Re-dated 2026-09-17 (M7.2 commit 2), the first call outside a test.** The capture is not taken because `OpenAIExplanationProvider._parse` reads only `choices` and `model` and discards `usage`, so recording the response whole needs recording tooling that does not exist~~ and a transport or adapter change this issue does not make~~ *(clause struck 2026-09-17, M7.3, as false: the adapter's `transport=` seam suffices, and only the script was missing; ADR-0032's M7.3 amendment striking the adapter-change clause)*. The departure stands, and the 30 s timeout stays unmeasured for the same reason.
    - **Re-dated 2026-09-17 (M7.3 commit 2), a second call path outside a test.** `describe` joins `explain` on `POST /projects/{project_id}/briefs`. The capture is not taken in this commit: it is taken by the owner with `scripts/capture_openai_responses.py` and lands in M7.3's next commit (ADR-0034 decision 7). No adapter change is needed for it (ADR-0032's M7.3 amendment striking the adapter-change clause).
    - ***(ENDED 2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 3.)*** **The end condition above is met.** `tests/integration/fixtures/openai/` holds a 200 captured from the real API for each prompt, `usage` whole, and a captured 401, redacted: the completion id, and the 401's echo of the throwaway key it was sent, which the condition's *"redacted"* did not know would be there (**G71**). `tests/integration/test_openai_explanation_provider.py` replays them through the same tests that ran the schema-derived bodies, and `test_explanation_provider_contract.py` replays the `explain` 200. The token and wall-time figures, with n and the condition that no `reasoning_effort` is sent, are in ADR-0032's Consequences. **What stays unexercised** is what OpenAI sends on any later day: nothing in CI calls it (**G65**).
    - **Checked at every boundary** by checklist step 5's sub-bullet for this departure.
  - **Delivered.**
    - **`risk_engine`**, a module this entry's `Module:` line does not name: `ports/explainable_decision.py` (`ExplainableDecision`, `ExplainableSignal`); `application/explainable_decision.py`, the one fill site, with no caller in `src/` until M7.2; three `*_DEFINITION` constants in `domain/scoring.py`.
    - **`brief`**: `ports/explanation_provider.py`; `domain/explanation.py` and `domain/exceptions.py`; `adapters/outbound/explanation/prompt.py` (`PROMPT_VERSION = "m7.1-1"`) and `openai_adapter.py`.
    - **`platform`**: `get_explanation_provider` in `di.py`, with no consumer; `openai_api_key` as the fourth `_DEV_ONLY_DEFAULTS` entry and `openai_model = "gpt-5-mini"` in `settings.py`. `httpx2` moved to `[project]` (**G72**).
    - **Tests: 15 unit and 29 integration added.** None is scanner-container-bound, and none sends a request outside the process. The integration ones need the Postgres service through `tests/integration/conftest.py`'s autouse `_clean_all_tables`, like every file there. Whole suite locally, after the guardian round: **1019 passed** (1018 before that round added one case). *(A local Windows run, deliberately not offered as the tracked CI figure.)*
    - **Eleven mutations**, applied physically one at a time and reverted sha256-clean, **all killed**. Ten are listed in **G65**'s note. The eleventh was found in the guardian round: a non-string `finish_reason` reaching the set lookup raised `TypeError` out of the port, and `test_an_unusable_200_is_unavailable[unhashable-finish-reason]` was added with the fix and fails against the old line. **The factory swap survived 1,017 tests** — the whole suite minus the one test written for it — and that test kills it.
    - **What this issue does not prove**: that OpenAI accepts the request, that `gpt-5-mini` takes the `developer` role, or that the model obeys the prompt. Nothing in CI calls the API (the departure above).

Two of FR-8's six parts. *What happened*, *recommended action* and *estimated effort* are **G74**; confidence is **G63**.
      ***(Marked 2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 2: three of FR-8's six parts since M7.3, which adds `what_happened` as a second whole `Explanation` in three nullable columns; `None` only for Briefs written before it. ADR-0033's M7.3 amendment superseding decision 2's rejection of `what_happened`.)***

Every route test overrides `get_explanation_provider`, so nothing reaches OpenAI. Whole suite locally, after the guardian round: **1077 passed** (1019 before). *(A local Windows run, not the tracked CI figure.)*
    - **Mutations: 35 runs, every one killed, each failure observed from the run's own output.** Each was applied physically and alone, then reverted sha256-clean. The two that change the migration downgraded the database first and re-upgraded after the revert.
      - **The first 26 ran before the guardian round.** Its fixes changed the code under three of them: the port's match became equality over sorted ids, and the version check became `type(...) is int`. Those three were re-run against the fixed code as 1′, 2′ and 22′.
      - **Five mutations were added for the round's new guards**: repeated ids collapsed into a set, a version compared with `==` only, leaf values not type-checked, the list route ignoring its paging query, and the list route dropping its upper bound. (The reader ignoring the version is 22′, the re-run.)
      - **So 35 runs = 26 + 3 re-runs + 5 new + 1 re-run of mutation 17.**

      - **Write order.** Writing before narration failed `test_a_provider_failure_writes_nothing`.

    - **What this issue does not prove.**
      - That OpenAI accepts the request, or what a narration costs (M7.1's departure, re-dated above).
      - That the request's session is held across the provider call in a way that matters under load (**G73**, read from the code).
      - That any real surface reaches a member count a POST body cannot carry.

- **M7.3 — Prompt safety** — done
  Module: `brief` · Depends on: M7.1
  - Explicit handling for the fact that raw finding/evidence text originates from scanned (potentially untrusted) source code — sanitize/constrain what gets interpolated into the LLM prompt to avoid prompt-injection-via-scanned-content.
  - **Carries G62** (added 2026-09-16): the Brief must not narrate `corroboration_signal` as two tools agreeing.
  - **Carries G74** (added 2026-09-17): its trigger names this issue, the first place scanned content could reach a prompt.
  - **Decided in `docs/adr/0034-what-happened-typed-members-two-calls-and-the-prompt-input-boundary.md`** (added 2026-09-17, before any of this issue's code is committed).
    - **The material.** One new Brief part, *what happened*, written from each member's typed `title` and `Location`. No `raw_payload` is parsed, and `normalization`'s mappers and `Finding` do not change. *Recommended action*, *estimated effort* and *confidence* stay out (**G74**, **G63**). A Semgrep member contributes a rule id, a file and a line, because its title is its rule id.
    - **The boundary.** `brief` reads members through `FindingRepositoryPort.get_by_id`, after `ExplainableRiskPort`'s verdict, into its own `BriefMember`. `ExplainableDecision` does not change, so the stored decision's version does not.
    - **Two calls.** `explain`'s prompt stays byte-identical (`m7.1-1`) and holds no attacker-controlled bytes. A new `describe` call narrates the members first; both succeed or nothing is written.
    - **The sanitizer.** Caps chosen from the measured per-surface aggregate (largest: `urllib3`, 12 members, 1,599 characters), Unicode control and format characters stripped, members JSON-encoded, and `describe` output validated without ever rejecting on text the members supplied. Every mechanism names the test that fails without it.
    - **The list** keeps *what happened* whole. **The capture** is taken by a script over the adapter's existing `transport=` seam, run by the owner, and lands in its own commit.
    - **Commits:** this ADR; the implementation; the capture and its replay.
  - **Delivered in commit 2 of 3, the implementation.** This issue is not done until the capture commit. *(It is done with commit 3, below.)*
    - **`brief`**:
      - `domain/brief_member.py` (`BriefMember`, the caps, `from_scalars`' stripping and truncation, `__post_init__`'s refusal).
      - `domain/exceptions.py` (`WhatHappenedRejected`, a subclass of `ExplanationUnavailable`; `BriefMemberMissing`) and `domain/security_brief.py` (`what_happened: Explanation | None`).
      - `ports/explanation_provider.py` (`describe`).
      - `application/generate_security_brief.py` (member reads through `FindingRepositoryPort`, the order, M6's output validation).
      - `adapters/outbound/explanation/describe_prompt.py` (`DESCRIBE_PROMPT_VERSION = "m7.3-1"`) and `openai_adapter.py` (`describe` over the same request path; `prompt.py`'s `PROMPT_VERSION` is unchanged at `m7.1-1`).
      - `adapters/outbound/db/` (three nullable columns and the all-or-none check) and `adapters/inbound/api/` (`what_happened` on both routes; a fixed 500 for `BriefMemberMissing`).
    - **`platform`**: `get_generate_security_brief_use_case` takes `FindingRepositoryDep`.
    - **Migration** `c5d2e8f41a93_add_what_happened_to_security_briefs.py`, revising `a9e3d6b40c17`. `upgrade`, `downgrade -1` and `upgrade` ran cleanly against the local database.
    - **Not changed, as ADR-0034 requires:** `ExplainableDecision`, `ExplainableSignal`, `Explanation`, `_DECISION_VERSION`, and `prompt.py`'s prompt text.
    - **Tests: 35 unit and 19 integration added** (collected counts, 717 → 752 and 360 → 379). Whole suite locally: **1131 passed** (1077 before). *(A local Windows run, not the tracked CI figure.)*
    - **Mutations: 23 runs, every one killed**, each applied physically and alone and reverted sha256-clean, each failure read from the run's own output. The runs cover `tests/unit`, the four `brief` integration files and `test_schema_matches_models.py` (821 tests), **not the whole suite**. Runs are named by the harness's labels below.
      - **M1, cleaning deleted, is killed by the constructor, not by the prompt test's assertion.** `test_from_scalars_cleans_hostile_input` and `test_describe_prompt_carries_no_control_bidi_or_zero_width_character` both fail on `ValueError: BriefMember.title carries a character sanitization strips`. **M1b**, deleting the refusal as well, reaches both tests' own assertions. M2 and M2b behave the same way for truncation.
      - **M3**, the cap where members are read: `test_a_surface_over_the_cap_reads_and_describes_twenty_of_its_full_size` only. **M3r**, the cap where members are rendered (added in the guardian round, as ADR-0034's M3 row requires a rendered-prompt test): `test_a_surface_over_twenty_members_renders_twenty_and_says_of_n` only. **M4**, `json.dumps` replaced by `' | '.join(f"{m['scanner']}: {m['title']}" ...)`: six tests, including `test_a_title_forging_json_cannot_add_a_member`.
      - **M5a** (members carried into `explain`) and **M5b** (the decision carried into `describe`): each killed only by `test_the_priority_request_carries_no_member_byte_and_the_member_request_no_decision`, which sends describe, explain, describe so a carry in either direction lands in a body it reads.
      - **M6**: each check deleted alone (three runs) is killed by its parametrized rejection case. **M6b, the member-supplied exclusion removed, is killed only by `test_a_member_supplied_fix_now_echoed_by_the_model_still_generates`.**
      - **M7**, `raw_payload` rendered as the title: five tests, including `test_no_raw_payload_byte_reaches_the_describe_prompt`.
      - **Order and atomicity**: `explain` before `describe` (9 tests), an `add` before the second call (16), member reads before the port (5, including the denial test), a missing member skipped (1).
      - **Storage and wiring**: the list dropping `what_happened` (3), the migration without its check (5, including the schema test), `BriefMember.url` narrowed to `str` (only the G33 conformance test), `what_happened=None` written (5), the factory wiring the Brief repository as the findings repository (11 route tests).
      - **A harness defect, recorded.** The first run of the migration mutation (label M14) restored the migration file before downgrading, so its downgrade failed on a constraint the mutated upgrade never created. The local schema was repaired by hand (columns dropped, `alembic stamp a9e3d6b40c17`, `upgrade head`), both constraints were confirmed present, and four runs were made under a corrected harness: the migration without its check, `url` narrowed, `what_happened=None` written, and the factory miswiring. **After the guardian round**, M1, M1b, M3, M3r, M4 and M7 were re-run against the changed tests, with the killers recorded above.
    - **What this commit does not prove**: that OpenAI accepts either request, what either costs, how long either takes, or that a real model follows `describe`'s instructions. The capture is the next commit's.
  - **Delivered in commit 3 of 3, the capture and its replay.**
    - **`scripts/capture_openai_responses.py`**, run by the owner. It drives the real `GenerateSecurityBriefUseCase` over the committed demo corpus with in-memory storage ports, records through the adapter's `transport=` seam, reads `OPENAI_API_KEY` from `infra/.env` through `Settings` with the environment as fallback, and writes nothing unless the leak scan reports zero. Three passes: one repetition, whose three `describe` paragraphs the owner read before going on; ten repetitions; and the invalid-key 401.
    - **`tests/integration/fixtures/openai/`**: the two 200s, the 401 and `measurements.json`, with a README carrying the redaction table.
    - **Replay.** `test_openai_explanation_provider.py` and `test_explanation_provider_contract.py` replay the captured bodies in place of the schema-derived ones, which ends the M7.1 departure.
    - **Tests: 5 integration added and no unit test** (collected counts, 379 to 384, and 752 unchanged). The five read the captured fixtures: one replay per prompt, `usage` whole and the id redacted per prompt, and one that the 401 carries each redaction marker exactly once. `tests/unit/test_settings.py` changed without gaining a case.
    - **Three mutations, applied physically one at a time, reverted sha256-clean, all killed.** A redaction that drops the key echo instead of marking it, and a 401 with no `message` field, are each killed by `test_the_captured_401_holds_each_marker_once_and_the_replay_echoes_this_modules_key` and by nothing else: with the echo gone the leak tests pass vacuously, which is why that test exists. `usage` trimmed to the field the adapter reads is killed by `test_a_captured_200_keeps_usage_whole_and_its_id_redacted`.
    - **Every file written was scanned** for Cc (other than tab, LF and CR), Cf, Zl and Zp before staging, `docs/ROADMAP.md` included: zero (**G80**).
    - **What this commit deliberately does not mark.** M7's own milestone marker and any qualification of its Goal against FR-8, and `README.md`'s M7 checkbox. All three of M7's issues are now done, and closing the milestone is the boundary review's act (`README.md`'s status line says where M7 stands). **G62**'s unread `explain` outputs are recorded for that review, which the owner prices at one `--repetitions 1` run with `explain` text printed.
    - **Measured** (ADR-0032's Consequences and M7.3 capture amendment; ADR-0034's M7.3 capture amendment):
      - **59 calls, not 60.** One `describe` on `/calculate` timed out at 30.32 s, so its exercise never called `explain`.
      - **The timeout stays 30 s, and its value is handed to whatever makes generation asynchronous** (**G73**), as one decision.
      - **Token totals are a floor**, 28,731 prompt and 76,518 completion, because the timed-out call returned no `usage`.
      - **M6's 2,000-character cap is live**: about 410 visible tokens on `describe urllib3`'s medians, which is **an estimated** 1,600 characters, since the capture records no text and the rate comes from the one committed `describe` body.
      - **Atomicity holds against the real provider** (exercise 20).
      - **The 401 echoes the key**, and the adapter held (**G71**).
    - **Register.** **G80** and **G81** opened. Notes on **G62**, **G65**, **G71**, **G73** and **G78**.
    - **A defect this commit's own verification run found, and fixed.** With a real `OPENAI_API_KEY` in `infra/.env`, which this issue's runbook asks for, two `tests/unit/test_settings.py` cases failed and pydantic's assertion diff printed the key. The key was rotated. That file's three `Settings` constructions now pass `_env_file=None` under an autouse fixture that deletes the four variables, so it depends on neither settings source. **G81** records the rest of the unit suite, which still reads that file.
    - **What this commit does not prove.** That the model follows `describe`'s instructions beyond what M6 checks, except over the three paragraphs the owner read (n=3). What OpenAI sends on any other day, for any other model or reasoning effort. Anything about surfaces larger than 12 members.

**Goal:** the actual product surface a user interacts with. FR-10.

**Rules for M8**, set at the M7→M8 boundary review (2026-09-18):

Widened 2026-09-19 by owner decision at this review's decisions phase.)*

  - ~~**M8.1's trace to the exit condition is not established.** It is a decision for this review's decisions phase, closing commit 3, as is what a Brief contains (FR-8).~~ *(Decided 2026-09-19: M8.1 traces to the widened condition. A Brief has four parts, `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` FR-8's 2026-09-19 note.)*
- **During M8, no issue is opened for register mechanics, checklist widening or a new `check_claims` check.** The rule forbids issues, not register entries. A `Blocks: internal` finding made during M8 gets its register entry as usual and no issue; an issue for it waits until the M8→M9 review.

  - Over `b81b3b8..454efde`, 13 register entries were opened and 1 was resolved.

**Execution order** (decided 2026-09-19; numbering unchanged): M8.8 → M8.1 → M8.5 → M8.6 → the three ports M8.2 consumes → M8.2 → M8.3 → M8.7 → M8.4.

Touches no file under `src/` and adds no check. The counting rule and the adjudication are in the review log row dated 2026-09-18.

M6.3 may choose to write a row earlier; if it does, it takes both of these with it. ***(Marked 2026-09-16, M6→M7 boundary: M6.3 did not, and **M7.2** may be forced to, depending on how it keys a `SecurityBrief` — **G68**. Whichever issue writes first takes both.)*** ***(Marked 2026-09-17, ADR-0033 decision 1: M7.2 is not forced to. - **M8.4 — Frontend: onboarding flow**
  Module: frontend · Depends on: M1.5, M2.3
  - Connect repo → confirm Security Context → trigger first scan, guided flow.

    - ADR-0031 decision 1 holds the access token in memory and sends it as a Bearer header.
    - `GET /auth/github/login` (`github_login`) requires that header and answers with a redirect, while a browser top-level navigation cannot attach an `Authorization` header. So the connect step cannot start as that route is written.
    - Read from the route; the browser half is not verified.
    - The options are to return the authorize URL as JSON, or to move to a cookie-based session, which changes `get_current_user_id`. Decide it here, by amendment to ADR-0031 or a new ADR (rule 16's credential-handling clause).
    - ***(Added 2026-09-16, from ADR-0031's P1.)* `POST /projects/` is unreachable through the rewrite.** This issue's scope does not name creating a project, but connecting a repository needs one to exist, so an onboarding flow that starts from nothing would call it. That is an inference, not something this bullet's scope states. Next strips the slash, and the API answers 307 to an absolute URL on its own origin. `skipTrailingSlashRedirect` was tested and does not fix it. **The bound, as measured:** it is the only one of 19 routes whose path ends in a slash, and none of the three `RedirectResponse` constructions derives its URL from the request. Decide the fix here; a route change enters rule 16's route clause. *(2026-09-19, M8.8: the count is **23** operations in `app.openapi()["paths"]`, after M7.2's two Brief routes and M8.8's two scan routes, and `POST /projects/` is still the only one ending in a slash. Neither new scan path ends in one, by ADR-0035 decision 1.)*

- **M8.8 — A route that starts a scan** — done
  Module: `scanning` · Depends on: M3.7 · Opened 2026-09-19 at the M7→M8 boundary review.
  - **Trace:** the exit condition's first clause, a user starts a scan. No route starts one today: `TriggerScanUseCase` is wired, and its only caller is the GitHub webhook. Carries **G70**. *(True when the issue opened; the route landed in commit 3, below.)*
  - **Decisions:** `docs/adr/0035-a-route-that-starts-a-scan.md`, written before the route code. Three commits:
    1. **Platform-wide, landed as `465d036`:** the database session is function-scoped, so every write route commits before its response is sent (**G86**, ADR-0008's amendment).
    2. **Documentation:** ADR-0035; the ADR-0022 and ADR-0017 amendments; `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` FR-4's qualification; **G87**, **G88** and **G89**.
    3. **Code.**
  - **The shape to deliver:**
    - `POST /projects/{project_id}/scans` → 202 with `{id, status}`, authorized by a new verdict, `ProjectAccessPort.may_manage_project`, which is owner-only. Every denial is one 404.
    - `GET /projects/{project_id}/scans/{scan_id}` → 200 with `{id, status, failure_reason}`, authorized by the read verdict. A scan of another project is 404.
    - `TriggerScanUseCase.execute(project_id, user_id, *, authorized)` raises one exception. The webhook passes `authorized=True` and keeps the owner as `triggered_by`.
    - The job is enqueued after the commit, through an after-commit hook in `get_db_session`.
    - `GitRepoCheckout` redacts its checkout directory from the failure message.
  - **Out of scope:** re-scan (M9.2), scan history (M8.2), onboarding (M8.4), a CI-hook adapter (cut by FR-4's qualification), a concurrency migration (**G88**), the frontend.
  - **Delivered** (commit 3), as shaped above:
    - **The routes.** `StartScanUseCase` and `GetScanUseCase`, behind a second router in `scanning`, mounted under `/projects`.
    - **The verdict.** `ProjectAccessPort.may_manage_project` over `projects/domain/authorization.may_manage`, implemented by `PostgresProjectAccessReader`.
    - **The ordering.** `AfterCommitJobQueue` over `platform/db.py`'s `after_commit`, wired by `get_job_queue`. `get_arq_pool` is now its own factory, so tests override the pool and keep the deferral.
    - **The redaction.** `GitRepoCheckout` redacts its checkout directory.
    - **The retry claims.** The nine src comments ADR-0035 decision 6 lists are corrected, and a tenth it missed: `normalize_scan.py`'s *"buys five wasted attempts"*, found by `architecture-guardian` on this commit.
    - **The rollback half of `after_commit`.** Two tests in `test_session_commit_precedes_response.py` drive the real `get_db_session`: callbacks run after the commit, and a rollback discards them.
    - **Tests.** `tests/integration/test_scan_routes.py`; `test_project_access_contract.py`, the first contract test holding `ProjectAccessPort`'s fake and real adapter to one verdict table; `tests/unit/test_start_scan.py`, `test_get_scan.py` and `test_project_authorization.py`; a rewritten `test_trigger_scan.py`. `InMemoryProjectAccess` moved to `tests/conftest.py`.
    - **Mutations, each applied and killed, with the file restored by sha256:**
      - `StartScanUseCase` asking the read verdict;
      - `may_manage` degraded to "a membership exists";
      - the enqueue inside the transaction;
      - no checkout-directory redaction (the route test kills it; the token redaction's kill is the adapter unit test only, since GitHub's stderr never echoes an env-borne token);
      - the GET without its project match;
      - the GET's verdict moved below its read;
      - the POST's denial mapped to 403;
      - `after_commit` callbacks run on the rollback path.
    - **What this does not change locally.** On a laptop with no GitHub connection, a scan of a connected GitHub repository fails at `GitHubConnectionNotFound`, which the GET now shows. `scripts/seed_demo_project.py` remains the demo path.

## Milestone 9 — Verification Loop (Week 13-14)

**Goal:** close the loop — fixing something and re-scanning demonstrably updates the Risk state.

- **M9.1 — Re-scan diffing**
  Module: `history` + `correlation` · Depends on: M8
  - When a new scan runs, previously open Risks are re-evaluated; resolved ones are marked with the evidence diff that justified it.
  - **Four things arrive together here, and they are listed in one place deliberately.** M4.5 did not ship any of them: it is project-scoped and scan-independent, so it had no scan-first query and, by ADR-0017's "an index without its query is a guess", no index either. Until this issue they were scattered across ADR-0019's Consequences, ADR-0020's Consequences and M4.5's roadmap bullet — three places, none of which is re-read at a boundary, which is the failure the working-style rule about Consequences sections exists to prevent. They are acceptance criteria, not notes:
    1. **The scan-first query** — "which findings were sighted in scan N". `FindingRepositoryPort` deliberately has none; every read it offers is finding-first or project-scoped.
    2. **`ix_finding_sightings_scan_id`, in the migration carrying that query** (ADR-0020). The sighting's primary key is `(finding_id, scan_id)`, so `scan_id` is not its leading column and the scan-first query is served by nothing today.
    3. **The absence check itself.** "Not sighted in scan N" is a missing row and "scan N was never normalized" is `normalization_runs`; ADR-0019 decision 1 guarantees those compose with no third state.
    4. **The succeeded-tools caveat that makes (3) correct** (ADR-0019's Consequences). "Not sighted in the latest scan" only means resolved for the tools that *succeeded* in that scan. Scoping by scan membership alone lets one failed Trivy silently resolve every dependency finding in the project — G4's failure shape, arriving one milestone later through a different door. Scope by `get_succeeded_by_scan_id`, which ADR-016 decision 2 already makes the pipeline's entry point.
  - **M4.5 exposes `last_seen_at` and deliberately claims nothing about resolution**, with a test asserting the response carries no `resolved`/`is_open`/`status` field. Anything this issue adds on top is the first thing in the system to make that claim, so it needs (4) before it needs anything else.
  - Also re-read here: **G13 and G14** (a `run_scan` retry that changes `ScanResult` rows after normalization completed), and **G11** (three tables depend on `scans`/`projects` never being deleted).

- **M9.2 — Frontend: verification UI**
  Module: frontend · Depends on: M9.1
  - "Re-scan" action, before/after evidence view on a resolved Risk.
  - **This issue fires ADR-0019 decision 5's only deferral, and it is recorded here because the ADR named M9.2 while M9.2 said nothing about it** — the same gap M4.5 closed for M9.1's four items, found by the M4→M5 boundary review's step 5. `Evidence` is **1:1 with the `Finding` and refreshed latest-wins, unconditionally**, so the database holds exactly one payload per finding: the most recent observation's. **A before/after view is the first consumer that needs a payload other than the current one, and today there is none to show.**
  - So this issue owns the decision ADR-0019 deferred to it: **per-sighting evidence retention**, which that ADR describes as additive rather than a rework — evidence keyed by `(finding_id, scan_id)` instead of `finding_id` alone. Two things to weigh, both already measured there: it is the "full per-scan snapshots" alternative ADR-0019 rejected **on cost, not principle**, storing the payload N times where N is the number of scans that sighted the finding; and `ScanResult.raw_output` is the floor beneath it — every past scan's full output is retained and addressable by scan, but recovering one past finding from it means re-parsing with per-tool knowledge, which is archaeology rather than a feature.
  - If the answer is "no retention", the UI has to say what it is actually showing — the current payload beside an absence, not a before/after — because a diff view built over one payload silently shows a comparison that did not happen.

## Milestone 10 — Security Hardening (Weeks 14-15)

**Goal:** Verion meets the security bar it recommends to others (dogfooding, per `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §11).

- **M10.1 — Threat model document**
  Module: docs · Depends on: all prior
  - Formal threat model for Verion itself (STRIDE or similar), covering the SSRF surface, secrets handling, and auth.

- **M10.2 — RBAC & rate limiting audit**
  Module: `identity`/`platform` · Depends on: M1
  - Verify enforcement at every endpoint, add rate limiting middleware.
  - **Inherits ADR-0031** (added 2026-09-16, M8.3 started early): requests from the frontend reach the API **through a Next rewrite**. Middleware keyed on the client address would see the frontend server for every browser, unless forwarded headers are set by the proxy and trusted by the API. Whether a rewrite adds `X-Forwarded-For` is recorded by ADR-0031's P1; trusting it is this issue's decision. ***(Recorded 2026-09-16: it does not.** Under both `next dev` and `next start`, the rewrite added `x-forwarded-host` and no `x-forwarded-for`, so there is no forwarded client address to trust. This issue has to add one at the proxy before any per-client limit can work.)*

- **M10.3 — Secrets management pass**
  Module: `platform` · Depends on: —
  - Ensure no secret ever touches logs/DB in plaintext; env-based secret injection.
  - **When logging is first configured, check `httpx2`'s INFO request log** (added 2026-09-15 by the M5→M6 boundary review). It logs the request URL, which for `GitHubAdapter.fetch_source_archive` is the codeload download link — a credential for a private repository. `src/` configures no logging today, so nothing is emitted, and the adapter's non-leak tests cover exception text only.

- **M10.4 — Security headers + dependency scanning on Verion itself**
  Module: `platform` · Depends on: —
  - Verion's own CI runs Trivy/Semgrep against its own repo — the dogfooding proof point.

## Milestone-boundary review

Run at **every milestone boundary**, timeboxed to **~45 minutes**. Reads the Deferred gaps register below as its main input.

**What the timebox covers** (decided at the M7→M8 boundary):
- Steps 1, 3 and 4 always run to completion.
- Steps 2, 5, 6 and 7 run until the box is spent.
- Whatever a step did not reach, and any step run only partly, is recorded as **not performed** for the exit ramp.

**Why this is scheduled rather than ad-hoc, stated honestly — because the case for it is narrower than the first pass makes it look.** The post-M3 review found seven documentation drifts, three latent bugs, and one critical-path blocker. Most of that does not recur:

- The **three bugs** were a one-time backlog flush, and were surfaced by *introducing* `mypy --strict` rather than by any step below. They existed because no type checker had ever run; it now runs on every commit and that class cannot accumulate again.
- The **doc drifts** are now largely automated by `scripts/check_claims.py`, whose blocking checks and non-blocking suppression report are enumerated in the script itself.
- The **critical-path blocker is the recurring value.** No per-commit gate can notice that M5 has become unbuildable — that takes stepping back and asking whether the next milestone's assumptions still hold in code.

So the review has shrunk to roughly its most valuable third. **That is the argument for scheduling it, not against it:** the expensive parts were automated away, and the part that remains is cheap and irreplaceable.

### The checklist

Steps 1–6 are ordered by observed value, not tidiness; step 7 is appended (its note says why). Steps 2 and 4 are mostly mechanical now.

1. **Do the next milestone's stated dependencies still hold in code?** Take its first two issues and trace each `Depends on:` to the code that must already exist. *This is the step that found M3.7, and the only step here that has ever found something no gate could.*
   - **While the M8.3 departure stands** (M8.3's 2026-09-16 marker), check its three end conditions: whether M8.2 is marked done, whether `frontend/` holds more than two page routes or a direct dependency outside ADR-0031 decision 3's list, and whether `ci.yml` has a frontend step. If any holds, the departure has ended, and that marker says what happens next.
   - **What this step cannot see as written** (recorded 2026-09-16, found through **G70**). It traces each issue's `Depends on:` field to code. A capability an issue assumes can instead sit in its bullet text, and then this step passes over it. M8.4's `Depends on: M1.5, M2.3` holds, in the sense that the code those issues ship exists — though M8.4's own "Inherits ADR-0031" bullet already records that its GitHub connect step cannot start as that route is written — while its bullet says "trigger first scan" and no route triggers a scan. M9.2's `Depends on: M9.1` has the same shape for its "Re-scan" action. That is a gap in the step, not in whoever ran it. *Recorded, not fixed: the step's wording is unchanged by this note.*
2. **Run `uv run python scripts/check_claims.py`, then review what it structurally cannot check.** *Scope is listed explicitly because the first execution of this checklist proved that leaving it implicit narrows it: the original wording named only code comments and time-stamped markers, so the sweep grepped for those, covered `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and never opened `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` or `README.md` — where three drifts were sitting, including a status line four milestones stale.*
   - **Files:** `CLAUDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `PRODUCT_SPEC.md`, `README.md`, `.claude/agents/`.
   - **Drift classes:** stale prose about implementation status; `TODO` / "as of M#.#" markers older than one milestone; broken or outdated internal cross-references (§ numbers, section names); and completion or status claims that outran reality.
   - *Found `vcs_provider.py`'s "GitHubAdapter doesn't implement these yet", three milestones stale; and, once the scope above was written down, `PRODUCT_SPEC.md`'s "see Section 12" pointing at Architecture Principles instead of Security Principles.*
   - When renumbering a numbered list in any of these files, grep for citations of the old numbers first — `§11.1` and `§11.5` are each cited by ADRs, so an insert would break a cross-reference while fixing one.
   - **The §-reference sweep: what it must cover, written down because this step reported "no broken §-references found" at the M4→M5 boundary and was wrong twice over.** *(Third time a step here has been widened by being run — after step 2's own scope in the post-M3.7 row and step 5's whole-document rule in the standalone row. Do not re-narrow it.)*
     - **Include `ROADMAP.md`, and specifically citations pointing *outward* from it into the two spec documents.** That is the class the failed sweep missed entirely: **36 of the 47 §-citations in the live docs — 77% — are in `ROADMAP.md`** *(an M4→M5 reading; at `3a89783`, `git grep -o -E '§[0-9]+'` finds 99 in `ROADMAP.md` of 116 across `CLAUDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` and `ROADMAP.md` — a token count rather than a citation count, so not a trend)*, and its file list did not include it. Re-running over that class found two wrong citations that had each survived M4.0's dedicated §-reference correction pass and the M3→M4 review.
     - **Match the backticked form**, because it is the only form this repo uses: citations are written `` `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 ``, so a pattern like `ARCHITECTURE.md §` matches nothing. Measured in one file, seconds apart: that pattern returns **0** hits in `ROADMAP.md` while the backticked one returns **12**.
     - **Flatten continuations before counting a phrase.** Comments and prose in this repository wrap across lines, so a line-based grep for a phrase undercounts, and nothing reports it. ADR-0031's *"Verified per ADR-009"* count reads 5 by line and 6 by package, because PyYAML's comment wraps; the line reading withdrew a correct finding for one round.
     - **Resolve every hit against the target document's actual headings** — an empty grep result is not a clean result, and reading it as one is the same sound-verification-disconnected-from-reality shape as G8's tool, G9's fixture and G19's generator, arriving inside this checklist.
     - Note `.claude/skills/` is outside this step's stated file list while carrying §-citations of its own. Checked ad hoc at the M4→M5 boundary and clean; add it to the list if it ever grows a second file.
3. **Review the Deferred gaps register** — anything at 3+ confirmations, and re-read every `Blocks-if-unresolved:` against the milestone now starting. A gap that was background debt last milestone may be critical path this one; that transition is the thing being watched for.
4. **Compare tracked metrics against the latest green CI run** — the `Tests (pytest …)` step duration and the suppression count — and record the new baseline in `CLAUDE.md`. *This is where the ±8s variance and the runtime concentration in a handful of container-bound tests surfaced.*
   - The step *duration* is available from the unauthenticated jobs API (`/actions/runs/<id>/jobs`), but the `--durations=10` per-test breakdown is only in the job **log**, which that API refuses without auth (403). Reading it needs `gh` or a **dedicated `actions:read`-only token** — not a reuse of the push credential. Until one exists, that half of this step needs the numbers pasted in by hand.
   - **While the M5.7 departure stands** (`CLAUDE.md`'s 2026-09-15 note), check whether the closing milestone added a container-bound scanner test. If it did, the departure has ended and M11.1's CI split moves to the next milestone.
5. **Spot-check the newest adapter against the Tier 2 ADRs, and re-read this milestone's own ADRs for undischarged deferrals.** ADR-011's nine subprocess points and ADR-013's gate placement for the adapter; for an adapter that sends data to a third party, ADR-0032 decision 6 (where the credential goes; failures that never carry the response body) and ADR-0034's prompt input boundary. CI cannot see these, and adapters are the only place they apply. *(The third-party clause was decided at the M7→M8 boundary: the step widened by being run.)* Then, for each ADR written or amended during the milestone just ending, read the whole document for a deferral carrying a trigger condition — they have turned up in Consequences, in Decision, and in Alternatives considered, so those three are where to look first, not where to stop — and confirm the trigger either has not fired or produced a register entry. Bounded on purpose — only the closing milestone's ADRs, so each is read at most once, at the boundary right after it was written. *Found ADR-012's Trivy-timeout deferral, whose named trigger fired in M3.7 and whose unvalidated number became an input to ADR-016's `job_timeout` arithmetic — recorded as G4. The "read the whole document" wording is itself a finding of that same pass: this step originally said "check its Consequences", which would have missed ADR-014's only trigger-carrying deferral, sitting in Alternatives considered. Do not re-narrow it to a section list.*
   - **While the M7.1 departure stands** (M7.1's 2026-09-17 marker), check two things: whether `tests/integration/test_openai_explanation_provider.py` replays a **captured** OpenAI response with its `usage` object whole, which ends the departure, and whether ADR-0032's Consequences carry that capture's token figures; and whether any `src/` code outside a test calls `ExplanationProviderPort.explain`, which makes the capture owed and requires the commit that added the call to say so. ***(ENDED 2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 3: the replay and ADR-0032's token figures both landed, so this sub-bullet is spent and kept as history. A new call to `explain` or `describe` outside a test no longer re-dates anything.)***
   - **What counts as a carrier** (decided at the M7→M8 boundary, applied from the next review). A carrier has two halves.
     - **WHO** is whatever names a specific issue: an ADR naming it, or a register entry's `Status: assigned`.
     - **WHAT** is that issue's own text carrying the obligation.
     - **One half alone is PARTIAL**, and is recorded naming the missing half.
   - **The cost of this step is verification, not reading, and the "~5 minutes" estimate measures the wrong half.** Timed at the M4→M5 boundary over six ADRs (25,191 words): **4m39s total, of which reading was 93s (33%) and verifying was 186s (67%)**. The expensive part is not reading the ADR — it is confirming each trigger's **carrier** still exists: that the named issue's roadmap entry actually carries what the ADR hands it, that a measurement a deferral rests on has something asserting it, that a claim about code is still true of the code. Both of that pass's findings came out of that half and neither was visible from the ADR text alone. **So estimate by the number of trigger-carrying deferrals, not by word count** — six ADRs produced roughly **24** of them. The usual caveat stands: an agent's read time is not a fair test of an estimate written for a human, but the 67/33 split and the trigger count are what transfer.
6. **Compare every ADR's description of a mechanical contract against the contract itself.** For each ADR that describes something CI enforces — the import-linter contracts, `mypy`'s scope, the Tier 1 gates, `check_claims` — diff what the ADR says the mechanism covers against `pyproject.toml`, `ci.yml` or the script. *Added at the M5→M6 boundary: ADR-0007 listed four framework-isolation packages while the contract forbids five, and no other step reads an ADR against its contract — step 2 excludes ADR bodies, and step 5 reads only the closing milestone's ADRs.*
7. **Does the closing milestone's rule set carry, lapse, or change?** For every rule scoped to the closing milestone — a `Rules for M<n>` section, or a rule whose own text names that milestone — record one of: *carries* (to where), *lapses* (why), or *changes* (to what). A milestone with no such rules records that. Search `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/PRODUCT_SPEC.md`, `.claude/agents/` and `docs/adr/`. **`docs/adr/` is on the list because an issue-scoped rule can live only in that issue's ADR:** ADR-0034 decision 5's *"Rule for this issue's tests"* was found by step 5 reading that ADR whole, not by this step. *Added 2026-09-16, after "Rules for M6" expired when M6 closed: the M6→M7 review measured those four rules without asking whether they continue, and no step here asked. Appended as 7 rather than placed by observed value, because steps are cited by number.*

### Keeping it from becoming box-ticking

A checklist that reports "all clear" every time is worse than no checklist. Two mitigations:

- **Every step produces a written artifact** — either a finding, or an explicit "checked X against Y, no change". Recorded in the review's commit message, and summarised as one row in the log below. "No change" is a valid result; silence is not.
- **The exit ramp, defined now rather than when it is needed.** If **two consecutive reviews** produce only "checked, no change" artifacts for **steps 1, 3, 5, 6 and 7** — meaning no finding, no new issue, and no register entry from any of those five, twice running — cut the cadence to every other milestone. A step recorded as **not performed** does not count as "checked, no change" and does not advance this test; only a step that was actually run and found nothing does. Record it as a new row in the log with `Cadence:` set to the new value, and say why in that review's commit message. Steps 2 and 4 are excluded from this test on purpose: they are mechanical and will keep passing, so including them would make the condition unreachable.

### Review log

One row per review, plus any standalone pass that re-runs part of the checklist outside a boundary — those say so in the Boundary column and mark every step they skipped as **not performed**, which per the exit-ramp rule above means they cannot advance it. **They are also skipped when identifying the last two rows** — otherwise inserting one would stall the ramp indefinitely rather than merely delaying it, since a row that cannot advance the test would permanently occupy one of the two slots it reads.

---

## Deferred gaps

Known gaps that are not yet anybody's issue. This exists because noting a gap in a commit message does not scale: **multi-scanner orchestration was correctly identified and correctly deferred three times (M3.4, M3.5, M3.6) and still became a critical-path blocker for M4 and M5 without anyone noticing.** It took a dedicated review pass to catch, and had it been missed, it would have surfaced at M8 with four milestones built on top.

**`Blocks-if-unresolved:` is the field that does the real work.** G1 was not missed because it went unrecorded — it was recorded three times, in increasing detail. It was missed because nobody wrote down *what it would break*, so its promotion from background debt to critical path was invisible. Fill that field in seriously: name the milestone and the concrete thing that breaks, not "may cause problems later". An entry whose `Blocks-if-unresolved:` is vague is an entry that will be re-read and skipped.

Rules, enforced by `scripts/check_claims.py` except where a rule says it is not:

- Entries use fixed fields so they can be parsed: `Confirmed:`, `Status:`, `Blocks-if-unresolved:`, and optionally `Deferral rationale:`.
- Add a milestone to `Confirmed:` each time the gap is re-encountered and deferred again.
- **At three or more confirmations**, an entry must carry either `Status: assigned → <issue>` or an explicit `Deferral rationale:`. Re-noting it a fourth time with neither fails CI. Three is the threshold because three is where this one broke: M3.4 and M3.5 were reasonable deferrals, but by M3.6 the repetition had become information nobody was acting on.
- Resolved entries stay here with `Status: resolved → <issue>`, as the record of how long it took and what it was blocking.
- An assignment is written `assigned → M<n>.<n>` at the start of the `Status:` value, and the id must be an issue heading in one of the milestones above. Leading bold, backticks or italics, a trailing qualifier such as `commit 4`, and a trailing full stop are accepted. A value that begins with the assignment keyword in any other form fails CI; it is not skipped.
- **Status terms, defined once, and every register count uses them.**
  - An *entry* is a `### ` heading in this section.
  - Its state comes from the `Status:` value on its `Confirmed:` line, compared case-insensitively after stripping leading `*`, `` ` ``, `_` and spaces: **resolved** if the value begins `resolved`, **assigned** if it begins `assigned`, **open** otherwise.
  - **Live** means open or assigned, i.e. not resolved, because an assignment does not spend a trigger. **Assigned is not open**; a count that merges the two says "live".
  - A count names the term it uses and the commit it was taken at.
  - *This is `check_fired_triggers_are_recorded`'s reading. `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` reads the same field differently, and a count taken on its reading disagrees with one taken on this — see **G58**'s 2026-09-16 note.*
  - *Not enforced: nothing requires a count to use these terms.* Measured on this definition at `b81b3b8` (commit 1 of the Rules-for-M6 promotion changes no entry): **67 entries — 14 resolved, 3 assigned, 50 open; 53 live.**
- An entry needs a labelled trigger with a bolded target, and a `Blocks-if-unresolved:`. An observation is not an entry. *(Not enforced: `check_fired_triggers_are_recorded` reads a labelled trigger but does not require one. Promoted 2026-09-16 from "Rules for M6", and applies to entries opened from that date. Existing entries are not retrofitted: at `b81b3b8`, **17 of 53 live** entries have no line matching `check_claims.TRIGGER_LABEL` (whose lookahead requires the bold) — 15 open (G4, G6, G7, G8, G11, G13, G14, G15, G19, G20, G39, G40, G43, G44, G45) and 2 assigned (G17, G18).)*
- **Every live entry carries `Kind: fact | owed`**, on the line after its `Confirmed:` line; an entry whose `Status:` begins `resolved` carries none, and commit 2's check applies only where `Status:` does not begin `resolved`. **owed**: you can name the action that would make the entry false, and it is within the project's scope. **fact**: naming that action requires either something the project has decided not to build, or a mechanism the entry records as not known to exist — and the entry cites that decision or that finding. Classify by the entry's SUBJECT, not by its current instance: an entry whose instance is discharged while its subject stands is not resolved (**G65**'s 2026-09-16 note). *(Enforced by `check_register_fields` since M8.0 commit 2.)*
- **Every `fact` carries `Reopens if: <condition>`** on its `Kind:` line — the event that would put the refused action back in scope, in the form **G62** already wrote as a trigger clause. *(Enforced by `check_register_fields` since M8.0 commit 2.)*
- **Every `owed` carries `Blocks: ship | internal`** on its `Kind:` line. **ship**: its absence stops the product being usable or deployable. **internal**: it costs the project and not the user. Distinct from `Blocks-if-unresolved:`, which says what breaks; this says who pays. *(Enforced by `check_register_fields` since M8.0 commit 2.)*
- **Every history line dated on or after 2026-09-18 carries `Confirms: <milestone> | none`** in its header, after the parenthetical: the occasion it appends to `Confirmed:`, or `none` when the line is a forecast, a correction, a cross-reference or an origin note. **Undated history lines, and lines dated before 2026-09-18, are legacy and unchecked.** *(Enforced by `check_register_fields` since M8.0 commit 2.)*

### G1 — Multi-scanner orchestration
Confirmed: M3.4, M3.5, M3.6 · Status: resolved → M3.7
Blocks-if-unresolved: M4 (the `Finding` schema and its per-scanner mappers would be designed without ever seeing Semgrep+Trivy+ZAP output for the same scan), M5 (`CorrelateFindingsUseCase` exists to link findings across *different* tools, and a `Scan` can only ever produce one)
Resolution: M3.7 — ADR-016. Three milestones between first confirmation (M3.4) and assignment, one issue between assignment and resolution. The `Blocks-if-unresolved:` field turned out to understate it: designing this properly also required a partial-failure model (`ScanResult.status`, `ScanStatus.PARTIAL`) and a home for per-project scanner configuration, neither of which was visible while the gap was recorded only as "orchestration is missing".

### G2 — `zap_target_url` is a per-integration value inside generic dispatch
Confirmed: M3.7, M5.4 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Deferral rationale: fixing it now means normalizing `scanner_configs` to `(project_id, tool)` for a second URL scanner that does not exist and that `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §9 puts in V2 — the pre-normalizing-for-a-hypothetical that ADR-016 decision 3 argues against, on the same reasoning ADR-014 used to reject a per-project webhook secret. Resolve on the trigger already stated there: **when a second tool needs tool-specific settings.**
Blocks-if-unresolved: any future `ScannerTargetKind.URL` scanner. `RunScanUseCase.execute` takes a single `zap_target_url: str | None` and routes it in the `else` branch of the `target_kind` check, so a second URL-kind adapter is silently handed **ZAP's** target rather than its own. It fails silently — the scanner runs, succeeds, and produces a real `ScanResult` for the wrong target, which M4 then normalizes and M5 correlates as if it were that tool's view of the system. Nothing raises, and no test today would catch it.
Note (M5.4): re-confirmed rather than resolved. ADR-0024 decision 1 rules that this entry's exit condition counts **tools**, not settings, so adding active-scan consent to `ScannerConfig` does not fire it; that decision also records what the re-confirmation costs — there are now two per-integration values rather than one, kept out of `RunScanUseCase.execute`'s signature by its decision 4.
Note: `ScannerPort.target_kind` itself is fine — dispatch's *shape* is generic and carries no `tool == "zap"` branch (rule 4). What is per-integration is the *value*, in the use case's signature. Recorded here and not only in ADR-016 because prose has no `Blocks-if-unresolved:` field and no escalation check, which is the exact lesson G1 above is the record of.

### G3 — CI action majors are behind, and running on a runtime they don't declare
Confirmed: post-M3.7, post-M4 · Status: **resolved → M5.0**
Resolution: M5.0. All four actions in `ci.yml` are now pinned to a full commit SHA with the resolved tag in a trailing comment — `actions/checkout@3d3c42e5…` (v7.0.1), `astral-sh/setup-uv@20cfd1bf…` (v10.0.1), `actions/cache@55cc8345…` (v6.1.0), `aquasecurity/setup-trivy@81e51434…` (v0.3.1, unchanged and still upstream's newest). Every SHA resolved with `git ls-remote` against the upstream repository; every runtime confirmed by reading **`runs.using` in that tag's own `action.yml`**, not the release notes. *(The method is right and the sentence that originally justified it was **invented** — it claimed checkout v6's and v7's release bodies "read as though they were node20 while their manifests say node24". Fetched afterwards: v7.0.0 and v7.0.1 mention Node nowhere at all, and v6.0.0's only Node line is "Update README to include Node.js 24 support details and requirements", which is the opposite. What happened is that a summarizer's paraphrase of those release notes was written down as a verified fact — **ADR-0009's own failure mode, committed inside the commit whose thesis is that release prose is not a primary source.** The claim is removed rather than softened; the method stands on its own, since a manifest is the thing the runner executes and a release body is not.)* **SHA pinning became the house style rather than `setup-trivy`'s exception**, settled once for all four: `setup-uv` publishes no floating tag above `v7` so it must be pinned exactly regardless, and one file with two conventions has no rule saying which applies to the next action added. The convention now lives as a comment at the top of `steps:`, which is where somebody adding an action reads; deliberately not an ADR (ADR-0009 already governs the verification, and the pinning *form* is one sentence with no live alternative) and deliberately not a sixteenth numbered rule in `CLAUDE.md`, since `check_claims.check_rule_count_is_in_sync` couples that count to `architecture-guardian.md`. Promote it if a second workflow file ever exists.
Annotation reading (the criterion, since a green CI is not one — the pre-upgrade run was already green *with* the annotation): **PASS, and by more than the criterion asked for.** Before, run `32715167778` / check run `97394742310`: `annotations_count: 3` — (1) "Node.js 20 is deprecated. The following actions target Node.js 20 but are being forced to run on Node.js 24: `actions/cache@v4`, `actions/checkout@v4`, `astral-sh/setup-uv@v3`", (2) "Failed to save: `<h2>Our services aren't available right now</h2>`…", (3) "Failed to restore: Cache service responded with 400". After, commit `713a50e`, run `32739032131` / check run `97468754466`: **`annotations_count: 0`**, and the annotations endpoint returns an empty array — verified directly rather than inferred from the count. **No annotation survives, cache ones included.** Corroborated by the step outcomes in the same run: `Cache Trivy vulnerability DB` (restore), `Post Cache Trivy vulnerability DB` (save) and `Post Install uv` (setup-uv's own cache save) all report `success`, where the first two were the sources of (3) and (2).
**Correction (2026-08-24, M5.0): the targets this entry named were wrong, not merely incomplete, and the sentences below are rewritten rather than annotated** — the treatment the M4→M5 boundary review gave G4's falsified 4-6s claim, and for the same reason: a note beside a superseded number leaves both in the entry and a reader arriving at "v5" has no reason to scroll. This entry said `actions/checkout` v4→**v5** and `actions/cache` v4→**v5**. Verified against the upstream repositories at M5.0, checkout's newest is **v7.0.1** (v5.1.0, v6.1.0 and v7.0.1 all exist) and cache's is **v6.1.0**. Both stated targets did satisfy the node24 trigger, so the entry was not wrong about the *fix* — it was wrong about the *distance*, and shipping two majors of known debt in the commit that closes it is how the next G3 gets opened. `setup-uv`'s v10 was correct. **This is ADR-0009 applied to a register entry rather than to a dependency:** a version number recorded at a past boundary is an assertion with an expiry date, and "it is what the entry says" is not evidence.
Assignment (post-M4 boundary review): **this entry is why M5.0 exists.** Re-read against M5 and found not to be critical path for correlation's *content* — and assigned anyway, on the one property that distinguishes it from every other entry in this register: **its trigger fires without anybody doing anything.** G2 waits on a second URL scanner, G6 on somebody editing the ruleset, G16 on a mapper changing how it truncates, G19 on somebody writing a seed script; each is preceded by a person acting, so each can be noticed. G3 waits on GitHub removing a compatibility shim, on no schedule visible from here, and when it fires **all of CI fails at once** — which makes every M5 issue's definition of done unsatisfiable until it is fixed, under exactly the time pressure the rationale below says this work should not be done under. A milestone boundary is also the only point in this cadence where a non-feature issue is not bundling. G12 is assigned to the same issue because the work is the same class; the argument for the issue is this entry alone.
Deferral rationale: found while removing a dead `python-version` input from `setup-uv`, and deliberately not bundled into that fix. Three actions' majors — `actions/checkout` (v4→v7.0.1), `actions/cache` (v4→v6.1.0) and `astral-sh/setup-uv` (v3→v10.0.1) — is real behaviour-change surface, seven setup-uv majors alone, including changes to cache handling and new `python-version`/`activate-environment` inputs that could change which interpreter CI runs. That deserves its own verification pass rather than riding along inside a one-line false-claim fix. *(The two `v5`s in this sentence were corrected to the actual newest majors at M5.0 — see the Correction above.)*
Blocks-if-unresolved: **all of CI, at once and without warning.** Until M5.0 every run annotated "The following actions target Node.js 20 but are being forced to run on Node.js 24: `actions/cache@v4`, `actions/checkout@v4`, `astral-sh/setup-uv@v3`" — verified against the annotations API on run `32715167778` (check run `97394742310`), which carried it as one of three warnings while reporting `success`. They were already executing on a runtime they did not declare; the only thing holding that up was GitHub's compatibility shim. Had it been removed, all three would have failed and no workflow would have run. Newer majors declaring `node24` already existed for all three, so this was ours to do rather than something that resolved by waiting.
Note: `setup-uv` stopped publishing floating major tags after v7 — `v8`/`v9`/`v10` do not resolve, only exact versions such as `v10.0.1`. **Confirmed at M5.0 by `git ls-remote`** (`refs/tags/v7` exists and nothing above it) and explained by v8.0.0's own release body, *"Remove update-major-minor-tags workflow"*. `actions/checkout` and `actions/cache` do still publish floating majors; they are SHA-pinned anyway, because the convention is now the file's rather than one action's.
