Note (M5.1): **the trigger "M5.1's signal choice" has fired, and the outcome takes this entry off M5's path without closing it.** ADR-0023's amendment, Decision B, puts `cwe` **out** of the correlation match key — citing this entry as one of its grounds — so no deterministic selection rule is needed for a signal correlation does not read. *("one of **four** grounds" until 2026-08-25; Decision B was re-founded on three when the active-scan probe falsified two of the original four. **This entry survives the rewrite as ground (3) of three**, which makes it more load-bearing than when this note was written, not less — and sharper still on the measure that matters to whoever closes **G6**: of the grounds that survive *resolving G6*, this entry is now **one of two rather than one of three** — and it is still the only ground that can be retired by a measurement anybody could take in an afternoon, which is what its own `Deferral rationale:` promises.)* **The entry stays open, and its `Blocks-if-unresolved:` is unchanged in the half that matters**: M6 still reads `cwe` as a scoring input under rule 5, and `"cwe"` is still in `_REFRESHED_COLUMNS`, so a `CweIDs` reordering still rewrites stored rows with nothing recording that it did. What has changed is only that the first of the two consumers this entry names — M5.1's signal choice — no longer applies; M6's use of `cwe` does. (The third bullet above, "What ADR-0018 decision 4 actually assumed", is an assumption this entry corrects, not a consumer.) Re-read at **M6**, or at the next Trivy version bump, as the entry already says.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **the trigger "or M6" has fired, and the outcome retires the second of the two consumers this entry was opened for.** ADR-0005 decision 4 declines `cwe` as a scoring input, citing this entry as a ground: an input a vulnerability-database update can rewrite with nothing recording it is untraceable, which is rule 5. So *"M6's use of `cwe`"* in `Blocks-if-unresolved:` above is now wrong, exactly as the first bullet went wrong at M5.1 — **both named consumers are retired and the entry is still open**. What keeps it open is what this entry always measured and no decision has changed: `"cwe"` remains in `_REFRESHED_COLUMNS`, so a `CweIDs` reordering still rewrites stored rows silently, and the value still reaches a user through `normalization`'s findings API. The cheap exit in `Deferral rationale:` — map the committed `trivy_scan.json` against two DB versions — is unchanged and is now the *only* way this entry closes, since no consumer's design decision can retire it further.

### G27 — ADR-016 decision 1's snapshot coherence excludes ZAP by construction, so nothing asserts that the scanned URL serves the scanned tree
Confirmed: M5.1 · Status: resolved → M5.6 *(was `assigned → M5.6 commit 4` from M5.6 commit 3, and `M5.5 (declaration) + M5.6 (conditioning)` before that — see the 2026-09-15 Note)*
Resolution: M5.6 commit 4 (2026-09-15). **Both halves, and on an end-to-end behaviour rather than a unit suite**, which commit 3's Note made the condition for resolving.
- **The declaration half** shipped at M5.5 and is unchanged.
- **The conditioning half is now true of production.** Two tests in `tests/integration/test_derived_group_end_to_end.py` run a real context build through the real router, which stores the route map, and then the real Risk listing, with no route-map override:
  - `test_a_built_context_produces_the_cross_tool_group_while_the_declaration_is_in_force` — one group at `/calculate` holding the Semgrep and ZAP findings.
  - `test_the_same_built_map_produces_no_group_once_the_declaration_is_out_of_force` — the same stored map, with the declaration voided by repointing the target, and no group.
  - Physically mutating the gate turns the second red, and a reader that never reads the stored map turns the first red. The M5.6 block carries both mutation tables.
- **What this resolution does not claim.**
  - The module serves the committed capture through `MockTransport`, so it proves the pipeline and not GitHub's archive format, which stays with the adapter's real-capture tests.
  - A green derived group still means ADR-0028 decision 2's *declared and not reconfigured since*, never *these two findings are about one system* (**G47**).
  - The map is re-derived at the tip (**G52**), keyed on a framework from a separate read (**G56**), and effectively written once per project (**G55**).
  - None of those is this entry's subject, and none reopens it.
Split (2026-09-09, ADR-0028 decision 0): **this entry is SPLIT into two halves with different owners, and it is deliberately not a reassignment.** A reassignment would move the whole entry to M5.6 and leave M5.5 owning nothing this register records; a split says that both halves are required and that landing one does not discharge the entry. **The declaration half is M5.5's** — the record, its voiding rule, and `projects`' read surface. **The conditioning half is M5.6's** — a derived SAST↔DAST group withheld when the declaration is absent, written as an acceptance criterion at that issue's own block. **This entry stays open until both land**, and a reader who sees only the M5.5 commit must not read it as discharged; that is why the split is in `Status:` rather than only in this note. **Why the assignment below could not be satisfied as written:** its criterion was set 2026-08-25, one day before ADR-0023's amendment froze the match key's three fields and M5.8 implemented the matcher — and `SIGNAL_FIELDS` is `("package", "url")`, which `mappers/semgrep.py` populates neither of — and, the half a compressed version of this argument drops, `mappers/trivy.py` populates `package` and never `url` while `mappers/zap.py` does the reverse, which is what excludes Trivy↔ZAP. So no pair of tools can co-group, and a gate written at M5.5 would be green whether wired in or not. **Neither `Blocks-if-unresolved:` nor `Deferral rationale:` below is rewritten**, and they are in different positions rather than both holding in full: the first holds unchanged, and its second bullet is precisely what M5.6 now owns. The second opens *"there is nothing to fix until somebody decides what the system should assert, and that decision is not this milestone's"* — **that clause is discharged by ADR-0028**, which is that decision, taken inside M5; what survives of the field is the direction it names and the alternatives it prices, which M5.5 and M5.6 now build. **One clause of that same field is also over-scoped** — *"the `Server` banner that ADR-0023 Decision A just refused"*, which refused it **as a `Location` correlation signal**; see ADR-0028 decision 2 for the scope.
Assignment (2026-08-25, active-scan probe): **the substance of this entry is unchanged — every sentence below still holds and none is corrected — but what it blocks has changed, and that is what moves it from open to assigned.** It was recorded as a condition on *correlation*: the route-to-line derivation and the banner match both assume the URL and the tree are the same system. **It is now on the critical path for `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10's demonstration goal**, which is a different and larger thing. The mechanism of the change is entirely in **G24**: while the scan plan was passive-only, no DAST finding about the sink existed, so this entry's *validity* condition had nothing to be a condition on — ADR-0023 Decision C names exactly that asymmetry ("resolving G27 alone yields a valid path with nothing to put through it"). The probe measured the finding into existence. **So this entry is now the only thing standing between a real SAST↔DAST pair and a §10 demonstration that could be confidently wrong** — and note which way that cuts: G24's resolution makes this entry *more* urgent, not less, because a correlation that is produced and wrong is worse than one that cannot be produced. That is this entry's own second bullet, arriving as a live risk rather than a forecast. Assigned to **M5.5**, whose deliverable is the declaration this entry's `Deferral rationale:` already names, with the conditioning — not merely the field — as the acceptance criterion.
Blocks-if-unresolved: **every cross-tool correlation involving DAST — and both available routes to one, for the same reason, which is why this is one entry rather than a caveat on two.** ADR-016 decision 1 exists to make cross-tool correlation well-founded: one job, one checkout, every scanner against the same tree, so that "both tools observed the same tree" holds by construction rather than by luck. That guarantee covers `REPO_PATH` scanners. **ZAP is dispatched by `target_kind` to a URL and never reads the checkout at all**, so the premise M5 relies on is established for two tools of three, and the third is the DAST half of the product's central claim. Two consequences, and the second is the dangerous one:
- **The banner match** — deriving `(package, installed_version)` from ZAP's `Server` header and comparing it against Trivy's manifest pin — is only true if the deployed server is the scanned tree. Refused on independent grounds by ADR-0023's Decision A, so this entry is not what blocks it, but the coupling is the reason a *truthful* banner would still be comparing two systems.
- **The route-to-line derivation** (ADR-0023 Decision C) depends on the identical coupling and **is the less visible of the two**. Its route-path-to-view-function stage — mapping `/calculate` to the `@app.route`-decorated function whose line span contains Semgrep's finding — assumes the route ZAP crawled is defined in the tree Semgrep read. **No version string betrays that assumption.** The banner case at least presents as a version comparison somebody might question; here the derivation simply produces a group, and a group produced from two different systems looks exactly like a group produced from one.
Evidence — and note that **ADR-016 does not overclaim.** Its own wording is already correctly scoped, and the gap is that *nothing states the exclusion as a limit on DAST correlation*, so M5's design reads a correctly-scoped guarantee as a general one:
- Decision 1 says a single job "checks out once and hands the same path to **every repo-based scanner**". Its Consequences names the pair it covers: "correlating a Semgrep location against a Trivy dependency is well-founded". ZAP appears in neither sentence.
- `run_scan.py`'s `_run_scanner` branches on `ScannerTargetKind.REPO_PATH` for `local_path` and takes `zap_target_url` in the `else`. Its `execute` performs the checkout only when some enabled scanner declares `REPO_PATH` — which decision 4 states as a feature in its own closing line: "a ZAP-only project never clones the repository at all."
- `test_multi_scanner_dispatch.py::test_one_trigger_produces_results_for_three_real_tools`'s docstring: ZAP "is the only URL-kind scanner", which is exactly why that test includes it.
- In production `zap_target_url` is per-project configuration pointing at a deployed environment — typically staging, which deploys from *some* commit and rarely the one just pushed. **The mismatch is the normal case, not a corner case.**
Deferral rationale: **there is nothing to fix until somebody decides what the system should assert**, and that decision is not this milestone's. The direction is named and deliberately not designed here: **an explicit per-project declaration that this URL serves this repo**, so a person signs the claim and correlation between tree-derived and URL-derived findings is conditioned on it. The alternatives are worse in stated ways — verifying it automatically means trusting the `Server` banner that ADR-0023 Decision A just refused, and recording it (a commit on `Scan`, per **G25**) makes the coupling *checkable* without making it *true*. Trigger: **the first issue that would correlate a DAST finding with a tree-derived one** — M5.3 if its proof artifact claims one — or any change to `zap_target_url`'s configuration surface.
Note: **distinct from G2, G24 and G25 in the G9/G10 manner**, and the four-way separation is stated so folding any pair later is an act rather than a tidy-up. **G2** is dispatch *shape* — the per-integration *value* sitting in the use case's signature, whose failure is a second URL scanner silently receiving ZAP's target. **G24** is the scan *plan* — passive-only, so no injection finding exists to correlate. **G25** is *which tree* — no commit on `Scan`, whose failure is cross-scan and lands on M9.1. **This is whether the URL and the tree are the same system at all**, whose failure is cross-tool and lands inside a single scan. Four different `Blocks-if-unresolved:` fields; none implies another.
Note: **shares one property with G24, and the comparison is against G23 rather than against the register — no corpus discharges it.** Most entries here are not corpus-discharged and never claimed to be (G25 is a missing domain field, G26 a mapper selection rule, G6 a ruleset edit); what makes this worth saying is the neighbourhood it was found in. G23 *was* fixed by capturing, and G24, G25 and G27 all surfaced from that same capture and look at first glance like more of the same. G24 and G27 are not: no corpus however well captured discharges either. Worse, **this corpus has the coupling by construction and that is exactly why it is invisible**: `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md` records the app served out-of-process *from the checkout itself* on the target's own pinned closure, so the one configuration in which the assumption holds is the only one anybody has measured.
Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3): **`Status:` moves to `M5.6 commit 4`, and this entry is deliberately NOT resolved.**
- **The declaration half** shipped at M5.5 and is unchanged.
- **The conditioning half is in code** at commit 3:
  - `CorrelateFindingsUseCase.execute` reads `ServingDeclarationPort`.
  - It does not attempt the derivation unless the declaration is in force.
  - `test_correlate_findings.py` pins that with an ordering test and a withheld/produced pair. The ordering test uses a route map that raises when read.
- **Production produces no derived group until commit 4.** Production's `RouteMapPort` is `EmptyRouteMapReader`, and ADR-0029's 2026-09-15 amendment puts population at Security Context build time, in commit 4.
  - *(Dated 2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4: no longer true. `EmptyRouteMapReader` is deleted, production reads a map stored at context build through `PostgresRouteMapReader`, and this entry is resolved — see `Resolution:` above.)*
- **Resolving the entry now would close it on a unit suite rather than on a behaviour.** If commit 4 slipped, the register would claim a capability the product does not have.
- **G48 is the precedent.** It stayed open until its port arrived. A gap between commits of one issue differs in size, not in kind.
- **Resolve at commit 4.** That commit flips `test_risks_routes.py::test_a_declared_project_gets_no_derived_group_until_the_route_map_is_populated`, deliberately.
  - *(Done 2026-09-15, and not quite as predicted. The flip is `test_derived_group_end_to_end.py`'s first test, where a detect runs and the group appears. The old test is renamed `..._with_no_built_route_map_gets_no_derived_group` and kept, because its narrower case still holds: a declared project with no stored map derives nothing, and now reads `NOT_BUILT`.)*


### G28 — `architecture-guardian` is scoped to the diff, so a pre-existing sentence a diff FALSIFIES WITHOUT TOUCHING is outside any diff-scoped pass by construction
Confirmed: M5.1, active-scan probe, M5→M6 boundary · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the class of defect a review step cannot see is the class nobody looks for — and this one was demonstrated at full scale before it was named.** M5.1's documentation commit ran **eight** guardian passes returning **46** findings. Every pass was given the working-tree diff. None of the first seven looked at `ROADMAP.md`'s Milestone 5 **Goal** line, which read *"related findings **across tools** become a single candidate Risk"* — the single sentence the commit's entire measurement falsifies, and the first thing a reader of that milestone sees. It was found only when a pass was asked to check for internal inconsistency *anywhere in the repo* rather than in the diff. The mechanism is structural, not inattention: **a diff shows what changed, and this defect is a sentence that did not change while the world around it did.** `git diff` cannot surface it, so neither can any reviewer or agent reading a diff.
Evidence: the Goal line predates M5.0 and was untouched by every commit in M5.1. The falsifying content — "what M5 actually delivers is INTRA-tool grouping" in M5.1, and "cannot honestly demonstrate cross-tool correlation" in M5.3 — sits further down the same file, in the two issues the Goal introduces. Seven passes read both without reading the sentence above them, because only the additions were in scope.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is a habit with a trigger, and the trigger is what makes it more than "look harder".** The candidate is a step in the guardian's instructions and in the milestone-boundary review: *when a change falsifies a claim, grep the repo for the claim's subject — not for the diff's contents — and read every hit.* For this commit that would have been `grep -n "across tools" docs/`, one command. It is deferred rather than done because changing how the guardian is invoked is a change to a review step this issue does not own, and because the trigger needs stating precisely enough to be checkable: it fires on any change that **falsifies or narrows a claim**, which is a judgement, not a file pattern. Trigger: **the next milestone-boundary review**, or the next commit whose stated purpose is to record a measurement that changes what an earlier document asserted.
Note (M5.1): **the prescribed sweep was run once, in the commit that opened this entry, and it immediately found a second instance no pass had reached — which is the entry working rather than a new gap.** `grep -rn "across tools" docs/ src/ README.md` returns, among correct hits, **`README.md`'s "Core differentiators" list**: *"**Evidence-backed correlation** — related findings **across tools** (e.g. SAST + DAST on the same endpoint) are grouped into one traceable risk."* Present tense, and it names as its example precisely the pair **G24** makes unobtainable and **G27** makes unvalidatable. It is the same defect as the Milestone 5 Goal line one file over, and the eighth pass explicitly reported the Goal line as "the only place in the repo … still stating M5's deliverable as cross-tool", having checked `README.md` — so a diff-scoped review missed it twice, once by scope and once by reading.

**A ninth pass, given the repo rather than the diff, then returned eight more instances of the same class — which is the measurement this entry exists to record, and it is larger than the entry's opening estimate.** The full list, so the next sweep starts from an enumeration rather than a phrase:
- `README.md`'s "Core differentiators" bullet (above), and **`README.md`'s opening line**: *"unifies signals from existing security tools … **correlates evidence across sources**"*. The second is unreachable by the grep that found the first — it says "sources", not "tools" — which is the practical form of this entry's own instruction to grep the *subject* rather than a phrase.
- `src/verion/modules/normalization/domain/cwe.py`'s module docstring: *"**Correlation compares these across tools**, so they have to be the same string"* — falsified by ADR-0023 Decision B, which puts `cwe` out of the match key. **This was a hit of the very grep the paragraph above reports running, and it was passed over as a correct hit.** A sweep is only as good as the reading of its output.
- `tests/unit/test_finding.py`'s canonicalisation test docstring, carrying the same claim.
- **Three restatements of ADR-016 decision 1 that overclaim where the ADR itself does not** — `CLAUDE.md`'s Tier 2 table (*"one checkout, **every scanner** against that same tree"*), `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s ADR-016 summary (*"scanners concurrent against a single checkout"*), and `run_scan.py`'s own comment above the `gather` (*"One checkout, N scanners, all against that same tree"*) — the last sitting directly above the branch that routes ZAP to `zap_target_url`. **G27** says ADR-016 does not overclaim, and that is true of the ADR and false of every summary of it.
- `run_scan.py`'s retry rationale: *"a **fresh-checkout ZAP result**"* — on G27's terms a ZAP result comes from no checkout at all.
- `ADR-0018`'s ZAP evidence-byte figures (`8,523 → 18,148` on "the real fixture"), a third unmarked copy of a pre-G23 measurement whose siblings in `ADR-0019` and M4.2 were marked.

**None of these are edited here.** Four are under `src/` or `tests/`, which the commit that found them declared out of scope; `README.md` is product voice, where softening a stated differentiator is a product decision; and `CLAUDE.md`'s Tier 2 row is a rule description, which is not a thing to amend as a side effect of a measurement commit. Recorded so each is a decision somebody takes rather than something a sweep found and nobody wrote down. **That the list is this long is the entry's argument, not an embarrassment to it**: eight diff-scoped passes over the same change returned none of them.
Note: **same category as the boundary review's citation sweep, and the comparison is the argument for registering it.** That sweep reported "no broken references" because its *file list* was short — a check whose incompleteness reads exactly like a clean result. This is the same shape one level up: a review whose *scope* is the diff reads exactly like a review of the repo, and returns findings either way, so nothing in its output says what it could not see. Both are holes in a review step rather than defects in any artifact, which is why neither is discoverable by re-reading the artifact.
Note: **distinct from G19 and G20, which are its neighbours in the "sound verification, disconnected from reality" family.** G19 is a verification invalidated by a *generator*; G20 is a documented measurement nothing *asserts*; this is a verification whose *scope* excludes the defect. G20 is the closest — both are about claims drifting out of true, and two of G20's stale figures (ADR-0021's and `finding.py`'s) were themselves found outside any diff, by the same widening that found this. What separates them is the fix: G20's is a test over fixtures, asserting a property of committed data; this one's is a search habit, and no test can express it.

Note (2026-08-25) — **a second instance of this entry's class, found in a mechanical check rather than in prose, and measured rather than characterised.** `scripts/check_claims.py`'s `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` — the guard that enforces this register's own three-confirmation rule — matches `Status:\s*(assigned|resolved)\s*→`. It has never rejected anything, and **the reason is that the input that would break it has not arrived, not that the register conforms.** That is the same shape as a sweep whose pattern list is incomplete: the output is identical either way, and nothing in it says what it could not see.

The measurement, in numbers because "some entries use a different form" is exactly the characterisation this entry exists to refuse. **Stated at two points, because this commit changes them and a present-tense count in a commit that moves it is the defect one level down** — G20's "the number moved, the property held" treatment:

| | at `HEAD`, where this was found | in the tree this commit produces |
|---|---|---|
| register entries | 28 | **29** (G29 added) |
| entries whose `Status:` claims an assignment or resolution | 10 | **13** |
| … of those, readable by the guard | 5 — G1, G5, G17, G18, G23 | **8** — plus G24, G27, G29 |
| … unreadable | 5 — G3, G9, G10, G12, G19 | **5**, unchanged |
| would pass at threshold via `Deferral rationale:` alone | 23 | **21** |
| would fail outright | 0 | **0** |

- **Two independent causes, not one.** G3, G12 and G19 wrap the value in bold — `**resolved → M5.0**`, `**assigned → M5.3**` — and the `**` sits between `Status:` and the keyword, which `\s*` does not match. G9 and G10 use a **different grammar entirely**: `**RESOLVED (M4.4)**`, uppercase and parenthesised, **with no arrow at all** — so unbolding and lowercasing them would still not make them readable. A fix aimed only at the bold would close three of five.
- **The assignment branch has never decided an outcome for any entry except G1**, and the three readable entries this commit adds do not change that, since all three are at one confirmation.
- **Only G1 has ever reached three confirmations, and it is on the readable side** — which is precisely why none of this has been visible. **Eight entries sit at two**, one confirmation from being tested: G3, G4, G6, G8, G12, G15, G19, G20 — **three of those eight (G3, G12, G19) are in the unreadable set.**
- **G1 is the one entry that does NOT carry a `Deferral rationale:`** — it carries `Resolution:`, being resolved rather than deferred; 28 of 29 carry the field. That single exception is load-bearing: **the injection experiment below works only because G1 lacks it**, so the entry that breaks the pattern is the one the proof depends on.

**The guard itself works — proved by injection, not assumed**, on the M0.2 / ADR-010 precedent this project uses for exactly this. Bolding G1's `Status:` in the working tree and running `uv run python scripts/check_claims.py` reports that gap `G1 — Multi-scanner orchestration` *"has been confirmed 3 times (threshold 3) but carries neither … nor 'Deferral rationale:' — re-noting it again is not a decision"*, and exits **1**; reverting restores `4 checks passed`, exit 0. **So the finding is coverage, not correctness.**

**The quotation above is elided in two places, and both elisions are this entry's own subject.** **Quoting the checker's failure message verbatim makes the checker's own regex match G28**, whose `Status:` is `open` — the message hands back the field name it wanted, and that string is the pattern. So the field name is described rather than reproduced; it was caught by running the parser over this file, not by reading it. **And the message's `docs/ROADMAP.md:<line>` prefix is dropped because this commit moved that line by 45** — G1's heading is at 556 in `HEAD` and 601 here — so a citation copied from the tool's output would have been broken by the diff that wrote it, in the same file, before ever being committed. **This was got wrong twice**: the first draft quoted it verbatim, and the correction for that re-introduced the literal string in the sentence explaining the elision. Both were caught by running the parser rather than by reading, which is this entry's whole argument in miniature. Both are recorded rather than quietly fixed, because an entry about checks that pass for the wrong reason has no standing to tidy away two of its own.

**The sharper result, and the reason this is worth registering rather than noting.** Raising G19 to three confirmations with its `**assigned → M5.3**` left bolded **passes** — and removing that entry's `Deferral rationale:` in addition makes it **fail**. So the bolded assignment is not merely unexercised, it is **inert**: G19 is carried entirely by a fallback asserting the *opposite* claim. "Assigned to M5.3" and "deferred with a rationale" are contradictory statements about the same gap, and on **23 entries at `HEAD` and 21 in the tree this commit produces** — the table's own two columns — the guard is reading the second. The concrete failure that sets up: **once a gap is assigned, its `Deferral rationale:` is obsolete by definition** — it explains why the work was not done, and the work now has an issue — so deleting it is the natural tidy-up, and doing that to G3, G12 or G19 makes the entry **fail CI for being correct**. The converse is the same defect: a future entry written with only a bolded assignment fails at its third confirmation for a formatting reason its author cannot see in the message.

Note (2026-08-25, second sweep) — **ten more sites of this entry's class, in nine bullets — and they did not all come from the sweep, which is the point rather than a caveat.** They came from **four** different places, and the split is the most useful thing in this note:
  - **Five from the prescribed sweep**, run over the claims this change falsifies rather than over the diff's contents, per this entry's own instruction. The subjects grepped were `no corpus can`, `no corpus ever will`, `passive-only`, `response-level`, `bounds ZAP`, `strict subset`, `can never report`, `five alerts` — and they hit only `docs/` and `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md`.
  - **Two from this entry's own earlier enumeration**, not from any sweep: `README.md`'s Core differentiators bullet, already listed in the note above, and `zap_adapter.py`'s `_build_plan_yaml` comment, already cited in G24's `Evidence:`. **Neither matches any of the eight subjects** — the adapter comment says "spider + passive scanning only", not `passive-only`, and `README.md` says "across tools". They are re-listed because this change gives each a *new* status, not because a search found them.
  - **Two from a gate run**, when `ruff format --check .` reported 309 files against a documented 308.
  - **One from re-reading a sentence for an unrelated reason** — the last bullet below.
  **That is the finding, and it is sharper than the list.** This entry's proposed fix is a *search habit*, and a search habit keyed on subjects would have found **five of ten**. Two needed somebody to remember a prior enumeration, two needed a number to stop matching, and one needed a sentence to be re-read for another reason entirely. **A sweep is a floor, not a method** — and attributing all ten to it would be one scope for the subject list and another for the site count, in one sentence. **None of these are fixed here** — they are enumerated so each is a decision somebody takes rather than something a sweep found and nobody wrote down.

- **`tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md`, "ZAP ran passive-only" section** — *"no corpus can, whatever it is captured against. That is registered as **G24**"*. **Falsified**: the probe obtained the pair by changing the scan plan, not the corpus. It is the same "any corpus / never" conflation G24's title was rewritten for, one file over, and the README additionally instructs the reader to *"not conclude that a better target would fix it"* — still true, and now for a reason the sentence does not give.
- **Same file, same section** — *"Its five alerts are all response-level"*. **Accurate about the committed corpus** and unchanged by anything here. Enumerated because it is the sentence ADR-0023 Decision B's struck ground (3) was generalised from, so whoever re-captures must not read it as a bound.
- **Same file, "What these fixtures do and do not cover"** — *"G24 bounds ZAP's reachable CWE vocabulary independently of any corpus"*. **Falsified, and this one is the struck ground (3) in shorter words** — not verbatim: the ADR's ground read *"bounds ZAP's reachable CWE vocabulary **to passive response-configuration weaknesses permanently and** independently of any corpus"*, the README drops the middle clause, and the claim is the same. The rewritten Decision B strikes it in the ADR and then sends readers to this README, which still asserts it in the present tense. The most consequential hit of the ten.
- **Same file, the CWE-cardinality table** — *"verified as `str` on all five alerts"*. Accurate about the corpus; a count that moves to **eight** alerts on any re-capture under an active plan — the five passive ones plus `10106`, `6-5` and `90036`.
- **`docs/ROADMAP.md`, G23's resolution** — *"Does NOT establish a SAST↔DAST pair over the same vulnerability, and **no corpus ever will**"*. **Falsified as a general claim**, true of that corpus. **This one is in a file this commit edits**, which makes leaving it a deliberate non-fix rather than an oversight: G23 is a *resolved* entry recording what one capture session established, and rewriting a resolved entry's history is a different act from amending a live one.
- **`README.md`, "Core differentiators"** — *"related findings **across tools** (e.g. SAST + DAST on the same endpoint) are grouped into one traceable risk"*. Already enumerated in the note above as unobtainable; **now falsified in the opposite direction** — obtainable, and still not built. Product voice, so softening or keeping it is a product decision.
- **`src/verion/modules/scanning/adapters/outbound/scanners/zap_adapter.py`, `_build_plan_yaml`'s comment** — *"deliberately no `activeScan` job … out of scope for this issue"*. **Not falsified**: it remains true of the issue that wrote it. Enumerated because it is **M5.4's to change**, and because it is the one artifact whose edit makes every other sentence here false at once.
- **`docs/ROADMAP.md`, Milestone 5's Goal sentence** — it ends *"…the asset/location signals FR-6 admits. **FR-6.** This is one of the two 'star' components"*, a dangling second `FR-6.` left by the previous rewrite. **Not falsified by anything here and pre-existing**, but this change's second Goal amendment re-reads that exact sentence to conclude it needs no third rewrite, so this was the pass that should have caught it. Enumerated so whoever next edits that line inherits it rather than rediscovering it.
- **`docs/ROADMAP.md`, in TWO places — M5.0's ruff file-set measurement and G12's `Resolution:`** — *"308 under 0.16.3 … 278 `.py` plus exactly 30 `.md`"*, and G12's stronger *"the repo tracks **exactly** 278 `.py` files and **exactly** 30 `.md` files"*. **Stale, present-tense, and not this commit's doing**: the repo tracks **31** `.md` files because ADR-0023 was added after M5.0's commit, so `ruff format --check .` now reports **309**. Enumerated because it is this entry's class arriving from **an ordinary file addition rather than from a measurement** — the cheapest possible trigger, and nothing caught it — and because the second site is worded more absolutely than the first, so fixing only the one a sweep happens to hit leaves the stronger false claim standing.

**Not fixed here, and the scope line is deliberate.** Rewriting five entries' `Status:` lines is a change to entries this commit is not otherwise touching, which is the bundling `CLAUDE.md` forbids and which M5.0's whole argument rests on. What this commit does instead is (1) record the measurement, and (2) write G24's, G27's and G29's new `Status:` lines in the plain readable form, so it does not add to the set. Whoever fixes it should decide between normalising the five entries and widening the regex — **they are not equivalent**: the regex is one line and would silently accept future variants nobody has thought of, while normalising leaves the parser strict and the register's form uniform. There is a third option worth naming, since it is the one this entry's own logic points at: assert the readability *itself*, so an entry whose `Status:` claims an assignment the parser cannot read fails regardless of confirmation count. That is the "three-line test" ask G20 already makes for a different measurement.

Note (2026-08-26) — **the second sweep's "None of these are fixed here" is QUALIFIED rather than falsified: it was true of the commit that wrote it, and four of the ten sites are now taken.** All four are in `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md` — the two falsified claims in its *"ZAP ran passive-only"* and *"What these fixtures do and do not cover"* sections are struck and corrected, and the two accurate-but-generalisable ones in those same sections are scoped with the treatment named in the text. **Six remain**, unchanged and still enumerated above; the list is deliberately not rewritten or renumbered, because it is the record of one sweep's output rather than a worklist.

Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **the trigger fired — this is the next milestone-boundary review.** What the review did about the class: step 2 ran with a listing of `src/` checked against the docs rather than a diff, which is how ARCHITECTURE's catalog drifts were found, and a calibration run measured the sweep against four drifts already known and added checklist step 6 for the one it missed. The drifts it fixed are in that commit; the older ones it did not fix are named in its review log row. The entry stays open: nothing makes a sentence that a diff falsifies without touching visible to a diff-scoped pass.

---

Note (2026-09-16, M6→M7 boundary): **the note-consolidation rule piloted on this entry is WITHDRAWN by its author, and the withdrawal is the finding.** The rule was: from the third note on, a new note replaces the earlier ones with one dated current-state paragraph keeping each conclusion and dropping each derivation. Piloted here it took the entry from **68 lines to 17**, and it is withdrawn for two reasons the pilot itself produced. **First, it treats notes as LEAVES when they are NODES WITH INBOUND EDGES.** Consolidating broke **five live citations in three files**: `scripts/check_claims.py`'s `check_assignments_name_real_issues` docstring, which cites *"G28's 2026-08-25 note"* and which the very same commit was editing; **G58** three times, one a verbatim quotation of the guard note and one the five-of-seven unreadable list; **G30** and **G32** on the two sweeps' site enumerations, G32's `Trigger:` naming *"the next commit that takes any remaining G28 site"*; and `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md`'s scoping parenthetical. **Second, "conclusion versus derivation" is not a clean cut.** The dropped claim that a hit of the sweep's own grep *was passed over as a correct hit* reads as derivation and **is** a conclusion — it is about the READING of a hit, which the surviving *"a sweep is a floor, not a method"* is not, that one being about the coverage of a pattern. **A narrower version scoped to entries with no inbound citations is deliberately NOT shipped**: the largest entries are large because they are central, and central entries get cited, so such a version would apply mainly where it does not help. **The result of the pilot is that the register's volume problem does not have this fix, and saying so is the finding** — not that this entry is an awkward first case. **One correction the pilot did earn, and it stands:** the 2026-08-26 note's *"Six remain"* was true at its date and is now **five**, `ccc73f4` (M5.4) having removed `_build_plan_yaml`'s *"deliberately no `activeScan` job"* comment, as **G32**'s own note already records.

Note (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **deferred under Rules for M8 rule 1.** M8 ships product only, and this entry does not trace to M8's exit condition. Trigger: **the M8→M9 boundary review**.

### G29 — The demo target does not boot under this project's own interpreter, and the failure presents as a clean scan
Confirmed: M5.1 · Status: resolved → M5.9
Resolution: M5.9 — **the constraint is in code rather than in this register, which is the fix this entry asked for.** `scripts/capture_active_scan.py`'s `_uv_serve_argv` pins `--python 3.11`, and `tests/integration/test_active_scan_finds_the_sink.py` imports that function rather than copying it, so one spelling serves both the committed capture and the CI test and neither can drift from the other. The gate is `wait_for_liveness`, keyed on **`1.24.1` in the response body** — rendered by the serving process from `urllib3.__version__`, the one string 3.12 cannot produce — rather than on an accepted connection or a bare 200, which is the sub-bullet above asking for a request that must succeed *and* come from the app. **The false negative is closed at both doors** (ADR-0027 decision 6): the gate is its own test, separate from the finding assertion, so a boot failure reports as a boot failure instead of as an absent vulnerability. **Verified by mutation, not by construction:** forcing `--python 3.12` reproduces this entry's `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'urllib3.packages.six.moves'` verbatim, the gate catches it, no scan runs at all, and the failure message carries the target's own traceback. The second consumer this entry names, **M5.3, is not retroactively covered** — its fixtures are curated rather than captured, and nothing in M5.9 reaches them.
Note (2026-08-27): the assignment moved from M5.4 to M5.9 when M5.4's scope was split.
Blocks-if-unresolved: **any test that stands `verion-demo-target` up — and it fails as a FALSE NEGATIVE that is indistinguishable from a correct result, which is the whole reason this is an entry rather than a README line.** `.python-version` pins **3.12** and `.venv` is 3.12.14, while the target's pinned closure requires **3.11**: `urllib3 1.24.1` raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'urllib3.packages.six.moves'` from `urllib3/exceptions.py` on 3.12, and `app.py` imports `urllib3` at module top, so **nothing ever binds a port.** In a hand-run that is a flag you trip over once and fix in ten seconds. In an automated test it is not: **nothing in the test code carries the constraint**, and the failure surfaces either as connection-refused, or — worse, if the scanner starts before the app was expected to be ready — as a ZAP run reporting **only passive header alerts**, which is byte-for-byte what a *correct* passive scan of a healthy target produces. **A boot failure would read as "no injection vulnerability found."** For **M5.9** that is fatal in the exact direction that matters: the issue exists to demonstrate that active scanning finds the sink, and its failure mode is a green suite asserting the sink is absent.
- **The issue that stands the target up must assert the target is actually serving before it scans** — a request that must succeed and whose response must come from the app, not merely a port that accepts a connection. Without that, this gap is undetectable from the test's own output.
- **M5.3 is the second consumer**, and inherits it whole: its fixtures are a proof artifact, and a fixture set captured against a target that never booted would be a correlation suite proving correlation over an empty DAST side. That is **G19**'s failure mode reached through the runtime rather than through the generator.
Evidence, re-derived rather than reported: `uv run --no-project --python 3.12 --with "urllib3==1.24.1" python -c "import urllib3"` fails with the `ModuleNotFoundError` above; the same command with `--python 3.11` imports cleanly (with the `-c` payload widened to print, it reports `OK 3.11.5 1.24.1`). The probe ran the target under `--python 3.11`, corroborated independently from the scan output itself — `report.json`'s alert `10036-2` carries `evidence: 'Werkzeug/2.3.8 Python/3.11.5'` on every instance, which is the target's own `Server` header — and by the target's `requirements.txt`, which records the closure as resolved under Python 3.11.5.
Deferral rationale: **there is nothing to fix in the target, and that is a permanent property rather than a temporary one.** `verion-demo-target`'s `requirements.txt` states in as many words that regenerating or bumping any line "invalidates every captured scanner result pinned to this SHA", and `urllib3 1.24.1` is not incidental — it is the deliberately vulnerable pin the Trivy half of the corpus rests on. So the version that breaks 3.12 is the version the fixtures exist to capture, and upgrading it would trade this gap for a re-capture of the whole corpus. The constraint therefore has to be **carried into whatever stands the target up**, which is why this is assigned to the first such issue rather than left as a note. Nothing is broken today: no test stands the target up.
Note: **distinct from G23 and G25 in the G9/G10 manner.** G23 was a corpus that did not exist, fixed by capturing one. G25 is a missing domain field, fixed under `src/`. **This is a runtime precondition for reproducing the corpus at all**, and no corpus however well captured discharges it — it shares that last property with **G24** and **G27** rather than with the entries it was found beside.

### G30 — `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` and the match key have no issue: M5.1 closed without building them, and three artifacts cite M5.1 as though it had
Confirmed: M5.4 planning read · Status: resolved → M5.8
Blocks-if-unresolved: **M5.2, and the absence does not present as absence — which is the whole of the risk.** M5.2 begins, finds no use case whose output it can persist, and the natural fix at that moment is to build the matcher inside M5.2: bundling the one piece ADR-0023 designed most carefully, under pressure to unblock something else, in a milestone whose own M5.0 argues at length that this is how debt is built rather than paid. Second and independent of it: **ADR-0023's Amendments defer the match key's own field list and section (b)'s no-narrowing constraint to an unnamed "implementation issue"**, so a reader of that ADR assumes the issue exists somewhere and does not go looking — a deferral pointing at nothing reads exactly like a deferral pointing at something.
Evidence: `git grep -n "CorrelateFindingsUseCase"` **at the commit BEFORE this one** returned **eight hits, every one in a `.md` file** — the type existed in prose only, in the milestone after the issue that was to build it closed. **The count is dated because this commit moves it** — it adds six, all of them this entry, M5.8 and the annotations this commit writes — for the reason M5.4–M5.7's introduction gives for its own two figures: a present-tense count in the commit that invalidates it is the defect one level down. **The other half of the reading — not one hit under `src/` or `tests/` — needs no dating: this commit does not change it, and it is the whole finding.** Cited by file and symbol, deliberately not by line: this commit's own annotations shift every `ROADMAP.md` hit below them, so a line number captured with the grep would be wrong before it was committed, which is **G28**'s recorded defect arriving in the entry that cites G28. The eight were `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.1's inbound-ports row and §8's sequence-diagram participant; `ROADMAP.md`'s M3.7 multi-scanner prerequisite bullet, M5.1's opening "Decide first" bullet, M5.1's own `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` bullet, and **G1**'s `Blocks-if-unresolved:`; ADR-016's Context; and ADR-0023's Context. Second grep — `field list|implementation issue|no-narrowing|frozen dataclass` over this file — returns **one** hit, in M5.6's placement discussion quoting ADR-0023 (b), and **no register entry**: the ADR's deferral had nothing behind it, which is what this entry now is.
- **The four hits this commit does not edit, with a decision each rather than silence**, because a sweep result survives in the register and nowhere else:
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md` **§8** — **not falsified, and read rather than assumed**, since the §5.1 row this commit edits points at §8 in its own words. §8 draws `Corr->>DB: read Findings`, unscoped — the disagreement §5.1 records and declares itself the more specific about — and its closing line names **"M5-M7"** rather than any issue, so it carries no citation that can rot. If M5.8 chooses per-project scope, that unscoped read becomes right rather than wrong.
  - `ROADMAP.md` **M3.7's prerequisite bullet** — **falsified in BOTH halves, and only one of them is this commit's doing.** It reads `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` **(M5.1)** *"exists to link findings from **different** tools"*. The attribution is falsified here; the **cross-tool** half was falsified by **M5.1 itself**, which measured every cross-tool equality empty. That second half appears in **neither of G28's sweeps**, so this disposition is the only place it is recorded — which is the argument for writing dispositions down rather than deciding them silently. Not edited, deliberately, on the precedent G28's second sweep set for G23's `Resolution:` — rewriting a *completed* issue's history is a different act from amending a live one. Whoever takes it takes both halves, in a change of its own.
  - `ROADMAP.md` **G1's `Blocks-if-unresolved:`** and **ADR-016's Context** — **not falsified by this**: both say "M5", not "M5.1", and M5.8 is in M5. Their *cross-tool* claim was falsified by M5.1's measurement and is already enumerated in G28's first sweep; G1 is resolved and ADR-016 is accepted, so neither is edited here either.
No `Deferral rationale:` field, and the absence is deliberate rather than an omission: this entry is **born assigned**, not deferred, so there is no deferral to justify. G28 measures why writing one anyway would be worse than useless — an assigned entry that also carries that field is passed by the escalation guard on a fallback asserting the opposite of its own assignment, leaving the assignment inert with nothing saying so.
Resolution (M5.8, 2026-08-26): **closed by the grep that opened it, not by the roadmap line that promised to close it.** This entry's whole finding was that `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` returned no hit under `src/` or `tests/`. Re-run at the commit before this one (`e0318e1`), `git grep -n "CorrelateFindingsUseCase" -- src tests` returned **nothing, exit 1**; run again here it returns **7 hits across 3 files — 1 under `src/` and 6 under `tests/`**. Both figures are dated to this commit, on the precedent the `Evidence:` field above sets for its own. What now exists is the pair the entry named: the frozen match key in `correlation/domain/match_key.py`, and the use case in `correlation/application/correlate_findings.py`. **`correlation`'s two import-linter contracts also rejected-or-kept real code for the first time** — they had existed since M0.2 with nothing in the module to constrain, which the ADR's Context names as the reason its constraints were probed rather than assumed; both are `KEPT` against code that now imports across two module boundaries. **The second clause of this entry's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` is stale and is deliberately left standing** — a resolved entry is the record of what it was blocking and when, so editing that field would destroy the thing it is kept for. Registered as **G34** instead.

### G31 — The match key groups ZAP on the FULL `url`, which ADR-0023's own Decision C keys its table on PATH to avoid, and M5.4's active plan is what makes the difference bite
Confirmed: M5.8 · Status: resolved → M5.6
Resolution: M5.6 commit 3 (2026-09-15) — **the first out, *"re-keys on path with a measurement behind it"*, is taken.**
- **What changed.** `build_match_key` keys every `url` on `urlsplit(url).path or "/"`.
- **Unconditional, not behind `ServingDeclarationPort`.** Grouping two query strings of one endpoint is a property of the key, not of whether cross-tool matching is valid. `test_zap_is_keyed_on_path_whether_or_not_a_declaration_is_in_force` pins both states.
- **The measurement.** M5.9's active capture: 16 ZAP findings give 5 groups on the full URL and 4 on the path. `6-5` and `90036` now share one `/calculate` group.
- **The passive corpus stays at 4 groups**, because its plan yields one query string per route.
- **Test flipped deliberately:** `test_correlation_accuracy.py`'s query-string test.
- **Not in this entry's `Blocks-if-unresolved:`, but part of this resolution:** the path key also drops the host. **G53** carries the corrected ground for why that is lossless.
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the moment an `activeScan` job ships, `url` equality splits `/calculate` into two groups and separates exactly the two alerts with discriminating power** — `6-5` fires at `?expr=calculate` and `90036` at `?expr=2*3`, which is why Decision C's table is keyed on path rather than on URL. Today the passive plan yields one `?expr` per route, so the grouping measures correctly (ZAP 13 findings → 4 groups) and ADR-0023's 2026-08-26 amendment keys on the full URL on that basis. Under an active plan it does not, and **the failure is a silent over-split**: two Risks where one belongs, with nothing in the output saying the split came from a query string rather than from the system. It lands on `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10 as well, since `90036` is the confidence-3 alert the SAST↔DAST demonstration turns on — separating it from the endpoint's other alerts weakens the group M5.6 exists to produce.
Deferral rationale: **the key is deliberately not re-keyed on path, and the reason is the rule the amendment that opens this entry is written under.** No measurement of a path-keyed *grouping* exists — Decision C's table is per-path alert *sets*, not a grouping of `Finding`s — and nothing in this repo normalizes a URL, so introducing a path derivation now would be unmeasured design inside the commit whose whole point is deciding before measuring code into existence. **Not born assigned, deliberately**: M5.4's scope is the scan plan and consent, not the match key, so this is a trigger on somebody else's issue rather than work inside it. *(An earlier draft justified that with "M5.8 ships before it", which this register does not assert and cannot: M5.4 is `Depends on: —`, M5.8 is `Depends on: M5.1`, neither depends on the other, and M5.4–M5.7's introduction says in as many words that number order is not execution order here. The scope argument stands without it, and if M5.4 ships first the trigger simply fires on the earlier issue.)* Re-entry trigger: **M5.4**, which now cites this entry in its own bullets rather than leaving the trigger reachable only by a reader of the register. Whoever adds the `activeScan` job re-reads section 6 of ADR-0023's 2026-08-26 amendment and either re-keys on path with a measurement behind it, or records why the split is acceptable.
- **The disposition sweep for this commit — run on the subjects it falsifies rather than on its own diff, per G28's prescription, and recorded with the entry the way this series records it.** The three commits before this one each enumerated what they falsified without touching; this one nearly shipped without a sweep, and the first hit below is what that cost.
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md` **§12's ADR-0023 summary bullet** — *"The key's own field list remains deferred to the implementation issue."* **Falsified, and CORRECTED in this commit**: it sits in a file this change already edits, and that bullet's own parenthetical says restatements of an ADR are exactly where such claims rot — G28 by name.
  - ADR-0023 **section (b)** — *"derive the key's expected annotations from `Finding`'s own declarations"*. **Falsified as an instruction, NOT corrected here.** Two of the three chosen fields are on `Location`, so a test following (b) literally covers one field of three and passes green. The amendment's section 7 records the constraint; (b) itself carries no marker. Left for its own commit rather than taken in this one's last round.
  - **G30's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` second clause** — *"ADR-0023's Amendments defer the match key's own field list … to an unnamed 'implementation issue'"*. **Falsified, NOT corrected here.** G30's assignment is correctly untouched: `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` still does not exist under `src/`. Only the clause is stale.
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md` **§8's unscoped `Corr->>DB: read Findings`** — **not falsified.** §5.1 already recorded that the two sections disagreed and that §5.1 was the more specific; per-project scope makes §8's unscoped read right rather than wrong.
  - **M5.2's and M5.3's `Depends on: M5.8`** — **not falsified.** Both depend on the matcher, which this commit designs and does not build.
Note (2026-08-26, M5.8): **the "today" this entry rests on is now measured through the matcher rather than predicted before it.** Run over the committed fixtures through the real mappers and the shipped key, ZAP's 13 findings group to **4** on the full `Location.url` — `http://target.example:8080/` 5, `…/calculate?expr=2*3` 4, `…/robots.txt` 2, `…/sitemap.xml` 2 — so the passive-plan half of this entry holds against code and not only against a hand count. **Nothing here is discharged**: the entry's subject is what happens when the plan stops being passive, and no active capture exists. The one thing that changes is the standing of the premise — it was a claim about how a key would behave, and it is now a property of a test that fails if it stops being true.
Note (2026-08-27, M5.4): **the trigger fired, and of this entry's two outs the second is taken — the split is accepted, not fixed.** The `activeScan` job shipped, so on a consented project ZAP's full-`url` grouping now splits a route the moment an active plan yields more than one query string for it. Re-keying on path is not done here, and this entry's own `Deferral rationale:` is why, unchanged: no measurement of a path-keyed *grouping* exists, and M5.4 produced no capture that could yield one — the first active capture is M5.9's. Deriving a path key inside the issue that ships the plan would be unmeasured design in the exact place this entry warns about it. **Nothing here discharges the entry, and its subject got worse rather than closer to resolved**: the over-split moved from hypothetical to reachable in production. Whoever ships M5.9's capture is the first person able to take the other out.
Note (2026-09-09, M5.6 commit 1): **the missing measurement now exists, and ADR-0029 decision 4 commits M5.6 to taking the FIRST out — but not in this commit.** This entry's `Deferral rationale:` rests on two clauses and both are now falsified. *"No measurement of a path-keyed grouping exists"*: M5.9's committed active capture, run through the real mappers and the shipped `group_by_match_key`, gives **16 ZAP findings → 5 groups on the full `url`, 4 on path**, with `6-5` at `?expr=calculate` and `90036` at `?expr=2*3` **in different groups** — this entry's silent over-split, measured through code rather than hand-counted from Decision C's per-path alert sets. *"Nothing in this repo normalizes a URL"*: ADR-0029 decision 4 puts a path derivation at the `build_match_key` call site, so that ceases to be true the moment commit 3 lands. **Both clauses survive in a SECOND place this commit does not correct, and naming it is the point of this sentence:** they are the ground of ADR-0023's 2026-08-26 amendment **section 6**, *"**The key is not changed to a path here.** No measurement of a path-keyed grouping exists … and nothing in this repo normalizes URLs"* — the text that seeded this entry, inside an accepted ADR, fixable only by a dated `## Amendments` entry. That site is added to **G46**'s enumeration rather than left for whoever fixes this one to discover; a reader who annotates only the register has annotated half. **The entry stays OPEN and its `Status:` is unchanged**, because this is a documentation commit that re-keys nothing — a falsified rationale is not a discharged entry, and reading it as one is the error this note exists to prevent. Which out was taken, and the re-measurement of the passive corpus under it, belong to **commit 3**. The citation is also no longer one-directional: **M5.6's block now cites this entry**, which it did not while this entry named M5.6 by number — the inert shape **G28** measures, arriving in the entry that predicted the consequence.

### G32 — G28's second sweep is itself incomplete, and the two sites it missed were found by editing beside it rather than by any sweep
Confirmed: G28 site correction (post-M5.8), M5→M6 boundary · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **two sites in `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md` that G28's own list was supposed to have enumerated exhaustively and does not — and the more dangerous half is what that says about the list rather than about the file. They need DIFFERENT treatments, stated separately because one clause covering both would prescribe the wrong one for site (2).** (1) **Falsified, and therefore a strike:** the *"What these fixtures do and do not cover"* cross-tool paragraph's *"the CWE intersection is **empty for all three pairs** … Trivy's 14 and ZAP's 4 are disjoint"*. That is **ADR-0023 Decision B's struck ground (1)** in README form, falsified by that ADR's new ground (1) — under an active scan `CWE-22` is on both sides — and G28's second sweep enumerated only ground (3)'s twin in the same file, so a reader discharging that list leaves this one behind. (2) **NOT falsified, and therefore a qualification:** the *Severity coverage* bullet's unscoped *"ZAP: … **No `3` (High)**"*. It sits inside a per-corpus tally, the committed corpus genuinely has no riskcode 3, and the probe changed no committed file — so it is accurate and unscoped, the same class as the two sentences the correcting commit scoped, while the probe's `6-5` and `90036` are both riskcode 3. **The finding is the sweep, not the sentences.** G28's Deferral rationale proposes a search habit — grep the falsified claim's *subject* — and both of these were sitting in a file that habit had already been run over; neither turned up. They surfaced only because somebody was editing the paragraphs next to them. That is one level past what G28 records: its own note already measures that a subject-keyed sweep would have found five of ten sites, and this entry is the first evidence that the *enumeration itself* under-reports rather than merely the search that produced it.
Deferral rationale: **not born assigned, and deliberately not folded into G28.** No issue owns correcting these two sentences: the commit that found them was scoped to G28's ten by an explicit ceiling, and absorbing a site outside that list would have made the ceiling meaningless — a sweep result quietly widened is the failure G28 exists to name. Folding them into G28 would also lose the distinction that makes this worth an entry: G28 is a defect in a *review step's scope*, while this is a defect in the *artifact that step produced*, and the fix differs — G28 wants a habit, this wants somebody to re-run the sweep over that one file and publish the enumeration. Trigger: **the next commit that takes any remaining G28 site**, or any re-capture of these fixtures, whichever is first. Whoever takes it should re-derive the enumeration for that README rather than trusting either list.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **the trigger fired at `ccc73f4` (M5.4) and was not recorded here** — that commit removed `_build_plan_yaml`'s "deliberately no `activeScan` job" comment, one of G28's sites. Recorded, not acted on.

### G33 — ADR-0023 section (b) is a live instruction that produces a conformance test covering one field of three, and passing
Confirmed: M5.8 design commit, M5.8 implementation, M6.2, M7.1, M6.3, M7.2, M7.3 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the next module that repeats this pattern ships a green conformance test over a fraction of its own key, and the green is what makes it dangerous.** Section (b) instructs a reader to derive the key's expected annotations from `Finding`'s own declarations. Two of the three fields the key actually carries are declared on `Location`, so a test that follows the instruction literally checks `project_id` and silently skips `package` and `url` — one field of three — while reporting success. The ADR binds M6, M7 and M8 in its own words, and each of those reads another module's entity, so each gets its own key and its own conformance test written from this instruction. The failure is not hypothetical for a second reason: the whole argument for this ADR's placement is that the construction site is a *total* check, and a partial test is exactly what would let a narrowed annotation pass — which section (c) already names as the thing the placement does not confer for free.
Deferral rationale: **the instruction is superseded in the same ADR and the supersession is not reachable from the instruction.** Section 7 of the 2026-08-26 amendment states the sharpened form, and M5.8's conformance test is written to it — `tests/unit/test_match_key.py` derives from both types and carries a dedicated assertion that fails if the source map is ever narrowed back to one. So the defect is not live in this repo's code; it is live in the *instruction*, for the next reader who starts at section (b) and stops there. Correcting it is not this issue's work and the two available corrections are both wrong-sized: rewriting section (b) inline would edit an accepted ADR's Decision, which M5.8's own criterion (b) forbids in as many words, and a fourth dated amendment whose entire content is a pointer to the third is ceremony that makes the ADR longer without making it more findable. Re-entry trigger: **M6.1**, the next issue whose module reads another module's entity and therefore writes the next key; or any change to ADR-0023's Decision section, which is the moment an inline marker stops being an out-of-band edit.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **the trigger fired and produced a forecast rather than a fix, which is worth recording because the two look identical from outside.** This entry's re-entry trigger is M6.1, *"the next issue whose module reads another module's entity and therefore writes the next key"*. M6.1 wrote no key and no conformance test: ADR-0005 decision 2 has `risk_engine` take **scalars** at one construction site, so there is no second type whose annotations could be derived from `Finding`'s, and section (b)'s instruction had nothing to mislead here. **It moves to M6.2**, which builds that site — and the hazard transfers intact, because a scalars site is exactly where section (b)'s partial derivation would go unnoticed: `build_match_key`'s own `file_path` and `start_line` parameters already get `mypy` and no conformance assertion (**G53**'s second half), and `risk_engine`'s `source` and `severity` parameters will arrive the same way. Not assigned: M6.2 has not run, and this entry's fix remains an edit to an accepted ADR rather than to code.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.3, written before any of this issue's code): **the trigger fired at M6.2 and the forecast HELD, so this is a CONFIRMATION and not a fix.** The M6.1 note above moved this entry to M6.2 as the issue that would build the scalars site, and it did: `ComputeRiskUseCase.execute` constructs `SurfaceMember` from `finding.source` and `finding.severity`, which is exactly the shape section (b)'s partial derivation goes unnoticed in. **The site is `mypy`-only.** There is one construction site in `src/` and one in `tests/` — `_member` in `tests/unit/test_risk_scoring.py`, through which every hand-written member in that file is built — and **no conformance assertion anywhere derives `SurfaceMember`'s annotations from `Finding`'s own declarations**, the way `tests/unit/test_match_key.py` does for the key over `Finding` and `Location`. A narrowed annotation on `Finding.source` or `Finding.severity` would fail at the construction site and nowhere else. **The fix is UNCHANGED** — still the same wrong-sized pair this entry's `Deferral rationale:` names: rewriting section (b) inline edits an accepted ADR's Decision, which M5.8's own criterion (b) forbids, and a fourth dated amendment pointing at the third is ceremony. **M6.2 is appended to `Confirmed:` above, taking this entry to three**, at which `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` passes it on its `Deferral rationale:` alone — so the guard will never force it, and this note is the only thing reporting that the count moved. Re-entry trigger re-points to **M7.1**, the next issue whose module reads another module's entity; **M6.3 is not it** — its adapter reads `risk_engine`'s own `ScoredSurface` and authorizes through a bool-returning port, so it builds no new conformance site. **Recorded also because `2b38d3b` named this entry from two OTHERS and not from itself**: G64's and G65's `Deferral rationale:` lines both cite G33, while this entry's own body was untouched by that commit — verified, its text is identical across `7164e01..2b38d3b`.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.1): **the trigger fired, and this time the site was built WITH the conformance test rather than left to `mypy`.** M7.1 copies another type's fields for the first time since M6.2: `ExplainableSignal` in `risk_engine/ports/explainable_decision.py` copies `Signal`. ADR-0032 decision 1 puts both the carrier and its one fill site, `explainable_decision`, inside `risk_engine`, so `mypy` sees both ends. `tests/unit/test_explainable_decision.py` derives the shared fields' names **and annotations** from `dataclasses.fields(Signal)` — section (b)'s derivation over the type actually copied — and a narrowing mutation (`note: str | None` → `note: str` on the carrier) was applied physically and **killed**. **The instance is covered and the entry's subject is not**: section (b)'s wording is unchanged, and the next reader who starts there still derives from `Finding` alone. M7.1 is appended to `Confirmed:`, taking this entry to four. Re-entry trigger re-points to **M7.2**, whose port delivers `ExplainableDecision` to `brief` together with a Risk address, and is the next site where a module fills a carrier from another module's type.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.2): **the trigger fired, and M7.2 fills no carrier by copying another module's fields.**
- **Inside `risk_engine`.** `ExplainableRisk.finding_ids` is filled from `ScoredSurface.finding_ids`, with both ends in `risk_engine`. `test_the_carriers_finding_ids_annotation_is_the_surfaces_own` derives the annotation from `dataclasses.fields(ScoredSurface)`, and narrowing it to `list[str]` was killed.
- **Held whole.** `SecurityBrief` holds `ExplainableDecision` and `Explanation` whole rather than copying their fields.
- **The one copy site, the stored JSON, is derived.** `_decision_to_json` builds its keys from `dataclasses.fields`, and a pinned v1 enumeration is compared against that derivation (ADR-0033 Consequences). That is this entry's lesson applied at a storage boundary.
- **Unchanged.** Section (b)'s wording is untouched, and the entry stays open.
- **Trigger** re-points to **the next issue that fills a carrier from another module's type**.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 2): **the trigger fired: `brief` fills `BriefMember` from `normalization`'s `Finding`**, taken by inference off `FindingRepositoryPort.get_by_id`. The conformance test derives the expected annotations from **both** `Finding` and `Location`, the sharpened form, not section (b)'s: `tests/unit/test_brief_member.py::test_every_annotation_copies_finding_or_location_without_narrowing`. Section (b)'s wording is still untouched and still produces the one-field-of-three test for a reader who stops there. Trigger re-points to **the next issue that fills a carrier from another module's type**.

Note (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **deferred under Rules for M8 rule 1.** M8 ships product only, and this entry does not trace to M8's exit condition. Trigger: **the M8→M9 boundary review**.

### G34 — G30's stated blocker names a deferral that no longer points anywhere, inside an entry that is now resolved and therefore frozen
Confirmed: M5.8, M5→M6 boundary · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **a reader who reaches G30 through a citation gets a description of the repo that stopped being true in two stages, and the entry's resolved status makes it look like settled history rather than a stale sentence.** Half of the clause went when M5.8 was created and named as the implementation issue the ADR's deferral pointed at; the rest went when that issue's field list and scope were decided. The concrete cost is misdirection rather than a wrong build: somebody auditing whether ADR-0023 still defers anything reads that clause, believes the field list is open, and either re-decides a settled question or opens a duplicate entry for it. Resolved entries are cited more than live ones in this register, so a stale one is read more often, not less.
Deferral rationale: **the entry is deliberately not edited, and that is the whole reason this exists separately.** The register's own rule keeps a resolved entry as the record of how long a gap took and what it was blocking; `Blocks-if-unresolved:` is the field that record is made of, so rewriting it to be accurate today would delete the statement of what was at stake then. **Not born assigned**: no issue owns re-reading resolved entries, and inventing one would be worse than the gap — a sweep over every resolved entry's blocker text is a task with no trigger and no end. What is wanted instead is narrower and belongs to whoever next touches this register's conventions: a way to mark a resolved entry's fields as historical, so accuracy and preservation stop competing. Re-entry trigger: **the next change to the Deferred-gaps rules above**, or the next resolved entry whose blocker text goes stale, whichever is first.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **the trigger fired at `3a89783`**, whose change to the Deferred-gaps rules added the assignment-format rule. G30's stale blocker is unchanged, and the class this entry names has grown: G24's and G46's resolutions still describe G27 as unresolved, which it no longer is. Not rewritten, for the reason this entry gives.

### G35 — `cross-module-correlation` and its seven siblings forbid other modules' `.domain` and `.adapters` and say nothing about `.application`
Confirmed: M5.8, M5.2, M6.1, M6.2 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **an import of another module's `application/` passes `lint-imports` green while carrying across exactly what the contracts exist to keep out, and the green report is the failure rather than a mitigation of it.** Concretely, and reachable from this module today: `correlation` may import `normalization`'s `ListProjectFindingsUseCase` and its `ProjectFindings` return type. That hands over another module's orchestration — its authorization order, its paging bounds, its completeness policy — and, through `ProjectFindings.items`, reaches `Finding` **by name**, which is the single outcome ADR-0023 spent its length routing around. Rule 3 forbids it in words and no contract forbids it in CI, so the violation ships with a passing architecture check attached, which is worse than an unchecked rule: a reviewer who trusts the gate has been told the change is compliant. It is a live hazard rather than a latent one for the reason this entry is dated here — M5.8 is the first code in `correlation`, so before it there was nothing in the module that could have exploited the hole.
Note (2026-08-26, M5.2): **first real encounter, and the hole was not taken.** `ListProjectRisksUseCase` needs paging bounds identical to M4.5's; importing `DEFAULT_PAGE_LIMIT`/`MAX_PAGE_LIMIT` from `normalization.application.list_project_findings` would have passed `lint-imports` green. It declares its own instead, with a comment naming this entry. Recorded because an entry whose first encounter goes unwritten is one nobody can tell is being honoured — the status is unchanged and the contract is still silent about other modules' `application/`.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is an edit to eight contracts and it is not this issue's, and the contracts are deliberately untouched here.** Every `cross-module-*` contract carries the same shape, so adding `.application` to one and not the rest would leave the repo asserting a boundary for one module that seven others do not have — the inconsistency being harder to reason about than the uniform hole. Whether the right fix is adding `.application` to all eight, or replacing the forbidden-module lists with an allow-list of `.ports` (which would close `.domain`, `.adapters`, `.application` and anything added later in one move, and is the shape that would not need revisiting), is a design question with a real trade-off and belongs in its own change with its own ADR note. **Not born assigned**: no current issue's scope includes the contracts, and M5.2 — the next code in this module — must not be where a repo-wide contract change gets bundled. Note the relationship to ADR-0023's alternative 5, which is adjacent and not the same: that alternative records the `.ports` hole and rejects using it, on the record, so that a reader who finds it legal does not stop there. It says nothing about `.application`, and `.application` is the more dangerous of the two — a port yields no name to annotate with, while a use case's return type does. Re-entry trigger: **any change to `[tool.importlinter]`**, or the first time a module needs something from another module that its ports do not expose, which is the pressure that turns this from unused to used.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **its second trigger is reached at M6.1.** `risk_engine` needs candidate Risks, which `correlation/ports/` does not expose, and `ListProjectRisksUseCase`'s docstring expects M6 to call an `application/` use case. Carried in M6.1.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.2): **the hole was available and was not taken; `correlation/ports/` now publishes.** `CandidateRiskPort` ships with `CorrelationCandidateRisks` over `CorrelateFindingsUseCase`, so `risk_engine` imports a port rather than another module's `application/`. **One concrete thing would have forced the hole and is recorded because it is not obvious from the port's signature:** `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` raises `correlation.domain.exceptions.ProjectAccessDenied`, and `cross-module-risk-engine` forbids `risk_engine` from naming anything under `correlation.domain` — so a port that let that exception propagate would have left M6.3's route able to handle a denial *only* by importing the forbidden module. The denial is therefore declared in `correlation/ports/candidate_risk.py` as `CandidateRiskAccessDenied` and translated at the wrapper. The contracts are still untouched and this entry's own fix — `.application` in all eight, or an allow-list of `.ports` — remains unclaimed, so status is unchanged.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **the second trigger fired and the hole was again not taken — the second time this entry records that, and the first where taking it would have been the obvious move.** `risk_engine` needs candidate Risks, `correlation/ports/` published nothing, and `ListProjectRisksUseCase`'s docstring expects M6 to call `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` — an import of another module's `application/` that `lint-imports` passes green and rule 3 forbids. ADR-0005 decision 2 declines it and has `correlation` **publish its first port** instead, which `risk_engine/application/` consumes by inference. **The contracts are deliberately untouched**, so the hole is exactly as wide as it was: this entry's `Deferral rationale:` says the fix is an edit to eight contracts with a real design question behind it (add `.application` to all eight, or replace the forbidden lists with an allow-list of `.ports`), and a scoring ADR is not where that gets decided. Status unchanged. What has changed is the evidence: two modules have now been offered this shortcut and neither took it, which is an argument that the convention holds — and no argument at all that the gate does.

Note (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **deferred under Rules for M8 rule 1.** M8 ships product only, and this entry does not trace to M8's exit condition. Trigger: **the M8→M9 boundary review**.

### G36 — ADR-0023's matching rule says a no-signal key matches itself and the shipped code says it does not, so a live ADR sentence is contradicted by the module that implements it
Confirmed: M5.8, M5.2 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
