Blocks-if-unresolved: **somebody implements the relation the ADR literally describes, and every no-signal finding in a project silently collapses into one Risk instead of becoming one Risk each.** The mechanism is short: all no-signal keys in a project are equal by value — same `project_id`, `package` `None`, `url` `None` — so a `matches` that returns true for them, plus a grouping derived from that relation, buckets them together. On a real repository that is every Semgrep finding in the project fused into a single candidate Risk whose members share no remediation, which is the over-grouping this key's `file_path` exclusion exists to prevent, re-entering through the matching rule instead of the field list. **The failure is silent in the two places that would normally catch it.** The finding count still reconciles — every finding is still in exactly one group, so only the group count moves — and **the committed corpus cannot detect it at all**, because it contains exactly one no-signal finding: one group of one under either implementation, so the section 8 prediction and the test that measures it pass unchanged. The first evidence would be a Risk listing on a real project.
Deferral rationale: **the code is believed correct and the ADR text is what needs amending, so this is not a defect report against `src/`.** Reflexivity belongs to the grouping rather than to the relation: `group_by_match_key` puts every finding in a group, including a no-signal one, which is the singleton the ADR wants; making `matches` itself reflexive over no-signal keys can only be done by object identity, which would make the answer depend on whether a caller constructed the key once or twice. **The departure was directed rather than accidental** — it was proposed as an identity branch, reviewed, and removed deliberately, and `tests/unit/test_match_key.py` pins the chosen behaviour head-on rather than letting it hold by accident. What is left undone is the ADR: section 2 of its 2026-08-26 amendment still describes a no-signal key as matching only itself and calls the alternative reading a denial of reflexivity, and that sentence is now false about the code. **Not born assigned**, and the reason is the same one that keeps it out of this commit: amending an accepted ADR is a dated `## Amendments` edit, which is an act this issue explicitly reserves for a departing field list, and the field list did not depart. **Nothing mechanical catches this** — the ADR's own amendment rule fires on the field list only, `mypy` and `lint-imports` see prose, and `check_claims.py` does not read `docs/adr/`. Whoever takes it should decide which of the two framings the ADR keeps rather than bolting a second one on: either reflexivity is restated as the grouping's property, or the identity-based reading is argued for and the code changes.
**A second sentence in this same ADR is false and is recorded here because nothing else covers it** — section 5 of the 2026-08-26 amendment, arguing why `file_path` is out, says Semgrep contributes nothing to correlation *"precisely **because** `file_path` is excluded — it is Semgrep's only populated location field"*. It is not: `mappers/semgrep.py` populates `file_path`, `start_line` **and** `end_line`. The conclusion is unaffected — the key carries none of the three, so every Semgrep finding still has no signal — but the stated reason is wrong, and it **propagated into `src/` once**, into `correlation/domain/match_key.py`'s `has_signal` docstring and into the corpus test, both **corrected at the point of writing** rather than left to be found later. **Not corrected in the ADR here**, which would be a fourth correction in a commit that fixed three by an explicit ceiling. It sits in this entry rather than its own because this entry already is the record of ADR-0023 sentences the shipped code contradicts, and because a commit message is not re-read at a milestone boundary — which is G28's shape, and the reason a disposition living only in one would have been no record at all. Whoever amends section 2 should take this sentence in the same pass.
Re-entry trigger: **M5.2**, the next code in this module and the first that would encode the relation into a schema, or any re-reading of that amendment's section 2.
Note (2026-08-26, M5.2): **the trigger fired and produced nothing, which is the useful outcome rather than a miss.** ADR-0025 decision 1 encodes the relation into no schema at all, so the over-grouping this entry describes has no persistence path to enter through and the entry is untouched by M5.2. The trigger moves with the table, to the first issue that writes one.

### G37 — A Risk upsert copying `Finding`'s shape would clobber a user's dismissal, and the transcription that makes it look safe expires at the issue that adds one
Confirmed: M5.2, M6.1, M7.2 · Status: assigned → M8.1
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the first re-correlation after a person dismisses a Risk silently un-dismisses it, and every mechanical check in this repo stays green while it happens.** ADR-0020 decision 3 states its own expiry condition — the `findings` upsert is a faithful transcription of `merge_observation` *only* because that function's refresh set is total except the two surrogate ids — and names the fields that end it: *"a dismissal, an owner, a suppression. Such a field belongs on `Risk` (M5/M6)"*. **M8.1 ships exactly those** (`ResolveRiskUseCase` / `DismissRiskUseCase` with required reason). So whoever writes the first `Risk` upsert by copying the `findings` one inherits a `SET` clause that reads every column from `EXCLUDED`, which is correct for a value derived from findings and destroys a value a person set. The failure is invisible in the two places that would normally catch it: the row still exists and the count still reconciles, so only the dismissal flag moves, and there is no test that could fail because the field does not exist yet at the moment the pattern is copied. ADR-0025 decision 1 is what keeps this latent — M5.2 persists nothing, so there is no upsert to get wrong today.
Deferral rationale: **there is nothing to fix yet, and building the protection now would be protecting a column that does not exist** — the speculative shape ADR-016 decision 3 refuses and ADR-0021 refused again for `skipped_count`. What is owed is a decision at the moment the first row is written, not a mechanism now. **Not born assigned**, and the reason is that the forcing issue and the optional one are different: **M8.1** is forced, because a dismissal cannot be recomputed; **M6.3** is not — a score is derived from stored inputs exactly as the grouping is, so persisting one there is a choice, and taking it means taking this obligation early. Whoever writes that row decides and records which columns a re-correlation may not overwrite, and makes it a property of the statement rather than a convention — the shape ADR-0020 decision 1 chose for `findings` by omitting the identity inputs from `set_` so that "identity is frozen" is structural instead of a coincidence. Re-entry trigger: **the first `Risk` row written**, whichever issue writes it.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **M6 does not write the first `Risk` row, so this stays latent and its trigger is unmoved.** ADR-0005 decision 3 scores per request and persists nothing, which is ADR-0025 decisions 1 and 2 applied rather than reopened — so the optional write this entry names at **M6.3** is still optional and still untaken, and **M8.1** remains the forced one. Recorded because this entry's whole exposure is that somebody writes a `Risk` upsert by copying the `findings` one, and M6 was the first milestone with a plausible reason to; it did not.
Note (2026-09-16, M6→M7 boundary): **M8.1 as the first forced write is QUALIFIED, not struck.** M7.2 may be forced to store a Risk to key a `SecurityBrief` on it (**G68**); if it is, this protected-field obligation arrives with that write, one milestone early.
Note (2026-09-17, M7.2 commit 1): **the qualification above is discharged. M7.2 writes no Risk row.**
- **The decision.** ADR-0033 decision 1 gives a Brief its own surrogate id and holds the Risk's ordered finding-id set as data.
- **Why no protected field arises.** A narrative describes one member set and must not follow the Risk when membership changes. ADR-0025's 2026-09-17 amendment replaces decision 2's premise with *"cannot be recomputed and must stay attached to it when its membership changes"*, and only values of that second kind make a protected field necessary.
- **Result.** The trigger stays *the first `Risk` row written*, and M8.1 is again the forced issue.

Assignment (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **assigned to M8.1.** M8.1 writes the first `Risk` row, the event this entry's trigger names.

### G38 — ADR-0017 asserts twice that M5's correlation is scan-scoped, which ADR-0023 falsified two commits before anything noticed
Confirmed: M5.2, M6.3 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **an accepted ADR gives a false premise as the ground for a decision that is probably still right, so the next reader either trusts the premise or reopens a settled question.** `docs/adr/0017-normalization-trigger-and-pipeline-progress.md` argues against a generic `(scan_id, stage)` pipeline-progress table in two places — its decision about where progress lives, and its *Alternatives considered* — and both rest on *"M5's correlation is scan-scoped, but M6's scoring and M7's brief are **Risk-scoped**"*. ADR-0023's 2026-08-26 amendment section 1 decided correlation is **per-project**, on four grounds. The rejection's conclusion very likely survives, since M6 and M7 are still Risk-scoped and one of three stages still does not generalize — but a conclusion whose stated ground is false is not a conclusion anybody can check, and the two sentences are the load-bearing half of an argument this repo will re-run the moment a second module wants a stage record.
Deferral rationale: **not this commit's to correct, on three grounds, and the second is the one that decides it.** (1) This commit does not falsify those sentences — `e19d3b0` did, and its own disposition sweep enumerated five subjects without reaching them. *(**Three commits sit between `e19d3b0` and `63e7760`**, the M5.2 commit that noticed: `e0318e1` and `37165d9`, both of which edited documents and touched neither site, and `d3ca532`, a CI retrigger with an empty tree. `git rev-list e19d3b0..63e7760 --count` reports 4, counting `63e7760` itself. **Both endpoints are fixed shas, and that is the point of this parenthetical.** Two earlier drafts got the number wrong in two different ways — first "four", counted in somebody's head; then a count anchored to `HEAD`, which was right when written and falsified within the hour by the commit that wrote it. The title reads "two commits" for the two that shipped documents without noticing; `d3ca532` changed nothing and is excluded there and included in the `rev-list` figure, which is why both numbers appear.)* That miss is **G32**'s thesis confirmed on a second file: the enumeration under-reports, not merely the search that produced it. (2) ADR-0017 is **Accepted**, so correcting it is a dated `## Amendments` edit — the same act **G36** declines for ADR-0023 and for the same reason, and one an implementation commit should not perform on a document it is not otherwise touching. (3) It needs a trigger a sweep cannot give it, because no sweep owns re-reading accepted ADRs for premises a later ADR moved. **Not born assigned.** Whoever takes it should decide whether the rejection survives its ground changing rather than only repairing the sentence — and should check the same amendment against ADR-0017's other scope claims in one pass. Re-entry trigger: **the next change to ADR-0017**, or the next issue that wants a pipeline-progress record for a second stage, which is **M6.3**.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.3): **the trigger named this issue and its condition is NOT met, which is the note.** This entry's second trigger is *"the next issue that wants a pipeline-progress record for a second stage, which is **M6.3**"*. M6.3 wants none: ADR-0030 decision 1 ships a read surface, ADR-0005 decision 3 has scoring persist nothing, and a stage that writes no row has no progress to record — so there is no second stage here to generalize a `(scan_id, stage)` table over, and ADR-0017's rejected alternative is not re-opened by anything this issue built. Recorded rather than left silent, because an entry that names an issue by number and then goes unmentioned when that issue ships is indistinguishable from an entry nobody re-read — which is the miss the M5→M6 boundary review found **seven** times. **Nothing is acted on and nothing is struck:** the two ADR-0017 sentences are still false, and the first trigger — *the next change to ADR-0017* — is unmoved and remains the one that will fire. Status unchanged.

### G39 — ADR-0026's profile is derived from a corpus whose shape a ruleset or scan-plan change moves, with nothing announcing the move
Confirmed: M5.3, M5.4 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the fixture-shape check goes on asserting a field's presence profile after the thing that produced that profile has changed, and it reports green while doing it.** ADR-0026 decision 1 derives its profile at run time from the three committed real fixtures in `tests/fixtures/scanners/`. That profile is a fact about **one scan configuration**, not about the tools. Two rows are live today and **both fail the same way, which is why the trigger below is keyed on the configuration rather than on either of them.** **ZAP's `parameter` is SOMETIMES (4/13)**, and 4/13 is a property of a spider-plus-passive plan — see that directory's README section *"ZAP ran passive-only — the absence of an injection finding is by design"*. **Semgrep's `cwe` is NEVER at `n = 1`**, and it is the row ADR-0026 exists to mechanise — M5.3's own entry above instructs that *"Semgrep findings carry `cwe = None` in production until G6 is resolved, so a fixture set giving them CWEs proves a signal that does not exist"*. M5.4's `activeScan` job changes what ZAP emits against a real target; resolving G6 means editing the pinned ruleset so it declares metadata. **Neither touches a committed fixture, so in both cases the profile does not move, the pinned membership assertion stays green, and the check goes on describing a configuration production no longer runs.** The harm begins at the configuration change and becomes visible only at a re-capture — the one event that moves the row and turns the assertion red, which ADR-0026's Consequences records as the guard working. Absent that, nothing turns any colour.
Deferral rationale: **this is the deferred-with-a-trigger obligation ADR-0026 creates, seeded in the same commit per `CLAUDE.md`'s working-style rule, and it cannot be discharged now because neither event has happened.** The profile is correct about the corpus it reads; what is missing is a way for it to notice that the corpus stopped describing production. **Not born assigned**, and deliberately: M5.4's scope is the scan plan and consent, G6's fixes are a scanning-configuration change, and neither issue's job is auditing a check in `correlation`'s test suite — this is a trigger on somebody else's issue rather than work inside one. Whoever takes it re-derives the profile and either re-pins the membership literals with the new reading behind them, or records why the old one still holds. **Re-entry trigger, keyed on this entry's own subject rather than on one instance of it: any change to a pinned scanner ruleset or scan plan, or any re-capture of a fixture in `tests/fixtures/scanners/`.** The width is the point, and it follows from the mechanism rather than from caution: what breaks the profile is a change to the configuration that produced it, and a re-capture is merely when that break becomes observable. A trigger naming only the scan plan and the re-capture — the two obvious ones — would miss G6 entirely, because a ruleset change is neither.
Note: **distinct from G20, and the difference is which side is unchecked.** G20 is about a property of *committed real data* — identifier presence — that two ADRs assert and no test asserts, so a re-capture falsifies a document. This is the mirror: a *derived* profile silently ceasing to describe its source. G20's fix is a three-line assertion over the fixtures; this one's is not an assertion at all, since the check already asserts everything it can — it is a question about whether the input still means what it meant. **Distinct from G31 too**, which they will be confused with because both fire at M5.4: G31 is about the **match key over-splitting** under an active plan, a property of grouping; this is about the **profile the check reads**, a property of measurement. M5.4 fires both, for unrelated reasons, and resolving either leaves the other open.
Note (2026-08-27, M5.4): **the trigger fired — the pinned scan plan changed — and it fires exactly the way this entry predicts it would.** `_build_plan_yaml` now emits an `activeScan` job when consent is in force, and `passiveScan-wait`'s ceiling dropped from 5 minutes to 2. Neither edit touches a committed fixture, so ADR-0026's profile still derives from the same three captures, the pinned membership assertion is still green, and the profile now describes a plan production runs on **neither** branch: the `activeScan` job is added for a consented project, and `passiveScan-wait`'s ceiling drops from 5 to 2 for every project regardless of consent. **Nothing turned any colour**, which is this entry's own description of the harm rather than a reassurance. Re-derivation waits on M5.9's capture, the event this entry names as the one that moves the row. Not discharged.

### G40 — 41 of 44 hand-written `Finding` shape values in `tests/` diverge from the corpus profile, and the check ADR-0026 specifies will not see any of them
Confirmed: M5.3 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **nothing today, and the entry says so rather than inventing a consequence — what it costs is a false impression of coverage the moment somebody reads ADR-0026 as a statement about `tests/`.** ADR-0026 decision 5 scopes the check to M5.3's own fixture module by explicit reference. Every value below is outside it, and stays outside it. The point of recording them is that they establish the base rate: writing a `Finding` by hand and getting production's shape is the exception here, not the rule. **Reported by mechanism rather than by count, because a count says nothing about the shape of the fix — and the decomposition says twenty-eight of the forty-one are two violation sets reachable through seven factory defaults, not twenty-eight per-value edits.** MEASURED at `444cd4635ef9ba0bef96a5aa702d48fe176003a4`:
- **19** — Semgrep-sourced, missing `end_line` alone. **Spread across five factories, not one.**
- **9** — Trivy-sourced, missing `cwe` and `cvss`. **Two factories**: `test_list_project_risks.py` 5, `test_risks_routes.py` 4.
- **3** — Semgrep-sourced, missing `start_line` and `end_line`.
- **3** — Trivy-sourced, missing `installed_version`, `cwe` and `cvss`.
- **7 singletons**, each its own violation set: four Semgrep-sourced values carrying scalars Semgrep never populates (`cwe`, `owasp_category`, `cvss` in three combinations, one of them also missing `end_line`, one also carrying `installed_version`), and three Trivy-sourced values all missing `installed_version`, `cwe` and `cvss`, of which two additionally carry line numbers Trivy never emits (one `start_line` alone, one both) and drop `package`, while the third instead drops `file_path` and carries no line numbers at all.
- **3 conform.** All Semgrep-sourced. 44 distinct values in total; 41 diverge.
**The method is stated as a predicate rather than as a label, because a figure a reader cannot re-run has the defect this entry exists to record.** A value is one distinct combination of the source tool with the eleven profile fields — `dataclasses.fields(Location)` plus `cwe`, `owasp_category`, `cvss`. Counted are constructions whose **immediate caller frame is under `tests/`**, which is the eight `return Finding(` sites inside one per-file factory each — `_finding` in seven files (`tests/integration/test_findings_routes.py`, `tests/integration/test_postgres_finding_repository.py`, `tests/unit/test_correlate_findings.py`, `tests/unit/test_finding.py`, `tests/unit/test_finding_merge.py`, `tests/unit/test_list_project_findings.py`, `tests/unit/test_list_project_risks.py`) and **`_trivy_finding`** in `tests/integration/test_risks_routes.py`. Excluded is everything reaching the constructor through a mapper frame, and every rehydration in `_finding_to_domain` — a wider filter admits 30 further values, all of them round-trips of corpus-derived findings through the Postgres adapter and `merge_observation`, none hand-written and none diverging.
Deferral rationale: **recorded, not fixed, and deliberately not assigned.** Fixing it means editing test factories in a documentation commit, and while the two largest groups are factory defaults rather than per-value edits, **they are not one default each**: the nineteen `end_line` values are spread over five factories and the nine `cwe`/`cvss` values over two, so twenty-eight values cost seven edits across seven files. Cheap work, but its cost is entirely in re-verifying those files, which no issue in M5 owns. **Not born assigned**, and the reason is that the value of fixing it is unclear rather than merely unfunded: several of these factories construct a deliberately minimal `Finding` because the test's subject is something else entirely, and giving them production shape would be noise in the diff and noise in the test. Whoever takes it should decide per factory whether shape fidelity is worth anything to that file's subject, rather than sweeping all eight. **Extension trigger, and it is the field that matters more than the count: the next module that writes a `Finding` by hand.** ADR-0026's check is scoped to M5.3's fixture module, so it does not reach M5.4's fixtures, M6's, or any of these eight. That is the shape **G19** records about itself — *"A comment in `seed_findings_benchmark.py` cannot reach M5.3, because M5.3 will write a different file."* — arriving one level up, in a check rather than in a comment.
Note (M5.3): **this entry's predicate and its enumeration now disagree, and the disagreement is not corrected here.** M5.3 added twelve hand-written values in `tests/unit/test_correlation_accuracy.py`'s `_curated`, all conforming; the predicate above admits them, while the enumeration of eight `return Finding(` sites does not reach them, because that constructor call is nested inside a `return CuratedFinding(`. So the undated ratio in this entry's title is no longer current. **Not re-derived here** — re-deriving is a measurement this commit did not take, and asserting a figure without re-deriving it is this entry's own subject.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **the extension trigger — "the next module that writes a `Finding` by hand" — is reached at M6.2.** ADR-0005 decision 5 obliges that issue to construct `UNKNOWN`- and `CRITICAL`-severity findings synthetically, because both are 0 of 34 and 0 of 37 in the captured corpora. Those values are outside ADR-0026's check by the scoping this entry already records, so they land in the class this entry counts rather than in the one the check sees. Not fixed and not assigned here, per this entry's own rationale; M6.2's text carries the obligation, and whoever writes those fixtures should decide shape fidelity per factory rather than sweeping. The undated ratio in this entry's title is still not re-derived, for the reason its M5.3 note gives.

### G41 — Three ADR-0026 clauses could not be satisfied as written, and for two of them this entry is the only record
Confirmed: M5.3, M5→M6 boundary · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **small, and it should read as small: a reader checking ADR-0026 against `tests/unit/test_correlation_accuracy.py` finds three clauses with no matching implementation and has to re-derive why each time.** *Decision 1* — *"Every profile row carries its denominator in the check's own output"* — and *decision 3* — the `parameter` exclusion *"named in the check's output so that a reader of a **passing** run knows the field was not examined"* — both ask for output a passing run does not surface, because pytest captures stdout and shows it only on failure; what stands in is `_EXPECTED_DENOMINATORS` and the failure messages for the first, and the name of `test_the_pinned_profile_holds_and_zap_parameter_is_the_only_row_no_fixture_is_checked_against` for the second. *Decision 2*'s first bullet calls the union layer LIVE and formulates it as the profile's field set against `dataclasses.fields(Location)` plus the three scalars — which is `A == A`, since the profile is built from exactly those two sources; what shipped instead asserts a partition of `dataclasses.fields(Finding)`, which is live, and a new `Location` field is caught by the pinned-membership layer. **Nothing is unchecked in any of the three; what is missing is that an accepted ADR says something the code does not do.** The decision 2 departure is recorded in `test_every_finding_field_is_a_shape_scalar_the_location_or_neither`'s docstring as well; **for decisions 1 and 3 this entry is the only record**, and nothing in the module points at either.
Deferral rationale: **not born assigned, because each fix is larger than the gap it closes and none is M5.3's to decide** — the two output clauses need either a global pytest terminal-output change or new `conftest.py` machinery for one line of text, and decision 2's formulation needs a dated amendment to an accepted ADR. Re-entry trigger: **any amendment to ADR-0026, or any change to this repository's pytest terminal-output configuration** — the first is when correcting decision 2 stops being an out-of-band edit, and the second is when the cheaper of the two output fixes stops being a change nobody asked for.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **the trigger fired at `2725ba1` (M5.4), which added `## Amendments` to ADR-0026, and was not recorded here.** The three clauses are unchanged. M6.2's hand-built fixtures are the next place ADR-0026's check could reach, carried in that issue.

### G42 — ADR-0024's blast-radius table is re-derivable since M5.9 committed a capture, and decision 5's parameter spellings are verifiable by no capture at all
Confirmed: M5.4, M5.9 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the ADR that decides what consent authorises rests entirely on numbers no reader can check, and the reader who tries cannot tell a transcription error from a measurement.** ADR-0024's table states each figure as the predicate that produced it — log lines, window, exclusions — which is what makes the numbers *re-runnable in principle*, and that is precisely as far as it goes: the access log, the ZAP report and the probe's working tree are not committed and not tracked, so no predicate can actually be run. Two named consequences. **The bounds in decision 5 are unverified**: `maxScanDurationInMins`, `maxRuleDurationInMins` and `threadPerHost` are ZAP Automation Framework parameter names taken from that framework rather than from a plan this repository has executed, so a misspelling would be accepted silently by ZAP and the job would run unbounded with the ADR asserting otherwise. **And the 62 s input to M5.7's projection is a hand-run on one developer machine**, which that issue's own bullet already flags as *"a projection and not a reading"* — this entry is the register's record of why it stays one.
Deferral rationale: **it cannot be discharged now, because discharging it is M5.9's deliverable rather than a document fix.** Committing the probe's own logs would be worse than the gap: they were produced by a plan the shipped adapter does not have, which is the trap `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md`'s ZAP row already records — an artifact that looks re-capturable by the documented procedure and is not. **Not born assigned, deliberately**: M5.4's scope is the plan and the consent decision, and the capture that makes these figures checkable is M5.9's. Re-entry trigger: **M5.9's committed active capture**, whose arrival is when every row of ADR-0024's table becomes re-derivable and when decision 5's parameter spellings become verifiable against a plan this repository has run. Whoever takes it re-derives each row against the new artifact and either restates it or records why the hand-run figure still stands.

Rewritten (2026-09-08, M5.9 commit 1) — **the two halves of that trigger have come apart, and each now blocks on a different kind of thing.** The paragraph above is left standing as the record of what was expected; this is what happened.

- **Half one — every row of ADR-0024's table re-derivable — is DISCHARGED.** `tests/integration/fixtures/active_scan/` commits the ZAP report and the target's own Werkzeug access log, which is the source that table's predicate column names for every row but the first, produced by the shipped `ZapAdapter` at its pinned digest on the plan `_build_plan_yaml` builds. ADR-0024's `## Amendments` carries the restated table. As predicted, the rows are **restated rather than confirmed** — 390 requests against 391, 86 paths against 86, 200×253/404×130/405×7 against 252/132/7, 7 malformed, 0 5xx — because the probe ran a plan that was not decision 5's.
- **Half two — decision 5's parameter spellings verifiable — is UNDISCHARGEABLE BY ANY CAPTURE, and that is the finding rather than a failure of this one.** A completed run proves the plan was *accepted*, not that a misspelled `maxScanDurationInMins` would have been *rejected*, and ZAP's console output is the only place an unrecognised job parameter is reported. `ZapAdapter.run` binds `_, stderr = await process.communicate()` and surfaces `stderr` only on a non-zero exit, so that output is discarded on every successful run. **No artifact closes this**: the entry has stopped waiting for a capture and started waiting for an **adapter change** — surfacing or persisting ZAP's console output — which is a different trigger, a different blast radius and somebody else's issue.
- **New re-entry trigger for the surviving half:** any change to how `ZapAdapter.run` handles the container's `stdout`/`stderr` on a **successful** exit, or any proposal to narrow the active-scan policy or alter decision 5's parameters — the second because changing an unverified parameter is when its being unverified starts to cost something. Whoever takes it should note the cheap version is not a test but a persisted artifact, and that ADR-0011 point 6 requires the output be redacted before it reaches a log or exception.
- **Not renumbered and not split into a second entry**, deliberately: the two halves share one subject — a live decision resting on unchecked numbers — and splitting them would leave the discharged half as a resolved entry that no longer names what is still open.

### G43 — Consent to attack a host leaves no history and no log, and `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §11.5 does not reach the act
Confirmed: M5.4 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the strongest authorisation act this system performs is the one it can say least about afterwards, and the question it cannot answer is the first one anybody asks.** ADR-0024 decision 1 records the current grant's actor and timestamp, so *"who consented to the target configured right now"* is answerable. Nothing answers *"was consent in force when scan X ran, and who granted it"* — a withdrawal overwrites the grant it replaces, a target change voids consent leaving no trace that it was ever in force, and `ScannerConfig.updated_at` is one timestamp for the whole row, so a consent grant and a scanner toggle are indistinguishable in it. There is no second record: `src/` contains zero `logging.getLogger` calls, so nothing is written anywhere else either. **`PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §11.5 is the principle that ought to cover this and does not** — it requires an actor and a timestamp for *"every risk state change (opened, dismissed, resolved)"*, and consenting to attack a host is not a risk state change. The gap is in the principle's scope, not in an implementation of it, which is why no amount of care in `projects` would have caught it.
Deferral rationale: **the shape of the fix is decided and its cost is not M5.4's to pay.** ADR-0024's `## Alternatives considered` records it: consent as its own entity with an append-only grant history, at which point decision 1's three columns become a projection of the newest row. That is a repository, a port, a read surface and a retention answer, for a project that has never granted consent once — and building it before the first grant would be designing an audit trail against no usage, which is the pre-normalizing ADR-016 decision 3 argues against in the adjacent case. **Not born assigned**: M5.9's scope is standing the target up and capturing, and no issue in M5 owns auditability. Re-entry trigger, and it is deliberately two conditions rather than one: **the first `Scan` that runs an active plan against a real target, or any change that widens `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §11.5's scope beyond risk state changes.** The first is when an unanswerable question starts being asked about a real event; the second is when the principle that should have covered this is being edited anyway, and skipping it then would be the same omission twice.

### G44 — Two captures of one target exist under two scan plans in two directories, and neither re-capture procedure reaches the other's files
Confirmed: M5.9 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **a ZAP image-digest bump or a scan-plan change re-captures the passive corpus by the documented procedure and silently leaves the active capture describing a ZAP the adapter no longer runs — in the artifact ADR-0024's blast-radius table is supposed to become checkable against.** `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md`'s *How to re-capture* is six numbered steps scoped to the three real files in that directory and reaching nothing else; ADR-0027 decision 1 puts the active capture under `tests/integration/fixtures/active_scan/` with a provenance README of its own. **Both captures are pinned to the same two things** — `ZapAdapter.docker_image`'s digest and the target SHA — **so a bump to either invalidates both, and only one procedure says so.** Which one a reader finds is not symmetric either: the corpus procedure is the one **G20** and **G39** already point at, so the active capture is the half that goes stale unattended. The failure is silent in the direction that matters, because the active capture is what makes **G42**'s figures re-derivable: ADR-0024's table would go on being checkable against a document produced by a ZAP that is no longer the pinned one — *"an artifact that looks re-capturable by the documented procedure and is not"*, which is G42's own words for the trap, arriving from the other side of it.
Deferral rationale: **created by ADR-0027 decision 1, seeded in the same commit per `CLAUDE.md`'s working-style rule, and not dischargeable in it — the second directory does not exist yet, so a cross-reference has nothing to point at.** The fix has a cheap half and a durable half, and naming both is the point of the entry. The cheap half is **M5.9's** and is owed by whoever writes the active capture's provenance README: a pointer in each direction, saying that a digest or plan change invalidates both captures and naming the other procedure. The durable half has no cheap fix — **a cross-reference is prose, and nothing mechanically detects that one of two captures has gone stale.** Neither the pinned digest nor the pinned plan is compared against anything a capture records, and `check_claims.py` has no notion of either. **Not born assigned**, deliberately: M5.9's scope is standing the target up, the capture and the measured step, and auditing two procedures against each other is work on somebody else's issue. Re-entry trigger, keyed on what actually invalidates a capture rather than on one instance of it: **any bump of `ZapAdapter.docker_image`'s pinned digest, any change to `_build_plan_yaml` or `_active_scan_jobs`, or any re-capture of either corpus.** Whoever takes it re-derives both captures against the current digest and plan, or records why the older one still stands.
Note (2026-09-08, M5.9 commit 1): **the cheap half is DISCHARGED and the durable half is untouched.** The active capture landed in `tests/integration/fixtures/active_scan/` and its provenance README was written, which is the event this entry's `Deferral rationale:` assigns the cheap half to. Both pointers exist: a blockquote in that README naming the corpus procedure, and one in `tests/fixtures/scanners/README.md` naming this one, each saying that a ZAP image-digest bump or a change to `_build_plan_yaml`/`_active_scan_jobs` invalidates **both** captures. **That is the whole of what was discharged, and it is prose.** The durable half stands exactly as written: nothing mechanically detects that one of two captures has gone stale, neither the pinned digest nor the pinned plan is compared against anything a capture records, and `check_claims.py` has no notion of either. **The entry stays open on its own re-entry trigger, unchanged.** One thing the capture makes concrete rather than hypothetical: the two procedures are now genuinely different in kind — the active one is a committed script (`scripts/capture_active_scan.py`) and the passive one is six prose steps that explicitly refuse to be one, so "re-take both" is not one operation done twice.
Note: **distinct from G39 and from G42, and it will be confused with both because all three are about a capture that stopped describing production.** G39 is about a **derived profile** silently ceasing to describe its source, and its fix is a re-derivation. G42 is about **one artifact's figures** not being re-derivable at all, and its fix is committing that artifact — which ADR-0027 decision 3 does. This is about **two procedures**, neither of which is wrong on its own, that no longer cover the same ground; it is created by the same decision that discharges G42, which is why it is opened in that decision's commit rather than found later.

### G45 — M5.9's test stands an RCE-vulnerable app on `0.0.0.0:8080` and adds ~70s to every `uv run pytest`, and the time is what will get it turned off
Confirmed: M5.9 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **two costs, and the second is the one that actually ends this demonstration.** *Exposure:* `tests/integration/test_active_scan_finds_the_sink.py` serves `verion-demo-target`, whose `/calculate?expr=` is remote code execution by design, on `0.0.0.0:8080` — LAN-reachable, under active attack by a ZAP container executing real shell commands — on **every machine that runs pytest**. **The window is bounded above by 70.45s and is not equal to it:** that figure is pytest's whole fixture-setup duration, which contains a `git clone` and a `uv` closure resolve during which nothing is bound to the port. The scan itself is the bulk of it — the committed capture's README records **65.5s** around `ZapAdapter.run()` with the target's own log spanning 60.0s — and the exposure is liveness-green to `stop_target`. Stated as a bound rather than as a measurement because this register's convention is to state each figure as the predicate that produced it, and no predicate here measured the window directly. ADR-0027's Consequences reasoned about one machine: *"The blast radius is the runner: an ephemeral VM, with the target subprocess started under a minimal environment (decision 2)."* Quoted whole because the clause after the comma is the mitigation, and the Note below is about exactly how far it reaches; a developer workstation is still not an ephemeral VM, and the ADR does not say it is. *Time:* the same ~70s is added to every local full-suite run — 657 tests in **166.09s** locally at this commit — and **63s** to every CI one (136s at `a745975`, run `34265788717`, against 73s at `25488a6`) — on a project whose author runs the suite each iteration. **The time cost is what disables this test, not the exposure.** Exposure is a thing somebody thinks about once; seventy seconds is a reason to reach for `-k` or a local skip in three weeks, and a deselection taken for convenience leaves no trace in any file — at which point this entry protected nothing and the demonstration is silently not running, which is the shape of **G29** one level up. Named here so that if it happens, it happens against a written record.
Deferral rationale: **the alternative that wins is not available yet, and it is not the one this decision rejected.** M5.9 runs the test unconditionally because a skip is itself a false negative that reads as green — the exact failure the test exists to detect — and no `skipif` marker exists anywhere in this suite, so the first one would be introduced for the single test least able to afford it. *(Stated as a property of the suite rather than as a grep result. `git grep -n "skipif" -- tests` was empty before this commit and is not now: M5.9's own test docstring contains the word, which is a small demonstration of why a command is a worse anchor than the fact it was standing in for.)* **The exit is a *guarded* opt-in and it is a different thing from the fragile one:** the test fails loudly outside CI unless an explicit variable is set, **and `check_claims.py` verifies `ci.yml` sets it**, so the failure mode of a bare opt-in — the variable quietly dropped and nothing running — cannot occur. That is strictly better than either unconditional or a bare marker, and it is not taken **today** because the guard does not exist and writing a `check_claims` check inside a test commit is the bundling `CLAUDE.md` forbids. **The guard has no owner, and M5.7 is not it** — that issue's scope bullet names its two artifacts as `ci.yml` and `CLAUDE.md`'s Tier 1 table and says in as many words to *"leave `check_claims.py` alone unless the split needs a new kind of assertion"*, which this would be and which its split does not need. So M5.7 is where the `ci.yml` half would naturally land and is explicitly not where the checking half does. **Trigger: the first time this test is deselected, marked, or proposed for either** — that is the event this entry exists to make visible — **or any `check_claims.py` change that makes the guard cheap to add.** Until then the disclosure *is* the mitigation, and `--deselect` is the intended escape because it is visible in a command line rather than invisible in a config.
Note: **what the environment envelope protects and what it has never protected**, recorded because M5.9 *adds* `HOME` to the child's allowlist and that reads as a weakening of something it is not. The envelope protects **secrets held in environment variables**, which is measured rather than hypothetical — the capture shell for M5.9 commit 1 carried a live `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN`, and the CI job carries `DATABASE_URL` and `REDIS_URL`; `os.environ` inheritance would hand all three to a process with a working RCE sink, which is why the allowlist is built from empty and the driver's deny pattern is re-asserted over it. It has **never** protected the **filesystem**: with `HOME` unset, `os.path.expanduser` falls back to `pwd.getpwuid(os.getuid()).pw_dir` (verified in `posixpath.expanduser`'s own source, not assumed), so a payload through the `eval()` sink reaches `~/.ssh` either way. `HOME` is added because uv keeps its cache and its managed Python 3.11 under it, and it costs nothing the envelope was providing.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **M5.7 moved to M11.1.** Read this entry's M5.7 as M11.1. Its scope bullet moved with it unchanged, so the guard still has no owner.

### G46 — ADR-0023 Decision C's trigger table still says no `activeScan` job has shipped, which M5.4 falsified and M5.9 falsified again — and two sibling restatements of that ADR are stale in the same way
Confirmed: M5.9 · Status: resolved → M5.6
Resolution: M5.6 commit 3 (2026-09-15).
- **Struck in this commit:**
  - Decision C's G24 row, in ADR-0023.
  - The `M5.5 (validity, G27)` phrase in ADR-0023's 2026-08-25 amendment.
  - Section 6's two stated grounds, in ADR-0023. Section 6's decision stands, because its own trigger fired.
  - `ARCHITECTURE.md` §12's *"both triggers remain open"*.
- **Where the reasoning lives:** ADR-0023's 2026-09-15 amendment, which strikes the three ADR sites in place and records why.
- **What made that amendment happen was ADR-0023 section 8, not this entry's trigger.** The trigger reads *"M5.6 resolving the conditioning half of G27"*, and it has NOT fired: G27 is assigned to M5.6 commit 4. Section 8's own rule required an amendment as soon as the key diverged from its prediction, and that amendment is where these sites were paid off.
- **Left standing:** G24's own dated `Note (2026-08-25, active-scan probe)`, the non-ADR site this entry names. It is a dated note, and stays one.
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the table a reader consults to find out whether the SAST↔DAST derivation is unblocked says one of its two triggers is unfired, and it is.** `docs/adr/0023-correlation-match-key.md`'s Decision C defers that derivation behind two triggers — **G24** for existence, **G27** for validity — and the G24 row reads *"Measured satisfiable by the probe above and still open — no `activeScan` job has shipped."* M5.4 shipped it behind consent; M5.9 sets G24 to `resolved → M5.9`. So the row now understates how far the deferral has moved, in the direction that matters: somebody deciding whether M5.6's derivation is reachable reads "still open" for a trigger that is closed, and concludes two things are missing when one is. **The same sentence survives in a second place, and that one is not an ADR:** G24's own `Note (2026-08-25, active-scan probe)` says *"both triggers remain open … No `activeScan` job has shipped, so no production scan yields the DAST half yet"*, inside `ROADMAP.md`, which **is** one of `check_claims.py`'s `LIVE_DOCS`. It is left standing as a dated note recording what was true when written — the treatment this register gives a field that has become dated rather than wrong — but a reader who only fixes the ADR will have fixed half of this. The opposite error is the dangerous one and this entry must not cause it — **G27 is genuinely unfired**, `assigned → M5.5 (declaration) + M5.6 (conditioning)` since 2026-09-09 *(this citation read `assigned → M5.5` until ADR-0028 split the entry; updated here rather than left as a literal quote of a value that changed)*, and a correlation built on a resolved G24 alone is exactly the *confidently wrong* pair G27's own Assignment note warns about.
Deferral rationale: **fixing it is an ADR amendment, and neither commit that falsified it had ADR-0023 in scope.** An accepted ADR is not rewritten inline in this project — M5.8's bullet states the convention — so the fix is a dated `## Amendments` entry, which is work on ADR-0023 rather than on M5.4's or M5.9's subject. **Recorded here rather than only inside G24's `Resolution:`, and the reason is mechanical**: `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` treats `Status: resolved →` exactly as it treats `assigned →`, so a resolved entry is exempt from escalation outright whatever it says, and it has no live trigger for anyone to arrive by. *(It does keep its `Blocks-if-unresolved:` — every resolved entry here does, which is the register's own rule — so the problem is reachability, not the fields.)* Nothing mechanical will catch the ADR sentence either way: that script reads no file under `docs/adr/` at all, by design, stated in its own module docstring. **Trigger: the next amendment to ADR-0023 for any reason, or M5.6 resolving the conditioning half of G27** — the second is the one that matters, because at that point both triggers are discharged and Decision C's deferral ends, and a table that misreports one of them is the thing a reader would act on. ***(Re-pointed 2026-09-09. It read "or M5.5 resolving G27", which ADR-0028 made unreachable in the commit that split G27 across two issues: M5.5 no longer resolves it, so the trigger this entry calls the one that matters could never have fired as written. A live conditional rendered inert by a later change is the class `CLAUDE.md`'s ADR-and-register bullet exists for, and it arrived here inside the same commit that created it.)*** **Two further sites carry the same staleness and are named here so one amendment catches all of them rather than half:** ADR-0023's own **2026-08-25 amendment** says *"`ROADMAP.md` schedules the three pieces as M5.4 (existence, behind consent), **M5.5 (validity, G27)** and M5.6 (the derivation itself)"*, which the split falsifies; and **`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s ADR-0023 summary bullet** still says *"while **both triggers remain open**, G24 has changed character"*, which M5.9's resolution of G24 falsified before this commit and which that file's re-dated `**Last updated:**` line now certifies as current.
Note (2026-09-09, M5.6 commit 1): **a THIRD stale site in ADR-0023 is added to this entry's enumeration, and it is not the same sentence as the two above.** The two named so far are about **G24's trigger row** saying no `activeScan` job has shipped. This one is about **section 6 of the same 2026-08-26 amendment**, whose deferral of a path key rests on *"No measurement of a path-keyed grouping exists … and nothing in this repo normalizes URLs"* — **both clauses now false**, the first by M5.9's committed active capture and the second by ADR-0029 decision 4, which puts a path derivation at the `build_match_key` call site. It is added here rather than given its own entry because the fix is identical and indivisible: one dated `## Amendments` entry on ADR-0023 discharges all three, and three entries pointing at one amendment would be the register over-reporting the way G32 records G28's list under-reporting. **Section 6's decision itself is NOT falsified** and must not be read as such — it says the key is not changed to a path *here*, with *"Re-entry trigger: **M5.4**, seeded as **G31**"*, and ADR-0029 decision 4 is that trigger firing as designed. Only its stated ground has gone stale. **This entry's trigger is unchanged and still has not fired**: this commit does not amend ADR-0023 and does not resolve G27's conditioning half, which is M5.6 commit 3's.
Note: **distinct from G39 and G44, all three being stale-artifact entries, and the separation is the usual G9/G10 one.** G39 is a **derived profile** ceasing to describe its source, fixed by re-deriving. G44 is **two capture procedures** that no longer cover the same ground, fixed by auditing them against each other. This is **one sentence in an accepted ADR** that a later commit made false, fixed by an amendment — the class `CLAUDE.md`'s working-style bullet names when it says a Consequences section is not re-read at a milestone boundary. Its nearest neighbour is not in this register at all: it is ADR-0024's *"not re-derivable from this repository"* paragraph, struck at M5.9 commit 1 for the same reason by the commit that falsified it.

### G47 — M5.5's declaration voids on RECONFIGURATION and never on DRIFT, and nothing in the system observes what the target is actually running
Confirmed: M5.5, M5→M6 boundary · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **M5.6's gate reports "declared" for a deployment that has drifted arbitrarily far from the declared branch, which is G27's own failure mode surviving inside G27's fix.** ADR-0028 decision 2 binds the declaration to three observable strings — `ScannerConfig.zap_target_url`, `ConnectedRepo.url`, `ConnectedRepo.default_branch` — compared verbatim against the values stored when a person declared. Editing any of the three voids it. **Deploying different code to the same URL does not, and no component would notice**: nothing fetches the deployed revision, `Scan` records no commit for the tree side either (**G25**), and ADR-0023 Decision A refused the one signal that would have hinted at the deployment's identity, the `Server` banner, on grounds this ADR leaves standing. So once M5.6's conditioning ships, a green gate means *a person declared this coupling and nobody has edited either side since* — and a reader of a produced SAST↔DAST group will take it to mean *these two findings are about one system*, which is exactly the substitution G27's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` calls the dangerous one. The distance between the two is unbounded and nothing measures it.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is unbuilt rather than unavailable, and the nearest thing to it does not close this.** Detecting drift needs the deployed revision, and there are three shapes for it, none scheduled: a build-info endpoint, which this project does not require of a scan target and could not require of somebody else's; a deployment-metadata port; and — the one worth naming precisely, because ADR-0028 decision 2 originally described it as foreclosed and does not now — **a Security Context deployment-signal read of the `Server` banner.** ADR-0023 Decision A refused that banner as a `Location.package` **correlation signal** and explicitly left the other use open, naming Security Context as its home: *"a live server's self-reported stack is a fact about the deployment rather than the location of a finding."* Its worth would still be bounded by that decision's perverse property — the banner exists only while the target carries the vulnerability the signal is derived from — so it is a weak third option and not a refused one. **G25 is explicitly NOT the fix**, and reading it as one is the error this entry exists to prevent: a commit on `Scan` versions the *tree* side at scan time and leaves the *URL* side unversioned, so the pair stays half-observed and the drift this entry names is entirely on the unversioned half. ADR-0028 decision 2 states that reasoning at its own site rather than deferring to here. Trigger: **M5.6's gate landing** — at which point this stops being a property of an unread column and becomes a property of a produced group — or any change that gives the system a view of what the target is running, a build-info read and a deployment-metadata port being the two shapes that would.
Note: **distinct from G25 and from G27, and the three are easy to fold and must not be.** **G25** is *which tree the scanner read*, whose failure is cross-scan and lands on M9.1's resolution detection. **G27** is *whether the URL and the tree are the same system at all*, whose fix is the declaration and the gate. **This is what the declaration cannot see once it exists** — the residue of G27's fix rather than G27 itself — and it is created by the fix rather than found alongside it. Three different `Blocks-if-unresolved:` fields; closing either of the others leaves this one open.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary, M5.6): **the trigger fired and was not recorded here** — M5.6's gate landed at `0d4f027`, and production derives since `2318d87`. Nothing changed about drift: the gate still voids on reconfiguration only.

### G48 — `ServingDeclarationPort` is decided at M5.5 and ships at M5.6, so between the two issues the declaration has no cross-module reader
Confirmed: M5.5 · Status: resolved → M5.6
Resolution: M5.6 commit 3 (2026-09-15) — `ServingDeclarationPort` ships with its consumer. The trigger fired when M5.6 commit 3 shipped the port, not as a descope. M5.6 itself has not landed, and G27 is held open on exactly that ground.
- **The port:** `projects/ports/serving_declaration.py`, one method, with ADR-0028 decision 4's exact signature.
- **The consumer:** `CorrelateFindingsUseCase` reads it from `correlation/application/`.
- **The conformance site:** `get_serving_declaration_port` in `platform/di.py` returns `PostgresServingDeclarationVerdictReader` against the port annotation. ADR-015 names that as the place where `mypy --strict` actually checks an adapter against its port.
- **The issue is now four commits.** This entry's 2026-09-09 Note says three, and is dated rather than wrong.
- **Not discharged here:** production's route map is empty until commit 4. **G27** carries that. This entry's subject was a declaration nothing read, and it now has a reader.
  - *(Dated 2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4: production's route map is no longer empty. It is stored at context build and read by `PostgresRouteMapReader`, and **G27** is resolved. This entry's resolution is unaffected.)*
Blocks-if-unresolved: **if M5.6 slips or is descoped, M5.5 has shipped exactly the thing its own struck bullet warned against — a column nothing reads — and the register would carry no entry saying so.** ADR-0028 decision 4 fixes the port's shape (`projects/ports/`, one method, `async def url_serves_scanned_tree(*, project_id: str) -> bool` — async by rule 7, since evaluating it reads three rows — read from `correlation/application/` and never from `correlation/domain/`) and defers shipping it to the issue that has a caller, on ADR-015's bound: a port with no consumer has no `di.py` factory anything depends on and no site where an adapter meets a port-annotated return type, so `mypy --strict` reports green over a file without having checked what the file exists for. That is the right call for a port and the wrong situation to leave unrecorded, because the two halves of the decision live in two issues and only one of them is scheduled to produce something a reviewer sees.
Deferral rationale: **shipping the port at M5.5 is the alternative, and it is worse in a way this project has already named.** An unconsumed port would be the mechanical-check-decays-into-theatre shape `CLAUDE.md`'s type-suppression baseline exists to detect one level down — green, uncalled, and indistinguishable from a working one. The other alternative, deferring the *decision* as well as the shipping, is worse still: it would let whoever writes M5.6 choose the port's shape while also writing the tests that accept it, which is the ordering ADR-0023's amendment section 3 rejected for the match key. So the decision lands early and the file lands late, which is the split this entry records. Trigger: **M5.6 landing**, which discharges it, **or M5.6 being descoped, deferred or split** — at which point the declaration's status as an unread column becomes live rather than transitional and this entry is the thing that says so.
Note (2026-09-09, M5.6 commit 1): **the trigger has not fired, and that is worth stating because this commit is inside M5.6.** The issue ships in **three commits** — ADR-0029, then the extractor, then the key entry with the port and the gate — and a multi-commit issue is none of the three things the trigger names. **The port is still not shipped and `di.py` still carries the deliberate gap it documents**, so this entry's `Blocks-if-unresolved:` holds unchanged and in full; **commit 3** discharges it. Recorded rather than left to inference, because a reader arriving between commits sees an issue underway beside an entry that looks as though it should already have moved. ADR-0029's Consequences says the same at its own site.

### G49 — Owner-gating makes one person the author of both M5.4's consent and M5.5's declaration, which M5.4's own bullet says are two different people's claims
Confirmed: M5.5 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the independence M5.4's bullet relies on is documentary rather than enforced, so the failure it rules out by design is available in practice.** That bullet states the two are *"two different claims, made by two different people, about two different things"* — *"you may attack this host"* from whoever owns the target, *"this URL serves this repository"* from whoever knows the deployment — and concludes that *"one field would make each imply the other."* ADR-0028 decision 3 keeps the two **records** separate, with two voiding rules and two acceptance criteria, so no field makes either imply the other. It does not keep the two **authors** separate: `projects/domain/project.py` declares only `Role.OWNER` and `Role.MEMBER`, there is no vocabulary for "whoever knows the deployment", and both claims are therefore owner-gated. So a single owner who is wrong about the deployment topology can supply both halves of the pair that M5.6's gate reads, and the second claim carries no independent knowledge the first did not already assume.
Deferral rationale: **the alternative available today is worse in the authorising direction, and the one that would fix it is a role-model change.** Of the two roles the model can express, `MEMBER` is the wider, so choosing it to approximate "whoever knows the deployment" would authorize people who should not declare — the opposite of what this entry is about. A third role, or a per-declaration approver, is a change to `projects`' authorization model made for one field, inside an issue whose subject is a declaration; ADR-0024 decision 1's defaulting argument already chose the narrower side where the vocabulary is too coarse, and this follows it. Trigger: **the first role beyond `OWNER`/`MEMBER`**, whatever motivates it, **or the first request to let a non-owner declare** — either makes the distinction expressible, at which point keeping both claims on one role becomes a choice rather than a limit.

---

### G50 — A dormant declaration returns to in force when the target is changed away and back, with nobody re-asserting anything
Confirmed: M5.5, M5→M6 boundary · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **an assertion nobody has restated can silently become live again, and it composes with G47 into something worse than either.** ADR-0028 decision 2 makes `declaration_in_force` a read-time comparison against the live values, so once a declaration exists, editing `zap_target_url` away and back restores the verdict to `True` with no write to the declaration row and no act by any person. Its `declared_at` and `declared_by` still name the original moment. **The compare-and-set this commit added does not close it** — that check only refuses a declaration whose values are *already* wrong at write time, and this is a row that was right at write time and became right again after being wrong. **How the two compose is the part that matters:** a dormant declaration can return to in force after an arbitrary interval, and **G47** means nothing constrained the deployment's drift during that interval. So when M5.6's gate ships, it would admit a derived SAST↔DAST group on an assertion nobody has restated in months, about a deployment that has changed underneath it — with the row reporting `in_force: true` and every string in it unchanged.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is available, partial, and changes semantics an accepted ADR defines — which is not something an implementation commit does.** Comparing `declared_at` against `ScannerConfig.updated_at` kills revival on the **URL side**, because repointing the target bumps that timestamp and a re-declaration would be required to move `declared_at` past it. It does **not** close the tree side: `ConnectedRepo` carries `id`, `project_id`, `provider`, `url` and `default_branch` and **no timestamp at all**, so there is nothing to compare against — the same asymmetry ADR-0028 decision 2 records for observability, arriving on a second axis. Implementing either half would redefine when a declaration is in force, which belongs to an amendment to ADR-0028 rather than to the commit that wired its routes. Trigger: **M5.6**, the issue that first makes the verdict load-bearing rather than merely readable.
Note: **the same shape one entity over is already recorded and is cited rather than re-registered.** ADR-0024's `## Amendments`, the entry dated 2026-08-27 (M5.4, commit 1), measured it on active-scan consent — *"editing the target to a trailing slash and back restores the grant, with the original `granted_at` and `granted_by`"* — amended *withdrawal* to *suspension* as a qualification, and attributed the consequence to **G43** as an instance rather than a new gap. A second entry covering consent would be a second record of one fact, free to drift from the first. What is new here is only that a second entity now has the property, and that it composes with G47.
Note: **not folded into G47, and folding them would be an act rather than a tidy-up.** G47's subject is **drift** — the deployment changing while the three strings do not. This is the opposite axis: **the strings changing and changing back** while the deployment is not in question. Different failure, different fix, and the `Blocks-if-unresolved:` above is about what happens when both hold at once.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary, M5.6): **the trigger fired at M5.6 and was not recorded here.** Since `2318d87` the verdict this entry is about gates a derived group in production, so a dormant declaration returning to force now changes what production produces, not only a stored field.

### G51 — A project can hold two connected repositories, and the read every consumer uses raises when it does
Confirmed: M5.5, M5.6, M8.8 · Status: assigned → M8.7
Kind: owed · Blocks: ship
Blocks-if-unresolved: **two owner-authorized connect calls, each individually legitimate and neither rejected, leave a project on which SCANNING stops working.** `PostgresConnectedRepoRepository.get_by_project_id` ends in `scalar_one_or_none()`, which raises `MultipleResultsFound` on a second row rather than returning either — and **`run_scan.py`'s checkout path is one of its callers**, so the failure lands on the pipeline rather than on the route that caused it. The other existing caller is `build_security_context_from_github.py`. Nothing constrains the second row: `ConnectedRepoModel` declares no `__table_args__`, `da15b49a2373`'s `connected_repos` table carries only a primary key on `id` and a foreign key on `project_id`, and neither `ConnectRepositoryUseCase.execute` nor `ConnectRepositoryViaGitHubUseCase.execute` reads for an existing repo before calling `add` — both go from `require_owner` straight to constructing a fresh entity. **The three artifacts disagree with each other**, which is why this is a gap rather than a bug in one of them: `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s data-model sketch renders the relation as `connected_repos: [ConnectedRepo]`, a **list**; the schema permits many; and the read forbids more than one at the moment of reading. None of it is recorded anywhere today — no entry in this register, no ADR, and nothing in `ARCHITECTURE.md` beyond the sketch that contradicts the code.
Deferral rationale: **what to do is a behaviour decision across two modules, not a constraint somebody adds in passing.** The three answers are materially different — a second connect *replaces* the first, or is *rejected*, or is *legitimate and the read is what is wrong* — and the third is what `ARCHITECTURE.md` currently describes, so choosing either of the first two contradicts a document rather than merely adding a migration. Whichever wins costs a unique constraint, a migration, a decision about existing rows, and a review of both callers. That is not something a route pair's commit takes on. Trigger: **the first issue that changes `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort`**, or any issue that makes a project's repository re-connectable.
Note: **what M5.5's part in this actually is, stated so it is not overstated.** This commit adds the **third and fourth callers** of that read — `DeclareServingUseCase` and `GetServingDeclarationUseCase`, both of which need the tree side of ADR-0028's pair — taking the tree from two to four. Counted per calling file, which is how the two existing ones are counted two sentences up; an earlier version of this note said "third consumer, two call sites", which mixed two units in one sentence. **It adds a consumer, not the exposure.** The exposure has existed since `da15b49a2373` created the table, and nothing about declaring a serving relationship makes a second connect more likely. What changes is only that one more code path now fails when it happens — which is the reason this is registered here rather than waved past as unrelated.
Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3): **a fifth caller, and the first on a read that every MEMBER makes.**
- **The caller.** `PostgresServingDeclarationVerdictReader`, the adapter behind `ServingDeclarationPort`, reads `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort.get_by_project_id`. It serves `GET /projects/{id}/risks`, so this exposure can now fail a dashboard read, not just a scan or an owner route.
- **Bounded in the same commit rather than left open.**
  - The adapter reads the declaration first. With none, it returns `False` without touching the connected-repo row.
  - So only a project that has declared can reach the read that raises.
  - `DeclareServingUseCase` itself raises while two repositories exist, so reaching that read needs a second connect *after* declaring.
  - Pinned against Postgres by `test_an_undeclared_project_with_two_connected_repos_reads_false_without_raising`.
- **This adds a caller and narrows its reach. It does not close the exposure.**

Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4): **the identical mechanism now exists on a second entity, registered as G55, and the two must be fixed as one decision.**
- **Same mechanism.** `security_contexts` has no unique constraint on `project_id`. Both of its writers `add` without reading for an existing row. Its read ends in `scalar_one_or_none`. That is this entry's shape exactly.
- **Different `Blocks-if-unresolved:`, which is why it is a separate entry.** Here, scanning stops for the project. There, the project's route map is frozen at its first detect, and a failed archive fetch is permanent.
- **One behaviour decision, applied twice.** A second write either replaces the first, or is rejected, or is legitimate and the read is what is wrong. Whoever takes either entry takes both. Neither is fixed piecemeal, or the two entities would end up answering the same question differently.
- **No new caller of this read.** `BuildSecurityContextFromGitHubUseCase` already called `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort.get_by_project_id`, and `PostgresRouteMapReader` reads no connected-repository row.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **one sentence above is dated.** *"both go from `require_owner` straight to constructing a fresh entity"* — `ConnectRepositoryUseCase` now calls `validate_connected_repo_url` between the two. The point it makes stands: neither use case reads for an existing repository before `add`. The fix did not touch `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort`, so this entry's trigger has not fired.

Assignment (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **assigned to M8.7**, which decides it as one decision with **G55**.

Note (2026-09-19, M8.8) · Confirms: M8.8: **M8.8's route puts the raise one button away from a user.**
- **The path, once M8.8's code lands.** `POST /projects/{project_id}/scans` (ADR-0035) enqueues a scan. With two connected repositories, `RunScanUseCase._checkout_repo`'s `get_by_project_id` raises `MultipleResultsFound`.
- **What the user sees.** That exception is not in `RunScanUseCase`'s `except` tuple, so the worker's `finally` commits the scan as `RUNNING`, and arq 0.28 does not retry it (**G87**). The status read M8.8 adds will report that scan as `running`, permanently.
- **Unchanged.** M8.7 carries the fix, as one decision with G55. M8.8 does not touch `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort`, so this entry's trigger has not fired.

### G52 — The route map is re-derived at the default-branch TIP, so a derived group is about a third tree, and nothing observes the distance
Confirmed: M5.6 commit 1 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **a derived SAST↔DAST group can be produced from a route table that no scanner ever read, and both of its failure directions are silent.** ADR-0029 decision 3 accepts this rather than deferring it, so it is a property of the shipped design and not an oversight. Nothing can supply a revision: `scanning/domain/scan.py`'s `Scan` carries `id, project_id, status, triggered_by, started_at, finished_at, failure_reason` and no commit; `RepoCheckoutPort.checkout` takes a URL and a token and no revision; `GitHubAdapter.list_repo_files` requests `/git/trees/HEAD` and `get_file_content` sends no `ref`. So the map comes from the **default-branch tip at derivation time**. A route renamed after the scan and before the derivation yields **no group where one belongs**; a route added after it yields **a group for a finding that was never at that route**. Neither raises, neither is recorded, and nothing measures how far the tip has moved. In ADR-0028 decision 2's formulation one axis over: **the map is re-derived at the tip and never at the observed revision**, so a green derived group means *this route path exists in the tip now and a finding's line falls inside its span there*, and never *the tree Semgrep scanned defined this route*.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is G25 and pulling it in is bundling, which ADR-0029 decision 3 refuses with an argument rather than a preference.** Option (ii), waiting on G25, would leave M5.6 unable to close the conditioning half of **G27** inside M5 — putting `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10's demonstration behind an entry whose own trigger is **M9.1** — and would not even be sufficient on its own, since `VcsProviderPort` would still need a `ref` parameter on two methods, which is outside G25's four-item scope. Option (iii), taking part of G25 here, would settle M9.1's diffing semantics inside the issue that wants a route map, and would add a schema change to an issue already carrying an ADR, an extractor, a port, a factory and a gate — the growth ADR-0028's Consequences named. **This entry does NOT block M5.6 closing G27's conditioning half**, and reading it as though it did is the mistake it exists to prevent: that criterion asks whether a group is withheld when the declaration is absent, which is a question about the declaration and not about the revision. Trigger: **G25 landing**, which closes it outright, **or any change giving `VcsProviderPort` a `ref` parameter**, at which point the value to pass becomes the only missing piece.
Note: **distinct from G25 and from G47, and the three are easy to fold and must not be.** Three unversioned things, three different `Blocks-if-unresolved:` fields, and closing any one leaves the others open. **G25** is *which tree the scanner read* — cross-scan, landing on M9.1's resolution detection. **G47** is *what the URL is actually serving* — the residue of G27's fix, on the URL axis. **This** is *which tree the route map was derived from* — the tree axis, created by M5.6's fix rather than found beside it, and it is the second entry in this register to arrive as the residue of the fix for G27 rather than as a defect in it.
Note: **CI cannot exhibit this, which is why it is written down rather than tested.** The demo target is pinned and unchanging, so its tip **is** its scanned tree, and the one configuration where the residue is invisible is the only one the suite exercises. That is the same accident ADR-0023's 2026-08-25 amendment records for the active-scan probe and G27 — *"the one configuration in which the coupling holds by construction"* — arriving a third time. A green M5.6 says nothing whatever about this entry.
Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3): **persistence is now decided. It makes this residue OLDER IN EXPECTATION, not wrong in a new way.**

ADR-0029's 2026-09-15 amendment stores the map at Security Context build time, in commit 4, instead of deriving it on every read. So *"the default-branch tip at derivation time"* becomes the tip as of the owner's last context build. That residue was already accepted, ~~and it now ages between rebuilds with nothing observing it~~ *(struck 2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4: there are no working rebuilds to age between — see that date's note below)*. It is recorded here at that width rather than as a new entry: decision 3 already accepts a map built from a tree no scanner read.

**Two additions to this entry's exits:**
- **An observation point.** The archive fetch commit 4 is priced to use carries its resolved commit in the archive's top-level directory: `CarlosPD8-verion-demo-target-c68caa7/` for the demo target. That would be the first observation of a map's revision anywhere in this system. It does not close this entry, because `Scan` still records no commit (**G25**). It does make the distance measurable once G25 lands.
- **The eventual fix.** Deriving the map at scan time, from `scanning`'s checkout, is the only option that shrinks the distance. The same amendment prices it and does not take it, because it is cross-module and needs its own ADR. Added trigger: **any issue proposing scan-time route derivation**.

Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 4): **the observation point now exists. It does not resolve this entry, nothing reads it yet, and the residue is FROZEN rather than ageing.**
- **What exists.** `route_maps.source_archive_commit_sha` holds the full 40-character commit the route map's archive was cut from. It is read from the archive's pax global header. **The note above is QUALIFIED on where the commit comes from:** the top-level directory `CarlosPD8-verion-demo-target-c68caa7/` carries only a 7-character abbreviation, which `GitHubAdapter` uses as a cross-check and never as the source.
- **At exactly what width.** It is the tree **the route map** came from, and nothing else. It is not the tree the framework was detected from, which is a separate read (**G56**). It is not the tree any scanner read (**G25**). Labelling it "the commit this context was built from" would be an over-claim that looks like provenance.
- **What it does not do.** Nothing compares it with anything, and no consumer reads it. It makes the distance this entry names *measurable once G25 lands*, and measures nothing today.
- **This entry's trigger did not fire.** The archive is requested at `tarball/HEAD`, and `VcsProviderPort.fetch_source_archive` takes no `ref` parameter.
- **"Older in expectation" is corrected to "frozen until G55 is fixed".** The note above assumed the map is refreshed at the owner's next context build. It cannot be without breaking the project, because a second detect writes a duplicate `security_contexts` row and that project's context reads then raise (**G55**). So the tip a map describes is the tip at the project's **first** detect, and it does not move. For a project that ran detect before this commit, no map exists, and none can be obtained without that breakage.

### G53 — A Semgrep finding's `url` will carry a value that did not come off `Location.url`, so section (b)'s provenance guarantee narrows while `mypy` stays green
Confirmed: M5.6 commit 1, M6.1, M6.2 · Status: assigned → M8.5
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the one site this project built to make a cross-module description drift loudly is where the drift is being introduced, deliberately, and it will not report it.** ADR-0023 section (b) makes `build_match_key` *"the single place `mypy` compares correlation's description of `Finding` against the real one"*.
