ADR-0029 decision 4 puts a derived route path into the key's `url` field, so the comparison still runs and still passes — `str | None` into `str | None` — while the **provenance** of the value changes: for a Semgrep finding it comes from a route map, not from `Location.url`. **The ADR already classified this as undetectable before anyone chose it**: section (c)'s second bullet is *"Semantic changes behind an unchanged signature … invisible to every check here."* So this is a known hole entered on purpose, not a new one, and the entry exists so that "on purpose" is recorded somewhere a later reader can find. **A second, narrower half rides with it:** the builder gains `file_path` and `start_line` as scalars so all five source values still meet their annotations at one site, but `test_match_key.py`'s conformance test derives its expectations from the **key's** fields, so those two parameters get `mypy` and **no conformance assertion**. A `Location.start_line` re-typed from `int | None` would fail at the builder and nowhere else.
Deferral rationale: **the alternatives are the two shapes ADR-0029 decision 4 priced and rejected, and both cost more than this.** A fourth key field is the shape ADR-0023's amendment section 4 states a preference for and it is **inert as written** — `matches` is `left == right` over the whole dataclass, so a Semgrep key and a ZAP key carrying an equal derived location still differ on `url` and do not co-group; making it work needs an amendment to section 2's equality rule, which that section rejected as non-transitive, or a change to what the mappers populate. It also cannot satisfy the conformance test at all, since `_FIELD_SOURCES` needs a declaring type per field and no `Finding` or `Location` field a derived route comes off exists. A second matching path breaks the one-group-per-finding property every shipped assertion rests on. So the chosen shape is the only one of three that leaves the frozen field list, the matching rule, the partition and the conformance test untouched, and its price is this entry. **What would actually close it** is a provenance-aware check at the construction site — a second parameter naming where `url` came from, or a distinct type for a derived path — either of which is a design change to a site an accepted ADR describes, and therefore an amendment rather than a tidy-up. Trigger: **any third writer of the key's `url` field**, at which point "two provenances, one documented" becomes "n provenances, unchecked", **or M6 reading a derived location as a scoring input**, where rule 5's traceability makes the provenance a user-visible property rather than an internal one.
Note: **the bound that makes it tolerable today, stated so it is not mistaken for a guarantee.** The derivation happens at the `build_match_key` call site in `correlation/application/` and **never in a mapper**, so `Location.url` keeps its meaning for every other consumer and `dedup_hash` is untouched (ADR-0019 decision 3). One further bound is load-bearing and fragile: keying on path drops the host, which is lossless only because `ScannerConfig` carries a **single** `zap_target_url`, so every ZAP URL in a project shares one host. A second target per project would make two paths collide silently, and nothing today would catch it.
Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3): **the narrowing now ships. The host bound's conclusion stands, but its stated ground did not follow, so that ground is replaced below.**

**What ships.**
- `build_match_key` keys every `url` on its path.
- Behind the declaration gate, it gives a no-signal finding a derived path.
- `test_match_key.py`'s conformance test is unchanged, so this entry's second half holds as written.
- A third input joins the site: `paths_serving`, annotated as `correlation`'s own `PathsServing` protocol. `mypy` checks it against `RouteMap.paths_serving` where the use case passes it. That is another conformance point that no test asserts.

**The Note above says keying on path is lossless *"only because `ScannerConfig` carries a single `zap_target_url`"*. That ground is STRUCK as not following.** One configured target does not imply one scheme, host and port across the findings — `urlsplit(url).path` drops all three — because a crawl can leave the target.

**What bounds it** (verified against `_build_plan_yaml` and ZAP's own documentation):
- The plan declares exactly one context, `verion-target`, with `urls: [target]`. ZAP documents that *"everything under each url will be included"*.
- `activeScan` names that context explicitly.
- `spider` passes no context, and its documented default is the first context.
- So the jobs that send requests are scoped to the target.

**What does not bound it:**
- The `report` job passes no `sites`, whose documented default is *"default all"*. The report is not filtered to the context.
  - **This gap is already observed, not only documented.** The committed active capture has two `site` entries: `http://target.example:8080` with all eight alerts, and an empty `https://target.example:8080`. The empty one is outside the context's `urls: [target]`. `tests/integration/fixtures/active_scan/README.md` records it under *"Two site entries"*.
  - That site carried no alerts, so no finding was keyed off it. Nothing prevents one that does.
- ZAP's spider documentation does not say whether it requests robots.txt or sitemap URLs that fall outside the context.

**Corpus evidence, not a guarantee:** 13 of 13 passive and 16 of 16 active alert instances in the committed captures are `http://target.example:8080`.

**What would make the bound a property of the plan, and why it is not taken:** adding `sites` to the report job. That is a scan-plan change, which fires **G39**, so it is out of scope here.

**Added trigger:** a real scan producing any finding whose url host differs from `zap_target_url`'s.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **the second trigger — "M6 reading a derived location as a scoring input" — has fired, and it bought LESS than the first draft of this note claimed.** Under ADR-0005 decision 0 the group key names the surface being scored, so for the Semgrep member of the `/calculate` surface the *identity of the thing scored* comes off a route map rather than off `Location.url`. That is the rule-5 exposure this trigger anticipated, and **M6.1 does not answer it.** The answer drafted for it — a surface `confidence` of `read` or `derived`, making the provenance a scored output rather than an internal detail — was cut from ADR-0005 by that issue's byte ceiling and deferred to M6.2 as **G63**. So as of this commit a derived attribution is scored **identically** to one read off the tool, and nothing in the response says which it was. The rest of this entry is untouched: the construction site still carries no provenance parameter, the conformance test still derives from the key's fields only, `file_path`/`start_line` still get `mypy` and no assertion, and the first trigger — a third writer of the key's `url` — has not fired.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.2): **the derived surface is now scored, and the provenance is still invisible.** `test_compute_risk.py` asserts that `/calculate`'s `severity_signal` of 4 is produced by the **Semgrep** member — measured, because every alert instance in `zap_scan.json` is riskcode 2 or below, so ZAP cannot have supplied it. That member reached the surface through the route map rather than through its own `Location.url`, so the one finding that decides the surface's severity is the one whose attribution is derived. Nothing in `ScoredSurface` or `RiskReasoning` says so, exactly as this entry's M6.1 note forecast; **G63** records why the `read`/`derived` scale that would have said it was not built. Unchanged otherwise.

Assignment (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **assigned to M8.5.** M8.5's confidence marks as *derived* anything that arrives through the route map.

### G54 — A Flask converter route maps successfully and can never correlate, so the route table asserts coverage it does not have
Confirmed: M5.6 commit 2 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the extractor emits `/user/<int:id>`, the entry looks complete, and under ADR-0029 decision 4 it can never equal a crawled `/user/123` for any target — so the map over-reports its own coverage and every other entry in this register describes something that is missing rather than something that is present and inert.** Matching is `left == right` over the frozen `MatchKey` (ADR-0023's 2026-08-26 amendment section 2), and a converter template is not the string any crawl produces. **This is a different claim from ADR-0029 decision 5's, and the difference is the register's own currency:** that bullet lists converters under *"untested by construction, because the tree cannot express them"*, which is epistemic — we do not know whether it works. This is structural and verified: it cannot. `tests/unit/test_route_extraction.py::test_a_converter_path_is_emitted_verbatim` pins the premise. **It generalizes in the direction `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10 needs**, which is what makes it worse than a footnote: `verion-demo-target`'s two routes are converter-free, so CI stays green forever, while any real Flask application is mostly converters. That is the accident **G52**'s second Note already records for the tree axis, arriving on a third.
Deferral rationale: **the fix that fabricates is the IN-KEY one, and the derivation-side option is available and unpriced rather than foreclosed — which is the distinction this entry exists to keep, because getting it wrong is a documented failure of this project.** Putting a pattern matcher **in the key** is refused on two grounds that hold there: it replaces the plain equality ADR-0023's amendment section 2 fixed, whose alternative that section rejected as non-transitive; and stripping `/user/<int:id>` to a `/user/` prefix collides with a sibling `/user/new`, fabricating a cross-tool group, which ADR-0019 decision 3 ranks as the worse failure. **Neither ground reaches the DERIVATION.** The route table can be applied to *both* sides: a crawled `/user/123` derives to the template it matches, the key then compares two templates, and `matches` stays `left == right` with ADR-0023 section 2 untouched. Its cost, named rather than left implied, is converter-aware matching for Flask's `int`, `string` and `path` converters — real work, which is why this is recorded as unpriced rather than recommended. **The precedent for insisting on the scope is ADR-0023 Decision A**, whose refusal of the `Server` banner *as a `Location` correlation signal* was restated as a refusal of the banner in general; correcting that reached at least four sites — ADR-0028's Consequences and its Alternatives, M5.5's roadmap bullet, and G27's Split note — with G47's own `Deferral rationale:` recording that ADR-0028 decision 2 *"originally described it as foreclosed and does not now"*. No exhaustive count is re-derived here. Trigger: **the first Flask target with a converter route reaching a derived group**, or **any change to how the derived path enters the key**.
Note: **this entry has a SECOND member, found by the guardian on the commit that opened it, and it is the more likely of the two to be met first.** A Flask **blueprint registered with a `url_prefix`** has exactly this structure: `Blueprint(..., url_prefix="/api")` with `@bp.route("/nested")` serves `/api/nested`, while the extractor emits `/nested` — because the prefix lives at the `register_blueprint` call and this module reads decorators. The entry is produced, looks complete, and can never equal a crawled path, which is this entry's own sentence with converters swapped out. It is named here rather than given a fourth register entry because the failure, the trigger and the fix surface are identical; the derivation-side option covers it too, by resolving a crawled path against a route table that knows the prefix. **What is NOT this entry** is `app.add_url_rule("/x", view_func=f)`, and the distinction is the direction of the failure: that produces **no** route and no residue, so it under-counts silently rather than over-reporting coverage. ADR-0029 decision 5 already lists it as untested by construction, under-counting is the failure ADR-0019 decision 3 prefers, and `route_extraction.py`'s own docstring names it — so it is not registered.
Note: **distinct from G52, G47 and G53, and the four are easy to fold and must not be.** **G52** is *which tree the route map was derived from*. **G47** is *what the URL is actually serving*. **G53** is *the provenance of the value in the key*. **This** is *the route table asserting coverage it does not have* — the only one of the four about an entry that is produced rather than one that is missing. The presentation half rides with **G53** and is **not** re-registered: a `MatchKey.url` of `/user/<int:id>` is not a URL and will be rendered as one at M6 or M8, which is that entry's second provenance narrowing rather than a fifth thing.
Note (2026-09-15, M5.6 commit 3): **a THIRD member. The rule commit 3 adopts creates it; it was not found alongside the change.**
- **The rule.** `build_match_key` derives a route path only when `RouteMap.paths_serving` returns **exactly one** path for a finding's line. ADR-0029's 2026-09-15 amendment records it. It is forced by two things: a key carries one `url`, and the extractor refuses tie-breaks.
- **What it does.** Take a view with two stacked route decorators, `@app.route("/a")` over `@app.route("/b")`. It **maps correctly**: both routes are present with overlapping spans, as `test_route_extraction.py` pins. Every finding in its shared body is then **silently excluded from correlation**. It gets no derived path, stays a singleton, and nothing in the output says a route served that line.
- **Why it belongs here.** That is this entry's subject exactly: the route table asserting coverage it does not have. As a residue inside the ADR amendment, it would carry no trigger.
- **The pin.** `test_correlate_findings.py::test_a_line_served_by_two_stacked_routes_derives_nothing`, so a later tie-break has to be a visible act.
- **Not a member:** two routes on one path. `paths_serving` returns distinct paths, so `@app.get("/x")` over `@app.post("/x")` derives `/x`.
- **Fix surface**, shared with the other members and wider now: the derivation-side resolution above, or a key that can carry a set.
- **Added triggers:** the first stacked-route view whose findings go uncorrelated in a real scan, or any change to the exactly-one rule.
- **The distinct-from paragraph above still holds.** This member is an entry that is produced and then **excluded**, so it sits on the produced side, not the missing side, which is what separates this entry from the other three.

### G55 — A second Security Context write adds a second row, after which that project's context reads raise — so the route map cannot be refreshed and a failed fetch is permanent
Confirmed: M5.6 commit 4, M5→M6 boundary, M6.1 · Status: assigned → M8.7
Kind: owed · Blocks: ship
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the route map M5.6 commit 4 persists is frozen at a project's first detect, and every recovery path runs through a write that breaks the project.**

Three facts about `security_contexts` combine:
- **No constraint.** `SecurityContextModel` declares no `__table_args__`. `a1b2c3d4e5f6`'s table carries only a primary key on `id` and a foreign key on `project_id`.
- **Two writers, neither checking first.**
  - `BuildSecurityContextUseCase.execute` calls `add` unconditionally.
  - `UpdateExposureTagsUseCase.execute` calls `add` on its no-existing-context branch.
- **A strict read.** `PostgresSecurityContextRepository.get_by_project_id` ends in `scalar_one_or_none`, which raises on a second row.

What breaks as a result:
1. **The map cannot be refreshed** without breaking that project's context reads.
2. **A stored `FETCH_FAILED`, `TOO_LARGE` or `MALFORMED` is effectively permanent**, because the retry is a second detect.
3. **A project that ran detect before M5.6 commit 4 has no route map, and cannot get one** without that breakage. Its next detect is a second detect.
4. **The API surface fails**: `GET` and `PATCH /projects/{id}/security-context` fail for the project, and nothing rejects the write that caused it.

Evidence: **reproduced 2026-09-15 against real Postgres, through the real app** (ASGI transport, real routers and DI, `GitHubAdapter` over `MockTransport` serving the committed capture), with the script's rows deleted afterwards:

```
A: detect -> detect
    detect #1: HTTP 201  -> contexts, route_maps = (1, 1)
    detect #2: HTTP 201  -> contexts, route_maps = (2, 1)   derived_at moved
    GET  /security-context: raised sqlalchemy.exc.MultipleResultsFound
    PATCH /security-context: raised sqlalchemy.exc.MultipleResultsFound
B: PATCH -> detect
    PATCH tags: HTTP 200  -> contexts, route_maps = (1, 0)
    detect #2: HTTP 201  -> contexts, route_maps = (2, 1)
    GET  /security-context: raised sqlalchemy.exc.MultipleResultsFound
    PATCH /security-context: raised sqlalchemy.exc.MultipleResultsFound
```

**Sequence B is the more ordinary one**: an owner who annotates before detecting. **The second detect in A does rewrite the route map** (one row, `derived_at` moved), which is why the precise claim is *cannot be refreshed without breaking the context reads* rather than *cannot be written*.

**No test is committed for this, deliberately.** A test asserting that a duplicate row is created would encode the defect as expected behaviour.

Deferral rationale: **the fix is the behaviour decision G51 already defers, applied to a second entity, and the two must be taken together.** It needs:
- a unique constraint and its migration;
- a decision about existing duplicate rows;
- a choice among the same three answers: a second write replaces the first, is rejected, or is legitimate and the read is wrong;
- a review of every writer, across two entities.

G51's own `Deferral rationale:` already says that is not something a feature commit takes on in passing, and a route-map commit is less entitled to it than a route pair was. Fixing one entity alone would leave the two answering the same question differently.

Trigger: **the first issue that changes `SecurityContextRepositoryPort`**, or **the first issue that changes `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort`** (which takes both, per G51's note), or **any issue that needs a route map refreshed**.
Note: **the identical mechanism as G51, and a separate entry because the `Blocks-if-unresolved:` fields differ materially.** G51 stops SCANNING for a project. This freezes its route map and makes a failed fetch permanent. The fix is ONE decision applied twice; G51 carries the reciprocal note.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary): **this now blocks M6.1 as well.** A project's exposure tags are readable only through `SecurityContextRepositoryPort.get_by_project_id`, the read that raises after a second detect, and exposure is one of M6.1's named inputs. Carried in M6.1.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.1): **this no longer blocks M6.1, and the correction is to the boundary review's note above rather than to the entry.** That note says a project's exposure tags are readable only through the read that raises after a second detect, *"and exposure is one of M6.1's named inputs"* — true when written. ADR-0005 decision 4 declines `exposure_tags` as a scoring input altogether: it is free text behind a persistence port another module must not consume (ADR-0022 decision 2), and this entry is one of the three grounds. The shipped `exposure_signal` is read from `Finding.source` instead — a DAST member means a scanner reached the surface over the network — so **no part of the scoring path touches `SecurityContextRepositoryPort`**. The entry is untouched in every other respect and its `Blocks-if-unresolved:` is unchanged: the route map is still frozen at first detect, a stored `FETCH_FAILED` is still permanent, and **§10's cross-tool surface still exists only while that map is readable**, which ADR-0005 decision 5 records as a thing a green M6.2 will not prove.

Assignment (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **assigned to M8.7.** M8.7 decides it, as one decision with **G51**.

### G56 — The framework a route map is keyed on and the source it is parsed from are two separate reads of the tip
Confirmed: M5.6 commit 4 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **a route map can be parsed under a framework detected from a different commit than the one its source came from, and the stored record gives no sign of it.**

`BuildSecurityContextFromGitHubUseCase.execute` reads the tip twice:
- First, it detects the framework from the manifests (`list_repo_files` on `/git/trees/HEAD`, then `get_file_content` with no `ref`).
- Then, in a separate request, it fetches `tarball/HEAD` and runs `extract_routes`, **keyed on that framework**, over the archive.

A push landing between the two requests pairs a framework from tree A with source from tree B. `route_maps.source_archive_commit_sha` names B, and nothing records A. The three cases, stated honestly rather than as "harmless":

1. **A is not Flask, B is.** No archive is fetched. An empty map is stored for a Flask tree, and G55 blocks the rebuild that would replace it.
2. **A is Flask, B is not.** B's source is parsed as Flask. Usually that yields nothing. But `extract_routes` matches `@app.get`, `@app.post` and the other method shortcuts, so a FastAPI tree's literal decorators ARE emitted as routes, and the record says `framework = "flask"`. A derived group can then come from a tree the framework key does not admit. **It is not a group about a route the tree does not serve**: the decorator does declare that path in B. So the error is in what the key admits, not in the path.
3. **Both are Flask.** The map is B's routes, correct for B, and the SHA names B. Only the framework's provenance is wrong, and harmlessly.

Nothing raises in any of the three. The window is the duration of the manifest reads, measured in ADR-0029's commit-4 amendment.

Deferral rationale: **the fix is named and is a scope refusal, not a quality one.** One archive read would feed both the manifests `detect_stack` reads and the source `extract_routes` parses. That removes this gap, makes the stored SHA describe the whole context, and costs one read instead of 1+k. `GitHubAdapter`'s member allowlist is written as a list so that the change reads as a list change: reading a manifest as text is not parsing it as code. It is not taken in M5.6 commit 4 because it rewrites M2.3's working, tested detection path inside the commit that must also resolve G27 end to end. Trigger: **any change to `BuildSecurityContextFromGitHubUseCase`'s fetch path**, or **the first observed mismatch between a stored route map's `framework` and its source**.
Note: **distinct from G52 and G25.** **G52** is *which tree the route map came from, relative to the tree a scanner read*. **G25** is *which tree the scanner read*. **This** is *whether the map's framework key and its source are the same tree*. It is inside one context build, and exists only because the build makes two reads.

### G58 — `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` cannot read a bold assignment, while `check_assignments_name_real_issues`, added beside it in the same file, can
Confirmed: M5→M6 boundary review commit 1, Rules-for-M6 promotion · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the escalation guard, at the third confirmation of any entry whose assignment is written in bold.** The guard matches `Status:\s*(assigned|resolved)\s*→` over the entry. In a bold value, `**` sits where the keyword is expected, so the assigned branch never fires and `Deferral rationale:` alone decides the entry. That has two outcomes, and neither is the right one:
- **With a rationale, the guard passes it on the wrong clause and reports nothing.** Measured on a temporary copy with G19 raised to three confirmations: **0** findings.
- **Without a rationale, a correct assignment fails CI.** Same copy with the rationale removed: **1** finding. Removing it is the natural tidy-up once a gap is assigned, because a rationale explains why no issue had the work. The control: unbolding that same value, rationale still removed, gives **0** findings. The bold is the only difference.

This is the guard G28's 2026-08-25 note already measured, *"the input that would break it has not arrived, not that the register conforms"*, arriving a second time in the same guard.

**The subject of this entry is a disagreement between two checks in one file, not the guard's blindness alone.** `scripts/check_claims.py` now holds two checks that read the same `Status:` field and disagree about what an assignment looks like:
- `check_assignments_name_real_issues` strips a leading `*`, backtick or `_`, and reads `**assigned → M5.3**` as an assignment to M5.3.
- `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` does not read it as an assignment at all.

Re-pointing G19's bold value at M5.10 on a copy shows it directly: the assignment check reports it, and the escalation guard reports nothing. **The rules list at the head of this register, amended in the same commit, now says leading bold is accepted.** That is true of one check and false of the other, so an author who follows the rules can write exactly the form one guard cannot see. The blindness predates this commit. The disagreement is what this commit creates, and why the entry is seeded here.
Evidence: measured in the tree this commit produces, before this entry existed, so over 56 entries.
- **Exactly one entry writes an assignment in bold: G19**, `**assigned → M5.3**`, at two confirmations (M4.5, post-M4). It is one confirmation from the threshold, so the blindness is real but not currently reachable.
- The guard is blind to **seven** bold `Status:` values in all. Only G19's is this entry's:
  - G3 and G12 read `**resolved → M5.0**`, at two confirmations each. That is the same mechanism on the other branch, but the assignment check does not read `resolved →` either, so there the two checks agree: both are silent.
  - G6 and G8 are bold `open` and claim nothing.
  - G9 and G10 are `**RESOLVED (M4.4)**`, a different grammar with no arrow.
  - G28's note records five of the seven as unreadable: G3, G9, G10, G12 and G19. It leaves out G6 and G8, which claim neither an assignment nor a resolution.
- Entries at three or more confirmations: G1 and G8. Neither carries a bold assignment.

Deferral rationale: **the fix is to make the guard reuse the tolerant reading the assignment check already implements, so the file has one definition of an assignment.** That is a change to a guard whose subject is not this commit's. It would also mean re-running the escalation guard's own verification, which no mutation in this commit covers: the three outcomes above as a mutation set, plus the bolded-G1 injection G28's note records. Trigger: **G19 reaching a third confirmation**, or **any entry at two confirmations whose `Status:` gains a bold assignment**, or **any change to `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated`**. The second trigger names an assignment deliberately. G3 and G12 are already bold at two confirmations, but they are resolved, so a trigger worded "a bold Status" would have fired the day it was written.
Note: **G57 is skipped deliberately, not lost.** `2318d87`'s commit message names a G57 that was retracted before it was registered. A commit message cannot be corrected, so reusing the number would give that id two subjects in `git log`.
Note: **distinct from G28, and the second instance of what its 2026-08-25 note measured rather than a restatement of it.** That note recorded the guard's blindness as a coverage finding inside an entry about review scope. This entry records that a sibling check now reads the same field differently.
Note (2026-09-15, M5→M6 boundary, commit 2): **no entry writes a bold assignment any more.** This boundary re-confirmed G19 to three, which is this entry's first trigger, and in the same commit withdrew its `**assigned → M5.3**`; so the trigger fired on an entry that no longer carries a bold assignment, and both checks read G19 correctly. The subject is unchanged: the two checks still disagree about what a `Status:` looks like, and the next bold assignment written anywhere is the case.
Note (2026-09-16, Rules-for-M6 promotion): **the same guard is blind to the RESOLVED branch of its regex too, and there the result is not a missed count but a false failure.**
- `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` treats an entry as settled only if `Status:\s*(assigned|resolved)\s*→` matches. `check_fired_triggers_are_recorded`, in the same file, strips leading `*`, `` ` ``, `_` and spaces and tests whether the value begins `resolved`.
- At `b81b3b8` the guard cannot see **4 of the 14 resolved** entries as resolved, in two forms. **G3 and G12** write `**resolved → M5.0**` (this entry's bold subject, on the resolved branch). **G9 and G10** write `**RESOLVED (M4.4)**` with no arrow.
- That split is why one register produced 12 resolved and 14 resolved in the M6→M7 week. Both counts were correct under their own parser. The register's Status terms, now defined in its rules list, follow the fired-trigger check's reading.
- **The consequence is not a counting problem.** The guard puts those four under the escalation rule as if they were open. They pass today on two independent grounds: each is below three confirmations (G3 and G12 at 2, G9 and G10 at 1), and each carries a `Deferral rationale:`.
- **The exposure is shown by the entry that has neither.** **G1** is the only resolved entry at three confirmations, and it has no `Deferral rationale:`. It passes only because its Status is the plain `resolved → M3.7`. Written as `**resolved → M3.7**` or `RESOLVED (M3.7)`, it would fail CI with "confirmed 3 times … but carries neither" — a guard about work not done, tripped by work that is done. *Shown by mutation, not deduced:* rewriting only G1's `Status:` in an in-memory copy of this file to each form, and running `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` over it, is red for both and green for the intact text. The shape is a gap escalated by assignment, not rationale, whose assignment is later overwritten by a resolution in either unreadable form.
- Both checks stay unfixed. This entry's `Deferral rationale:` already names the fix: one definition of the field, reused by both checks.

### G59 — The DNS-rebinding gate checks the worker's resolution, and ZAP resolves the hostname again
Confirmed: M5→M6 boundary review · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: ship
Blocks-if-unresolved: **ADR-0013's rebinding gate, under an active scan.** `ZapAdapter.run` resolves the target hostname on the worker through `DnsResolverPort` and rejects private, loopback, link-local, reserved and multicast addresses, but the resolved addresses go nowhere: no `--add-host` maps the target hostname to them (the one `--add-host` is `host.docker.internal:host-gateway`), there is no `--dns`, and the mounted plan carries the hostname. ZAP resolves it again itself, for up to 540s — since M5.4 including an `activeScan` job that sends cloud-metadata probes and executes shell commands on the target (M5.4's measurement). A hostname that answers a public address to the gate and a private one to ZAP passes, which is the attack ADR-0013's Context names. **Read from the code, not demonstrated** against a rebinding resolver; whether ZAP re-resolves per request or once per run was not measured.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is a choice about scanner networking, not a documentation change.** The options are pinning the checked address into the container (`--add-host` for the target hostname, which changes what a redirect to another host reaches), or an egress policy on the ZAP container that refuses private ranges whatever it resolves. Both change what `ZapAdapter` can scan, so they belong to an issue that can test them against a real rebinding resolver. The documentation half is taken: ADR-0013's and `ARCHITECTURE.md` §9's "immediately before use" are struck. Trigger: **any change to `ZapAdapter.run`'s gate or its `docker run` arguments**, **M10.1's threat model**, which names the SSRF surface, or **the first ZAP target configured by someone other than the owner of the host it names**.

### G60 — A connected repository written before the rule-12 fix can still carry userinfo, and that project cannot be detected, declared or scanned
Confirmed: M5→M6 boundary review · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: ship
Blocks-if-unresolved: **every project whose `connected_repos.url` was written with a credential before 2026-09-15.** The write path refuses userinfo since then (`validate_connected_repo_url`), but no migration inspected existing rows. For such a row, the credential sits in plaintext in `connected_repos.url`, against `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §11 as rule 12 extends it. The detect route answers 400, and so does the declare route, since the declared value would have to repeat the credential. A scan fails in `parse_github_clone_url`, and its `UnsupportedRepoUrl` is not among the exceptions `RunScanUseCase` catches, so it leaves the job uncaught. There is no update route and a second connect makes the read raise (**G51**), so the owner has no way out. Before the fix that uncaught message quoted the URL, so the error from a failed scan of such a project may also have carried the credential wherever the worker recorded it.
Deferral rationale: **nothing in the repository says any such row exists, and what to do with one is a data decision** — strip the credential, delete the row, or refuse the project until it is reconnected — which needs G51's re-connect question answered first. Trigger: **M10.3's secrets pass**, **the first change to `ConnectedRepoRepositoryPort`** (which G51 also names), or **the first report of a scan failing with `UnsupportedRepoUrl`**.

### G61 — A scored Risk listing reads the project's findings twice, and only one of the two reads has ever been measured
Confirmed: M6.1, M6.3, M6.2, M7.2, M7.3 · Status: assigned → M8.2
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the read cost of every scored listing, doubled, against a decision whose own premise is that a candidate Risk is cheap to recompute.** ADR-0005 decision 2 has `risk_engine` consume two ports — `correlation`'s candidate-Risk port and `normalization`'s `FindingRepositoryPort` — because a `MatchGroup` carries `finding_ids` and no severity, so the engine cannot see a `Finding.severity` without reading findings itself. One scored request therefore calls `get_by_project_id` **twice**: once inside `CorrelateFindingsUseCase`, once in `risk_engine`. That method is unpaged and outer-joins `evidence`, so each read hydrates every `raw_payload` in the project even though scoring reads none of them. ADR-0025 measured one such read at **10.974–12.050 ms** (parallel nested loop) and **26.293–30.271 ms** (hash join over all 100,000 evidence rows) at 2,000 findings; a scored request is two of those, and **no scored request has been measured at all** — the doubling is arithmetic, not a reading. ADR-0025's own warning transfers with more force than it was written with: a bad number is evidence against its decision 1, not a tuning task, and this makes the number worse without touching the decision.
Deferral rationale: **the fix is a port-shape decision and it belongs to the issue that can measure it.** The alternative ADR-0005 names is widening `correlation`'s port to carry per-finding scoring scalars, which makes `correlation` decide what `risk_engine` needs — a coupling worse than the read unless the read is actually slow. Measuring requires `ComputeRiskUseCase` to exist (M6.2) and a seeded project, which `scripts/seed_findings_benchmark.py` already provides at the volume ADR-0025 used. Fixing before measuring would be tuning against arithmetic, and the three responses ADR-0025 refuses in advance — an index, a cache, paging the input — are refused here for the same reasons. Trigger: ~~**M6.3's endpoint, measured**~~ *(FIRED 2026-09-16 and spent — that endpoint exists and the measurement is in the note below, so this clause named an event that had already happened and would have surfaced this entry to nobody. Re-pointed rather than left, because a trigger aimed at a spent event on an entry that can never reach the escalation threshold is **G1**'s shape exactly, and is the same defect this register caught on **G33** three commits earlier.)* **Re-pointed at events that have NOT happened:** **M8.2**, the dashboard API — the first consumer that makes a scored read user-facing, the issue with standing to own the ADR-0025 amendment, and the one whose scale exercises this cost most directly, since the clause struck from ADR-0025 in this same commit means the read grows with the CORPUS and a dashboard is where many projects' worth of it arrives; **M7.2**, the Brief's generation and endpoint, which must obtain a project's scored Risks and inherits ADR-0022 decision 2's project-scoped read — **deliberately not M7.1**, whose bullet takes a single structured `RiskReasoning` **in** and is tested against a fake adapter, so it never performs this read at all; or ADR-0025's own scale trigger, *"the first project an order of magnitude beyond the measured 2,000 findings, or any report of a slow Risk listing"* — whichever is first.

**EXIT CONDITION, stated because an escalation whose deciding experiment is unnamed is an opinion with a number attached.** This entry's argument stands on two legs: the read is the dominant term, and the scoring residual is a **worst-case ceiling** that production grouping shrinks. **One experiment settles both — a re-run of `scripts/seed_findings_benchmark.py` with production-shaped grouping**, `package` and `url` seeded so a project's findings form realistic surfaces instead of 2,000 singletons. That is precisely the branch ADR-0030 decision 7 priced and deferred, and it is a **G19** event: its shape question must be answered in writing before any number out of it is quoted. **What the outcome decides, fixed in advance so it cannot be read backwards:** if the residual collapses while the read term holds — and it should hold, being the same statement over the same rows — then the escalation stands and ADR-0025 decision 1 is owed its amendment; if instead the whole scored request lands far enough below anything a consumer would call slow, the escalation is **withdrawn on evidence rather than on preference** and this entry closes as measured-and-acceptable rather than lingering. ~~**Not assigned, and deliberately so:** M8.2 is the natural owner on the reasoning above, but assigning an ADR-0025 amendment to a roadmap issue is a scope decision, so this entry proposes that owner and does not take it.~~ ***(Superseded 2026-09-16 at the M6→M7 boundary review: the scope decision this sentence declined to take has been taken, and `Status:` above now reads `assigned → M8.2`. The reasoning that named M8.2 is unchanged and is what the assignment rests on; what changed is only that a proposal became an owner. Left struck rather than deleted, because the distinction it draws — that a register entry may not assign its own owner — is the rule that made the proposal legitimate, and a deleted sentence would read as though the entry had always carried an assignment. **The `Deferral rationale:` above is deliberately kept, and the consequence is stated rather than left to be discovered:** this entry now carries an assignment *and* a rationale, which **G30** names as the inert configuration — at three confirmations `check_deferred_gaps_are_escalated` would pass it on the rationale, which explains why the work was not done, rather than on the assignment, which says who must do it. It is at two, so nothing fires today. The rationale is kept because it is the record of the port-shape argument M8.2 inherits, not merely an excuse for deferring; **G58** is where the two-guards-disagreeing problem is owned.)***

Note (2026-09-16, M6.3): **MEASURED, and the doubling is the dominant term — which makes this entry's own framing the optimistic one.** `scripts/seed_findings_benchmark.py --projects 50 --findings-per-project 2000 --request-runs 8`, at ADR-0025's volume, over a project whose guard counts confirm 2,000 findings and 2,000 evidence rows inside a 100,000-finding corpus. One discarded warm-up per subject; eight readings each, all printed, no average — ADR-0025's own practice, since a mean would name a latency no run produced.

| subject | min | median | max |
|---|---|---|---|
| `get_by_project_id`, end to end | 84.1 | **108.8** | 136.6 |
| `GET /risks` — ONE read | 94.8 | 144.1 | 150.9 |
| `GET /scored-risks` — TWO reads + scoring | 253.8 | 259.5 | 335.8 |

**The result:** the route gap is 115.3 ms at the medians, 159.0 at the minima and 184.9 at the maxima, and the end-to-end read covers **94.4%, 52.9% and 73.9%** of it respectively. So the second read is **the larger term on every estimator**, and at the medians very nearly the whole of it. Eight readings with this dispersion do not pin the share tighter than "between about half and nearly all", and it is stated that way rather than as the median figure alone.

**The asymmetry that matters more than the share.** The residual — 6.5 to 74.9 ms depending on estimator — is the scoring and ranking pass over **2,000 singleton surfaces**, every seeded finding being `package: None, url: None`. That is the worst case for per-surface overhead and it shrinks with real grouping. The read term does not: it is the same statement over the same rows whatever the shape. **The dominant term is the one production shape cannot reduce.**

**A claim this replaces, recorded because this entry would have inherited it.** An earlier reading of this run's predecessor reported the second read as "the minor half" at **16.6% at the medians and 18.1% at the minima** — re-derived from that run's own artifact, an earlier draft of this note having quoted "~17–20%", whose upper end nothing produces — arrived at by subtracting **`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE)`'s server-side `Execution Time`** from an **end-to-end** route difference. Those are not commensurable: `EXPLAIN` sees no driver round-trip, no transfer of 2,000 rows roughly 872 bytes wide, and no ORM hydration. Measured here, end to end is **4.08×** the server-side figure — 108.8 ms against 26.661 — so about 82 ms of every read is invisible to `EXPLAIN`. **ADR-0025 already carried the warning** — *"Read the plans for access-path shape; do not quote the milliseconds as production latency"* — so this was a failure to heed an existing caveat, not a novel trap, and the correction is why `_measure_reads` exists at all (**G19** carries that half).

**ESCALATION, and it is this entry's whole point.** ADR-0025's Consequences states that a bad number is *"evidence AGAINST decision 1, not a tuning task"*, and that if a candidate Risk is not cheap to recompute then *"persistence is forced by cost rather than by state, which is an amendment to this document and not a follow-up"*. Per-request purity is exactly what buys the doubling, and the doubling is now measured as the dominant term. **What that does and does not establish, because an earlier draft of this note ran the two together.** ADR-0025's criterion is that a candidate Risk be **cheap to recompute**; what is measured here is how the cost **splits**, which is not the same claim. A read that were 94% of a 5 ms gap would falsify nothing. That ADR also refuses a threshold on purpose — *"inventing a number would make an arbitrary figure look like a criterion"* — and the one scale anchor it does offer cuts the other way, since it records **763 ms** to serve fifty findings before ADR-0022's query was rewritten, against **259.5 ms** here at a worst-case shape. **So this is NOT a finding that decision 1's premise has failed.** What it is: the doubling is the dominant term **and it is the term production shape cannot reduce**, while the scoring residual is the one it can — an argument about the cost's *shape* rather than its size, and the argument that belongs in front of whoever takes the amendment. M6.3 does not take the amendment: its scope is a read surface, the optional write was declined at design time, and amending an accepted ADR on the strength of one run is not an implementation commit's act. **Not born assigned** — no current issue owns an ADR-0025 amendment, and inventing one would point at nothing. Trigger sharpens to: **the ADR-0025 decision 1 amendment this measurement calls for**, or M8.1, the first issue forced to write a Risk anyway, whichever comes first. ADR-0025 carries a dated note saying the same, beside the paragraph that set the criterion.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.2): **the second read now exists in code and is still measured by nothing.** `ComputeRiskUseCase.execute` calls `get_by_project_id` after the port has already caused one inside `CorrelateFindingsUseCase`, exactly as this entry describes; the doubling is no longer a forecast about a use case that does not exist. It remains arithmetic rather than a reading, because M6.2 ships no endpoint and no benchmark — the unit tests run against in-memory fakes at corpus scale — the largest scenario any of them assembles is **20 findings** in 10 groups (`semgrep_scan.json` 1 + `trivy_synthetic_edges.json` 6 + `zap_scan.json` 13) — which measures nothing about Postgres. Trigger unchanged: M6.3, which owes the first measurement of a scored request.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.2): **the M7.2 trigger fired. The doubled read now happens once per Brief generated, and M7.2 measures nothing.**
- **Where the read happens.** `ScoredExplainableRisks` runs `ComputeRiskUseCase` once per `POST /projects/{project_id}/briefs`, which is both findings reads, but not `ListScoredRisksUseCase`'s two envelope reads or its ranking. `GET …/briefs` reads only `security_briefs` and never recomputes.
- **Why it is not measured.** The term this issue adds is the provider call, and its latency needs the real calls M7.1's departure owes and this issue does not take. The recompute term is the one this entry already measured inside `/scored-risks`: **259.5 ms** median at ADR-0025's volume, for a whole scored request.
- **What would make it worse.** Briefing every surface one POST at a time multiplies that recompute by the number of surfaces, because nothing batches generation.
- **Unchanged.** Status `assigned → M8.2`.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 2): **generation adds a third findings access.** After the doubled read, `GenerateSecurityBriefUseCase` reads up to 20 members by `get_by_id`, each outer-joining its evidence row, whose payload of up to 20,000 characters `brief` never touches (ADR-0034 decision 2). `get_by_project_id` was rejected as a third whole-project read. **Unmeasured**, like the rest of this entry's per-Brief cost. The list route still never recomputes. Status unchanged.

### G62 — The corroboration signal cannot tell a substantive cross-tool pair from a coincidental co-location, and its only passive instance is the coincidental one
Confirmed: M6.1, M7.1, M6.2, M7.3 · Status: open
Kind: fact · Reopens if: any capture in which a DAST finding carries a source location
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the one claim `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10 is cited for, in the direction that overstates it.** ADR-0005 decision 1 gives a surface `+1` when its members carry two or more distinct `Finding.source` values, and that term is half of what lifts the `/calculate` surface from `plan` to `fix_now`. What it measures is that two tools reported on one surface. What a reader hears is that two tools found the same vulnerability. Measured over the committed corpora: in the passive one **4 of 4** DAST members of that surface are header hygiene (`10038-1`, `10020-1`, `10036-2`, `10021`), unrelated to the `dangerous-eval` finding they are grouped with; in the active one **2 of 6** (`6-5` Path Traversal, `90036` Blind SSTI) are plausibly the same vulnerability and 4 are still headers. **No field on any member distinguishes the two cases**, so the signal fires identically on both, and the only instance the passive corpus can exhibit is entirely coincidental. A `RiskReasoning` text or a Brief that narrates "two tools agree" would assert something the engine did not compute — and M7's LLM is downstream of exactly this number (rule 6, ADR-0004).
Deferral rationale: **the missing signal is ADR-0005 decision 0's rejected option and designing it is a milestone's work, not an issue's.** Separating the two cases needs something relating a SAST sink to a DAST observation — a taint path, a sink-to-parameter mapping, or a DAST finding carrying a source location — none of which appears in any tool output this project captures, and the CWE route is closed for the reason that decision gives (Semgrep supplies none, **G6**). What is cheap and is done instead: the signal is named `corroboration_signal` rather than `agreement`, ADR-0005 decision 5 records that a green M6.2 proves nothing about it, and §10's claim is stated at the width the evidence supports in both that ADR and M6.1's entry. Trigger: **M7.3's prompt safety work**, which is the first consumer that turns this number into a sentence a person reads, or any capture in which a DAST finding carries a source location.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.2): **the term now computes, and it turns out to be the ENTIRE margin in §10's demonstration rather than half of it.** `test_what_cross_tool_correlation_buys_is_one_point_that_breaks_a_tie_with_a_critical_cve` subtracts this signal from the `/calculate` surface's score and the surface drops to 5 — tying the `CRITICAL` package surface exactly, in the same `plan` bucket — because exposure is not something correlation buys, a ZAP-only surface having it too. So the whole of `PRODUCT_SPEC.md` §10's *"materially better prioritization"* rests on this one point, computed over four DAST members that are header hygiene, unrelated to the `dangerous-eval` finding they are grouped with. Cross-reference **G64**, whose subject is the other half of the same shape: the single reachable route to `fix_now` runs through this signal.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.1): **the prompt's wording is M7.1's, so this entry's trigger named the wrong issue first, and M7.1 answers what it can.** M7.3 is scoped to injection via scanned content; what the model is told about this signal is prompt design, which ADR-0032 decision 4 settles. **The mechanism**: `scoring.py` declares `CORROBORATION_DEFINITION` beside `_corroboration_signal` — *"It does not mean the scanners agree, confirm each other, or found the same vulnerability"* — and the prompt forwards it verbatim, with rule 3 of its instructions confining each signal to its definition's terms. Pinned by `test_the_corroboration_definition_states_what_the_signal_does_not_mean` and `test_each_signal_is_rendered_with_its_definition_verbatim`, and a mutation giving corroboration the exposure definition was **killed**. **What is NOT shown: that a real model obeys.** No CI test reaches OpenAI (**G65**, M7.1's departure), so the instruction is verified as sent and not as followed. The missing signal itself is unchanged. M7.1 is appended to `Confirmed:`. Trigger: **M7.3**, as before; or the first real model output a person reviews.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 2): **the priority narrative is now structurally blind to what this entry fears it would overstate, and the new narrative is not.** ADR-0034 decision 3 splits generation into two calls. `explain`, which narrates the bucket and the corroboration signal, keeps its M7.1 prompt byte-identical and sees no finding title. `describe` sees the members' titles and locations and no decision, so on `/calculate` it is shown `dangerous-eval` beside Path Traversal, Blind SSTI and four header alerts, with nothing saying whether they are one vulnerability. Its instructions forbid stating urgency or adding facts beyond the fields, and its output is rejected if it names `fix_now` unprompted. **Neither instruction is verified as followed**: nothing in CI calls a real model (**G65**). The missing signal is unchanged. Trigger: **M7.3**'s capture commit, the first real model output a person reviews; or any capture in which a DAST finding carries a source location.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 3): **the trigger fired: real model output was read by a person, and what was read is narrower than what was captured.**
- **Read by hand, n=3.** In the one-repetition pass the owner read the three `describe` paragraphs, `Flask`, `/calculate` and `urllib3`, for four things: a Semgrep member read as more than a rule id, file and line; an asserted priority, fix, effort or confidence; a statement or implication that two tools agree; anything not in the members. On `/calculate` `describe` is shown `dangerous-eval` beside Path Traversal, Blind SSTI and header alerts. The owner then ran the ten-repetition pass, which the runbook reserves for paragraphs showing none of those failures. **No asserted priority, no implied agreement, the four-sentence limit and the identifier rule rest on those three paragraphs, and on nothing else.**
- **What M6 adds, and what it does not.** Over the ten-repetition pass 29 `describe` outputs reached M6 and none was rejected. M6 checks three things: a control or format character, the 2,000-character bound, and a `fix_now` the members did not supply. **It has no check for implied agreement**, so `m6_rejections=0` is not evidence for this entry, and **none of those 29 was read by anyone**: the three paragraphs came from the one-repetition pass, which is a different run and which `measurements.json` does not cover.
- **The 29 `explain` outputs, which narrate `corroboration_signal` itself, were not read by anyone.** The one committed is `Flask`'s, where the signal is 0.
- **Unchanged:** the missing signal, and compliance verified only as sent for every output nobody read.
- **Trigger** re-points: the first `explain` output on a surface whose corroboration signal is 1 that a person reads; or any capture in which a DAST finding carries a source location.

Note (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **the owner's decision on the 29 unread `explain` outputs.** `scripts/capture_openai_responses.py` is changed to print `explain` text and re-run once with the service declaration in force, so those outputs get a read. The run is the owner's action after this review and is recorded here when done. Nothing under `scripts/` changes in the commit that records this decision.

### G63 — A scored Risk carries a priority and no confidence, so FR-7's second output is unmet while the first ships
Confirmed: M6.1, M7.1, M6.2, M7.2 · Status: assigned → M8.5
Kind: owed · Blocks: ship
Blocks-if-unresolved: **FR-7's second named output, and ADR-0003's stated shape, absent from the thing that satisfies both.** FR-7 requires the engine to compute *"priority and confidence for each Risk"*; ADR-0003's Decision names *"a priority bucket (`fix_now` / `plan` / `monitor`), a confidence value, and a `RiskReasoning`"*. ADR-0005 ships the bucket and the reasoning and defers the confidence scale, so M6.2 implements a Risk with no confidence field at all. Two consequences, and the second is the one that bites: **M7's Brief presents a confidence to the user** (FR-8 names it among the Brief's parts), so the gap arrives at the LLM layer as a missing input rather than as a build failure — and rule 6 forbids the Explanation Layer from supplying one itself, since that would be the LLM deciding something the engine did not. Meanwhile **the provenance distinction stays invisible**: the scale drafted for it made a surface `derived` whenever any member attached through a route map under a serving declaration, which is **G53**'s rule-5 exposure made user-visible. Without it, the `/calculate` surface — always derived, in both corpora — reports exactly what a Trivy package group reports, whose membership came straight off the tool.
Deferral rationale: **it is a second decision, it was drafted inside the first, and M6.1's declared byte ceiling is what caught it** — which is that rule doing its job rather than an accident, and is recorded here because a deferral whose cause is undocumented reads afterwards as an oversight. The scale is also not obviously the drafted one: confidence could be a property of the **grouping** (how well-founded the membership is — the `read`/`derived` axis) or of the **evidence** (how sure the tools are), and the second is the reading FR-7's own sentence suggests while being the one ADR-0005 decision 4 just declined an input for, since only ZAP supplies a per-finding confidence and its states are unexhibited by any capture. Choosing between them needs the consumer that will render it, which is M7.1. Trigger: **M6.2**, the first issue that must either emit a confidence or state that it does not; or **M7.1**, the first consumer that needs one to narrate.
Note (2026-09-16, M6.2): **the first trigger fired and the answer is that M6.2 emits NO confidence.** Recorded as a decision rather than left as an omission, which is what this entry's first trigger asked for. `ScoredSurface` and `RiskReasoning` carry no `confidence` field and `test_risk_scoring.py` asserts the absence, so it cannot be added by accident and then depended on. **Why not**: the grouping-vs-evidence choice this entry names is real and unresolved, and choosing needs the consumer that renders it; designing the scale here would also have cost M6's second and last ADR slot on something M7.1 may redesign, and would repeat the failure ADR-0018's 2026-09-16 amendment recorded for `Finding.confidence` — a scale designed against documentation rather than against data. **What it costs, unchanged and now real rather than forecast**: FR-7's second output and ADR-0003's stated shape are unmet by shipped code, and `docs/adr/0004-llm-downstream-of-scoring.md` carries a dated amendment saying so, because M7.1 reads that ADR before building against it. Trigger narrows to **M7.1** alone.
Note (2026-09-17, M7.1): **the trigger fired and M7.1 records the absence rather than choosing a scale** (ADR-0032 decision 3). Either candidate reaches a third module: a grouping confidence needs provenance out of `correlation`, whose `MatchGroup` carries only `key` and `finding_ids`, and an evidence confidence needs `Finding.confidence`, which ADR-0005 decision 4 declined, plus a migration. **What M7.1 does instead**: `ExplainableDecision` has no confidence, and its field set is asserted equal to an enumeration, so one cannot be added in passing; the prompt tells the model it is not told *"how confident anyone is"* and must not guess. ADR-0003's 2026-09-16 amendment, which claimed ADR-0005 satisfies *"a confidence"*, carries a dated correction. **Cost, unchanged**: FR-7's second output and FR-8's confidence both stay unmet. M7.1 is appended to `Confirmed:`. Trigger re-points to **M7.2**, whose `SecurityBrief` is designed with a `confidence` field (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.1); or M8.3's cards, which render one.
Note (2026-09-17, M7.2): **the M7.2 trigger fired, and `SecurityBrief` is designed without a `confidence` field, with the absence asserted.**
- **Where it is asserted.** `test_the_field_set_equals_the_decisions_enumeration_exactly` pins the domain field set. `test_what_a_brief_does_not_carry` asserts `confidence` is absent from both routes' bodies. Adding `confidence: str | None = None` to `SecurityBrief` was killed.
- **Why.** ADR-0033 decision 2 rejects the field on this entry's ground: either scale reaches a third module.
- **Cost, unchanged.** FR-7's second output and FR-8's confidence both stay unmet.
- **Trigger** re-points to **M8.3**, whose cards render one.

Assignment (2026-09-19, post-M7 boundary review) · Confirms: none: **assigned to M8.5.** M8.5 gives a Risk its confidence.

### G64 — `fix_now` is reachable by exactly one route, so the top bucket is closed to every dependency finding no matter how severe
Confirmed: M6.2, M6.3 · Status: open
Kind: fact · Reopens if: any change to the match key that lets two tools share a package surface, or the first real project whose highest-scoring surface is a `CRITICAL` package one
Blocks-if-unresolved: **a ranked Risk listing whose top bucket no SCA finding can ever enter, presented to a developer as a priority order.** Derived from ADR-0018 decision 1's mapping table and the shipped `build_match_key`, and asserted in `test_compute_risk.py`: only Trivy emits `CRITICAL`, and a Trivy finding is either package-keyed or — where the vulnerability carries no `PkgName`, which `trivy.py` maps to `package=None` — carries no signal at all and becomes a singleton under `group_by_match_key`. Either way it cannot share a surface with another tool, so a `CRITICAL` surface is always single-source and scores `5 + 0 + 0 = 5 → plan`. *(The mechanism is stated in both branches because the first draft of this entry said only "every Trivy finding is package-keyed", which is not true of a `PkgName`-less vulnerability; the conclusion is unchanged and its reason is not.)* The only cross-source pairing `build_match_key` can produce is Semgrep-with-a-derived-route plus ZAP — a Trivy key is `(package, None)` and a ZAP key is `(None, path)`, never equal — so **corroboration implies exposure**, and the two `CRITICAL`-based routes the arithmetic admits (`5+1+0`, `5+0+1`) are unreachable from real scanner output. The only reachable decomposition of 6 is therefore `4 + 1 + 1`: a **route-path surface carrying both tools, with at least one `HIGH` member**. ***(Corrected in the guardian round. This entry first said the route was "a Semgrep `ERROR` on a derived path, plus any ZAP alert on that path". A Semgrep `ERROR` is sufficient but NOT necessary: `severity_signal` is the maximum over members and ZAP's riskcode 3 maps to `HIGH`, exhibited by the committed active capture's `6-5` Path Traversal and `90036` Blind SSTI, both on `/calculate`. So a Semgrep `WARNING` grouped with a ZAP `HIGH` on the same derived path also reaches `fix_now`. The correction matters because this entry is the durable record a later reader will use to reason about reachability, and the narrower sentence would have understated which surfaces can reach the top bucket.)*** So a `CRITICAL` `urllib3` CVE cannot exceed `plan` however many tools confirm it, and **M6.3's endpoint would present that ordering as the product's answer**. Adjacent to **G62**: the one reachable route runs entirely through the signal that entry records as unable to tell a substantive pair from a coincidental one.
Deferral rationale: **this is a property of ADR-0005 decision 1's arithmetic, accepted at M6.1 with its inputs measured, and changing it now would be re-deciding a scoring model against the same corpus that produced it.** The three plausible repairs each cost more than the gap: giving `CRITICAL` a second point re-tunes a scale whose thresholds were chosen against measured distributions; letting a package surface earn exposure needs an asset-facing signal that has no source in `src/` (ADR-0005 decision 4 declines all three of FR-7's); and making Trivy and ZAP co-groupable is a match-key change, which is ADR-0023's territory and would fire **G33**. What is cheap and is done instead: the closures are asserted rather than implied — `test_a_critical_package_surface_scores_five_and_buckets_plan` and `test_a_dast_only_high_surface_scores_five_and_buckets_plan` — and `test_no_package_surface_in_the_corpus_reaches_the_top_bucket` states the consequence directly, so no later reader discovers it from a demo. ADR-0005 decision 5 carries a dated amendment recording that its prescribed coverage of the `CRITICAL` **branch** does not reach the `fix_now` **bucket**, the two being disjoint. Trigger: **M6.3's ranked endpoint**, the first place this ordering is shown to anyone; or the first real project whose highest-scoring surface is a `CRITICAL` package one; or any change to the match key that lets two tools share a package surface.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.3): **the first trigger fired — the ranked endpoint ships — and silence was therefore not available.** This entry's own `Blocks-if-unresolved:` says M6.3's endpoint *"would present that ordering as the product's answer"*, and it now does. What is done: the closure is stated at the surface, in the route's docstring and in `ScoredRiskResponse`'s, in the terms derived above; and it is pinned by `test_a_critical_package_surface_is_shown_as_plan_and_never_fix_now`, which seeds a `CRITICAL` package finding, asserts the response shows it as `plan` at 5, and asserts **no** item in that response is `fix_now` — the endpoint-level twin of `test_a_critical_package_surface_scores_five_and_buckets_plan`. A docstring alone is a comment; the assertion is what makes it a claim, and ADR-0030 decision 6 records that as the reason both ship rather than either. **No response FIELD asserts the closure**, deliberately: it is a property of the scoring function and of no individual Risk, so a field would fabricate rather than report (ADR-0025 decision 4). Worth recording because it is easy to miss: the closure is already **locally** visible per item through `Signal.note` — *"every member was reported by trivy"*, *"no member was reported by a DAST scanner"* — and what the docstrings add is the **global** claim those notes cannot make. **Nothing is repaired.** `fix_now` still has exactly one reachable decomposition and the top bucket is still closed to every SCA finding; the three repairs this entry prices still cost more than the gap. Status unchanged.

### G65 — A faked port proves the consumer's contract and leaves the only adapter behind it unexercised, and `di.py`'s conformance site checks shape, not behaviour
Confirmed: M6.2, M6.3, M7.1, M7.2, M7.3 · Status: open
Kind: fact · Reopens if: anybody proposes a mechanical check that a port's implementation is exercised by something other than a fake
Blocks-if-unresolved: **every port this project publishes from here on, starting with M6.3's endpoint and its DI factories.** MEASURED in this issue: deleting `CorrelationCandidateRisks`' `ProjectAccessDenied → CandidateRiskAccessDenied` translation, and swapping the two ids sent to the port, **each passed 923 tests** — the suite exactly as it stood when they survived, which is today's 927 minus the four tests the correction round added. *(Measured by deselecting those four and running everything else, both mutations applied physically and reverted sha256-clean. The first reading was the `architecture-guardian`'s `pytest tests/unit` → 630 passed; the whole-suite figure was **re-derived rather than extrapolated**, because "630 unit tests" and "the entire suite" are a plausible claim and a measured one, and this entry's whole subject is the gap between those.)* Cause: every test faked the port, so the published **contract** was exercised and its only **implementation** was never constructed outside `platform/di.py`. That factory's port-annotated return type — this project's stated conformance site (ADR-015, named again in **G48**) — checks the adapter has the right **shape** and asserts nothing about what it does. The failure is silent in the way that matters: the suite is green, the architecture check is green, and the adapter could be replaced by one that does nothing.
Deferral rationale: **what is done rather than deferred is the one adapter this issue shipped**, now covered by `tests/unit/test_candidate_risk_provider.py` and a `.calls` assertion, each re-run against the mutation that survived it and confirmed killed. What is deferred is any general mechanism, because there is no mechanical check that a `Protocol`'s implementation is exercised by something other than a fake, and it is not obvious one can exist — it is a statement about what a test double stands in for, which is the same reason **G19** gives for having no gate. **This is the fourth instance of the pattern G8, G9 and G19 record** — sound verification disconnected from reality, by a tool, by a fixture, by a generator, and now by a fake — and it stays a separate number for the reason G19 states about the other three: the triggers differ, and no existing entry would have made anyone re-read at the moment a port is published. Adjacent to **G33**, which is the *structural* half of this gap rather than the same one: there a conformance test covers one field of three and passes; here a conformance site covers shape and passes. Trigger: **M6.3's endpoint and its DI factories**, or **the next module that publishes a port**.

Note (2026-09-16, M6.3): **the trigger fired; the INSTANCE is discharged and the ENTRY is not.** `tests/integration/test_scored_risks_routes.py` reaches the route through `httpx2.ASGITransport` over the real app and overrides **neither** `get_candidate_risk_port` nor `get_compute_risk_use_case`, so `CorrelationCandidateRisks` is genuinely constructed rather than stood in for. Both of its paths are covered, and the second is the one that matters: `test_a_non_member_and_an_absent_project_are_indistinguishable` exercises exactly the mutation that survived 923 tests at M6.2 — delete the wrapper's translation and `correlation.domain`'s `ProjectAccessDenied` escapes the port uncaught, the route's `except CandidateRiskAccessDenied` does not catch it, and that test reads **500 instead of 404**. `test_the_ports_denial_type_is_what_the_route_catches` pins the narrowing that makes this real, since a bare `except` would have swallowed the same mutation into a 404 anyway. **One test in that file DOES override the port** — the `MemberFindingMissing` 500 case, which is unreachable otherwise — and its docstring says so, precisely so the two kinds are not confused. **What is NOT discharged is this entry's subject:** no mechanical check says a `Protocol`'s implementation is exercised by something other than a fake, `platform/di.py`'s conformance site still checks shape and not behaviour, and the next module to publish a port inherits the hole intact. Status unchanged; the trigger narrows to **the next module that publishes a port**.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.1): **the trigger fired — `brief` publishes `ExplanationProviderPort` and `risk_engine` its first ports module — and an adapter-side hole was closed, while this entry's subject stays open.** *Closed:* `get_explanation_provider`'s arguments are both `str`, so swapping `api_key` and `model` type-checks. Applied physically, that swap **passed 1,017 tests — the whole suite with one test deselected** — and `tests/unit/test_di_wiring.py`'s factory test, which sends a request through the real factory and reads which value reached the header and which the body, **kills it**. Nine more mutations were applied one at a time, each reverted sha256-clean, and all were killed: a chained transport error, a forwarded error body, a dropped `store`, rendered finding ids, a wrong definition, a narrowed carrier field, a widened carrier, `length` accepted as success, and the rule-11 entry removed. The adapter's own code runs over `MockTransport`, and a contract test holds `FakeExplanationProvider` and the real adapter to the same assertions, reading the fake's `.calls`. **NOT closed, and the reason this note exists: OpenAI's real contract is exercised by nothing in CI.** The fixtures follow openai-python's types, not a capture (M7.1's departure). So every mutation that agrees with the fixtures and disagrees with the API survives by construction — a response field OpenAI actually names differently, `gpt-5-mini` rejecting the `developer` role, a 401 body shaped otherwise — and no seam can see one. `platform/di.py`'s conformance site still checks shape only, `explainable_decision` has no caller in `src/`, and the next module to publish a port inherits this entry intact. Trigger unchanged: **the next module that publishes a port**; or the first captured OpenAI response, which narrows the unexercised half.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.2): **the trigger fired again: `risk_engine` publishes `ExplainableRiskPort` and `brief` publishes `SecurityBriefRepositoryPort`.**
- **Both implementations run for real.** `tests/integration/test_security_brief_routes.py` overrides `get_explanation_provider` in every test and neither new port's factory in any test but one, so `ScoredExplainableRisks` and `PostgresSecurityBriefRepository` are constructed through real wiring.
- **The one port override** is `get_explainable_risk_port`, in the disagreeing-reads test, whose docstring says it does not exercise the real adapter.
- **Two tests also replace `get_clock`** with an advancing clock, so that ordering is asserted on distinct timestamps. That override replaces no port this entry is about.
- **Wiring is covered.** A port factory built over an empty compute was applied as a mutation and killed by eight route tests.
- **The repository has its own real-Postgres suite.**
- **What stays open is this entry's subject.** No mechanical check says an implementation is exercised by something other than a fake, and OpenAI's contract is still exercised by nothing in CI.
- **Trigger unchanged.**

Note (2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 2): **the trigger fired again: `ExplanationProviderPort` gains `describe`.**
- **What covers it.** `test_explanation_provider_contract.py` holds the fake and the real adapter to the same `describe` assertions. `test_openai_explanation_provider.py` runs the adapter's `describe` over `MockTransport`, including the cross-call separation test that reads three request bodies. Every route test overrides `get_explanation_provider`, and the generating ones override nothing else except where named: two replace `get_clock`, one `get_explainable_risk_port`, and one, `test_a_member_that_cannot_be_read_back_is_a_fixed_500`, replaces `get_generate_security_brief_use_case` and says so. Elsewhere `GenerateSecurityBriefUseCase` reads members through the real `PostgresFindingRepository`.
- **What stays open.** OpenAI's contract is still exercised by nothing in CI, for both methods. M7.3's capture commit replays captured responses and narrows this.
- **Trigger unchanged**: the next module that publishes a port; or that capture.

Note (2026-09-17, M7.3 commit 3): **the capture trigger fired, and the unexercised half narrows to OpenAI on any later day.**
- **What the replay now covers.** The two 200s and the 401 are OpenAI's own bodies from 2026-09-17, replayed through the adapter's existing tests in place of the schema-derived ones, and the M7.1 departure has ended. Three of the mutations this entry's M7.1 note said survive by construction are answered for that day. 58 of 59 calls returned 200 with `finish_reason` `stop`, so the fields the adapter reads are named as it reads them and `gpt-5-mini` accepted the `developer` role for both prompts. The 401 has the shape the tests assumed.
- **A control verified against a real body, not only a double.** The captured 401 echoed the key, and the adapter's exception carried no fragment of it (**G71**).
- **What `m6_rejections=0` checks, and what it does NOT.** 29 `describe` outputs reached M6 (30 were sent, and one timed out without a body). None was rejected. M6 checks a control or format character, the 2,000-character bound, and a `fix_now` the members did not supply. **It does not show that the model obeyed the rest of `describe`'s instructions.** No asserted priority, no implied agreement, the four-sentence limit and the identifier rule were read by hand over the three paragraphs of the one-repetition pass: **n=3, not 29** (**G62**).
- **Still open.** Nothing in CI calls OpenAI, so a later change in what it sends is invisible. This entry's subject is unchanged: no mechanical check says an implementation is exercised by something other than a fake.
- **Trigger**: the next module that publishes a port. The capture clause is spent.

### G66 — Two project-scoped Risk routes ship with one consumer between them, and the thing that distinguishes them is named in neither URL
Confirmed: M6.3 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **a reader of this API has two answers to "what are this project's Risks?" and nothing in either URL says which one they want.** `GET /projects/{id}/risks` (M5.2) returns candidate Risks ordered by `_group_order` — total, deterministic and explicitly not a ranking. `GET /projects/{id}/scored-risks` (M6.3) returns the same surfaces ranked by `priority_score`. **The scored response is a strict superset of the unscored one**: each scored item carries `match`, `finding_ids` and `finding_count` — the whole of `RiskResponse` — plus `priority_score`, `priority` and `reasoning`, and the scored envelope carries the same `total`/`limit`/`offset`, the same completeness envelope, and the bucket thresholds. **That item shape is specified in ADR-0030 decision 3, and shipping it is what makes this entry true**; note that `ScoredSurface` carries neither of the two shared fields under those names — `match` is its `package`/`url` and `finding_count` is `len(finding_ids)`, both assembled by the adapter, which is why the ADR enumerates the response rather than leaving the claim to the domain type. *(The first draft of this entry rested that claim on ADR-0030 decision 1, which enumerates no fields at all; the guardian round found it, and the fix was to make the ADR specify the item rather than to soften the claim.)* So the unscored route carries **nothing** the scored one does not, and the only difference between them is the **ORDER** — the one thing neither URL names, since `scored-risks` names the content on this project's own domain vocabulary (`ScoredSurface`, `score_surface`, `priority_score`). The concrete cost is a client paging the unscored route and rendering a deterministic order as a priority order, which is the exact misreading `test_the_item_order_is_not_a_priority_order` was written to prevent at one route and cannot prevent at two. **M8.3's frontend is the first place that would show**, since Journey 3's whole premise is a ranked list.
Deferral rationale: **which route survives is a decision about what the dashboard consumes, and the dashboard is not built.** M8.2's `GetProjectDashboardUseCase` is *"ranked Risks + Briefs + scan history read model"* and M8.3's cards render the `Fix now`/`Plan`/`Monitor` list, so on the evidence in this repository the scored route is the one with a consumer and the unscored one has none outside its own tests. Retiring it inside M6.3 would mean rehoming ADR-0025 decision 5's eight negative-claim tests — that decision's executable form, several of which have no counterpart in a scored response — which is a second deliverable in an issue that already owes an endpoint, a measurement and three register triggers. **Not born assigned**: M8.2 has not been built, and the choice is its to make rather than this issue's to pre-empt. Trigger: **M8.2**, the issue that decides what the dashboard reads and therefore which of the two has a consumer; or the first client of either route outside this repository.

### G67 — A second module copies `NormalizationRun`'s six fields into a carrier of its own, and the obvious tidy-up puts a TRANSPORTED structure in `shared_kernel/`
Confirmed: M6.3, M7.2 · Status: open
Kind: owed · Blocks: internal
Blocks-if-unresolved: **the next reader who notices the duplication repairs it the one way ADR-0018's criterion forbids, and nothing mechanical objects.** The completeness envelope is six fields — `scan_id`, `status`, `requested_at`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `failure_reason`. `normalization` holds them as the **entity**: `ProjectFindings.latest_run` is a `NormalizationRun`, which that module may name.
